### Tracker row 1: write-off vs slippage. Falsifier does not fire on my arithmetic

The deck says H1 FY27 was "just one-third" of the ₹346 Cr of slippage over the 12 months to Sep-26 (deck p.22). That makes H1 slippage about ₹115 Cr. Less Q1's ₹49 Cr (Notion, Q1 concall), Q2 slippage was about ₹66 Cr.

On-book GNPA moved from about ₹219 Cr (2.18% × ₹10,035 Cr on-book) to ₹197 Cr. So write-offs, recoveries and upgrades together were at most about ₹88 Cr. That puts write-offs at most about 1.3x slippage. Stretching "one-third" from 30% to 36% keeps the ratio below 1.8x. All my arithmetic, not filed. The falsifier is write-offs above twice slippage, so it does not fire.

Supporting points:
- "The company has not transferred any NPA loans" this quarter (note 7(iii)). So the GNPA fall was not helped by an ARC (asset reconstruction company) sale.
- Stage 2 coverage jumped from 21% to 62%. Stage 3 coverage went from 85% to 91% (deck p.22).

Still missing: the actual Q2 write-off figure. Ask on today's call.

### Tracker row 2: subsidiaries. The ROE leg cleared, the PAT leg did not move

Consolidated less standalone AUM went from ₹2,623 Cr to ₹2,981 Cr, up ₹358 Cr. That is about 55% of the ₹654 Cr consolidated QoQ increase. My arithmetic.
- SFL grew ₹401 Cr (₹1,360 Cr to ₹1,761 Cr, +184% YoY). SHFL shrank ₹44 Cr (deck p.28).
- Subsidiary PAT contribution was about ₹4.3 Cr (₹175.9 Cr less ₹171.6 Cr), or 2.5% of standalone. Q1 was 1.96%, Q4 FY26 was 18.33%.
- SFL earned ₹5.1 Cr on ₹460 Cr net worth, about 4.4% ROE annualised (PR p.4; deck p.8). My arithmetic.
- SCNL put another ₹110 Cr of equity into SFL in Q2 (note 5) and ₹12 Cr into SGAL (note 6).

The row's falsifier was consolidated ROE pulled below 17%. That did not happen. The drag is real but small next to a 22.8% group ROE. Signal Health can move from Threatens to Neutral; that is your call.

### What the filing does not say, and what is odd

- **Assam restructuring.** 42,394 accounts with ₹92.03 Cr outstanding are under a calamity relief plan (note 10) [FILED]. The Q1 call gave about 44,000 borrowers and ₹149 Cr.
  - Assam is 16% of the on-book book and shows PAR 90 of 0.4% (deck p.26).
  - Restructured loans stay standard, so the low Assam PAR is partly a product of the relief plan. [INFERENCE]
  - This is a new item, not on file.
- **Security receipts.** SCNL holds ₹81.87 Cr of instruments rated RR2/RR3 (note 9). The note's header is garbled in the text layer. These are most likely security receipts from earlier ARC sales [INFERENCE]. That bears on the open ARC-vs-organic question from Q1.
- **Borrower growth.** Standalone active clients are 33.1 lakh, against 32.4 lakh a year ago, +2.2%. AUM is +23% (deck p.17). The over-indebtedness gap is about 21 points, above the 15-20 warning band. Q1 was about 18. That is two quarters running; three trigger the warning. My arithmetic.
  - The 7-Oct update said the standalone client base was 33.3 lakh. The deck says 33.1. Minor.
- **AUM guidance.** The deck states FY27 consolidated AUM guidance as 20-25%, with 19% annualised achieved (deck p.8) [FILED]. That settles the conflict with the 25-30% broker note logged 09-Oct; 20-25% is the live guide. H2 needs +9.8% to +14.3% from ₹16,589 Cr. My arithmetic.
- **Net worth gap.** Consolidated net worth (₹3,177.8 Cr total equity) is still ₹259 Cr below standalone (₹3,437 Cr). It was ₹275 Cr below in Q1.

### Two readings

**Bull:**
- Reported consolidated ROE is 22.8% for Q2 and 19.8% for H1, both above the 17% line on the clean basis.
- GNPA is 1.94% with no NPA sale. Slippage is slowing.
- Stage 2 and Stage 3 coverage are up sharply. CRAR is 27.9%.
- H1 PAT of ₹299 Cr is already 70% of the ₹425 Cr FY27 base.

**Bear:**
- The QoQ profit jump is DA, other income and lower provisions. Spread less opex fell ₹9 Cr.
- "Adjusted" metrics add back the whole overlay balance.
- Borrowers are flat while AUM grows 23%.
- ₹92 Cr of Assam restructured loans are sitting as standard.
- Lender count fell from 77 to 69. Subsidiaries still earn 2.5%.

**The single observation that separates them:** Q3 FY27 reported consolidated ROE at or above 16% (your time-stop line), with DA income no higher than about ₹100 Cr (Q1 level). Results are due around late Jan-2027.
- Holding 16% without a DA spike confirms the bull reading.
- ROE below 16% once DA normalises, or the Assam book slipping into NPA, confirms the bear reading.

### Fair value

The post-CARE FY27 base PAT is ₹425 Cr (Notion, Revised Fair Values). H1 is ₹298.5 Cr. At the H1 average pace, FY27 lands near ₹597 Cr. That is about ₹52 EPS on 11.39 Cr diluted shares, above even the FY28 base EPS of ₹46.10. My arithmetic.

It supports the "ROE recovery to 17-18% sustainable" assumption behind the 12x destination. Haircut it for DA timing before carrying it into the bands. I would not re-price on one quarter.

### Price

₹228 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:39 IST). This is most likely the 28-Oct close: the results were filed at 16:01, after market. 52-week range ₹136-274; market cap ₹2,520 Cr. Against the lines on file (my arithmetic):
- ₹8 (3.6%) above the ₹220 halt-add / stop line. ₹5.18 above the ₹222.82 warrant floor. 12.3% below the ₹260 warrant price.
- 8.4% below the ₹249 MoS. Inside Strong Buy (below ₹255).
- P/B is 0.79x on the new consolidated BVPS of ₹287.21 (PR p.3).
- Growth trigger T5's kill line is P/B below 0.80x for two quarters. On the new book that line is about ₹230, not ₹216, so the price is already below it.
- At the Sep-26 quarter-end the reading was 0.81x (₹232.10 / ₹287.21, deck p.32), so no quarter-end reading has fired. The Dec-26 quarter-end reading is now the first live one.

I cannot tell from this record whether the 26-Oct and 27-Oct closes were below ₹220. The stop-loss question from 24-Oct stays open.

**Verdict.** No thesis-broken trigger fires, and no stop-loss trigger fires on the print. The master gate passes on both bases. On your rules the pre-committed action at a green gate is escalation toward the 2.5-3.5% tier. Weigh that against a Q2 profit built on DA and treasury lines rather than spread. That is your call.

**Item 169 (29-Oct, "Rating update", no PDF).** This is the third blank rating line in ten days. The full filing would show the agency, instrument and action. Only a second agency moving would test item #22. Not testable today.

## Unihealth Hosp: approvals passed; main board opens the door to a QIP

Both items (192, 193) are the same event: the 28-Oct-2026 postal ballot result. Three resolutions passed: a related-party transaction, a variation of IPO objects, and migration from NSE Emerge to the main board. There is no PDF. The exchange fetch failed (HTTP 403, INDEX.md), so I have the Screener summary only. The scrutiniser's report would add three things this review does not have: the vote split by category (promoter, institutional, retail), turnout, and the exact counterparty and cap of the RPT. Neither summary gives the margin of passage.

What is on file. The 17-Sep-2026 board outcome, logged in Notion on 23-Sep, put three proposals to postal ballot [FILED, per Notion 23-Sep]:
- Migration from SME Emerge to the NSE main board, plus a BSE main-board listing, subject to approvals.
- Conversion of ₹26 Cr of loans into equity at UMC Hospitals Pvt Ltd (UHPL).
- Variation of IPO objects (prospectus 14-Sep-2023): ₹1.69 Cr of unspent proceeds moved from Nigeria to UHPL India.

So today's result closes a step the record already expected. The question is what it does to the gates.

**IPO variation: small money, slow housekeeping.**
- ₹1.69 Cr against the ₹56.54 Cr IPO (Notion, Company Snapshot) is 3.0% of the issue. That is my arithmetic, not filed. It does not move the numbers.
- The Nigeria hospital exit was logged as April 2025 (Notion, Key Corrections). The object stayed pointed at Nigeria for about 18 months after the business it funded closed. That is slow, not sinister.
- The money goes to UHPL, which is 80% owned, not 100% [FILED, AGM Notice Item 8, per Notion Role 6 Correction 2]. Unless the transfer is structured as equity that raises UHL's stake, a fifth of the benefit accrues to UHPL's minority holder [INFERENCE].
- The summary does not say whether dissenting holders got an exit offer, or at what price.

**RPT: why a second approval three months after the AGM?** The 28-Jul AGM already carried ₹287.27 Cr of FY26-27 RPT caps, including ₹202.02 Cr for UHPL (Notion, Role 6 5C). A fresh RPT resolution by postal ballot means one of three things: a new counterparty, a bigger cap, or a different kind of transaction [INFERENCE]. The obvious candidates on file are the ₹26 Cr UHPL loan-to-equity conversion (17-Sep) and the ₹22.9 Cr Victoria guarantee to Bank of India Uganda (23-Sep). The summary names neither. Two record items stay open:
- The vote split. Monitoring items 12 and 13 set thresholds on institutional voting for the RPT block: red at 40% institutional against for Item 8, red at 30% for Items 9-12 (Notion, 10-Jul log).
- The 28-Jul AGM outcome itself. As of the 25-Aug log it was "NOT YET reviewed in Notion", and no later entry logs it.

So the record has two RPT votes and no vote split for either.

**Main-board migration: the catalyst, and the trigger behind it.** Migration is Tier 3 catalyst #13 ("FY27-FY28, inferred", Notion 9-Jun catalyst stack). Shareholder approval moves it from inferred to filed intent. Exchange approval is still pending, and the record's own pre-mortem allows 14-18 months of processing (Notion, Role 3 pre-mortem). Three consequences follow.

1. *Bull reading.* Liquidity, institutional access and the end of the SME discount. Role 2 put about half the peer discount down to structure, including SME status (Notion, Role 2 peer positioning). Evidence: the board moved within a month of the Victoria close, and the AGM caps show capital going into India.

2. *Bear reading: it is the route to dilution.* Management told the Bharat Connect conference that a QIP or preferential allotment is being evaluated for Q3 or Q4 FY27 [MGMT via broker, Notion 09-Oct]. Main-board status widens the buyer pool for that raise [INFERENCE]. Now test it against exit trigger #3 ("Promoter holding drops below 65% → INVESTIGATE; REDUCE 50% on confirmation"):
   - Post-swap promoter holding is 64.82% [FILED SHP Sep-26, per Notion 23-Sep]. The trigger is already breached on level. The record set it aside under the "accretive M&A" carve-out (23-Jul and 25-Aug logs).
   - The cure was the 2 lakh outstanding promoter warrants. They take fully diluted holding to 65.22%, a 22 bp cushion (Notion, 23-Jul). The 23-Jul log itself says: "Any further issuance without matching promoter warrant participation would breach it."
   - A ₹100 Cr raise at ₹550, the 09-Oct working assumption, is 18.18 lakh shares. That takes promoter holding to 1,13,15,912 / 1,91,68,134 = about 59.0%. At today's ₹565 it is about 59.2%. My arithmetic, not filed.
   - The carve-out was written for share-swap M&A, not for a cash raise. **Ruling needed:** does the carve-out extend to a QIP? If it does not, trigger #3 fires the day a QIP prices without promoter participation.

3. *Gate calendar.* An SME reports half-yearly. Several live tests are written as quarterly: exit trigger 1 ("Q1 FY27 MoD quarterly payment ≤ ₹3 Cr"), the pre-commitment ("≥2 of triggers 1-5 firing in Q1 FY27 → EXIT FULLY"), and the Q1 FY27 binary tests (consolidated CFO ≥ ₹15 Cr). There was no Q1 FY27 print to test them on. The 25-Aug log still reads "Q1 FY27 print (verification pending)". The 09-Oct entry quietly moved to an "H1 FY27 checklist (result due Nov-2026)". On the main board, quarterly results would make these gates testable as written [INFERENCE]. Until then, **ruling needed:** re-point the Q1 FY27 gates and the pre-commitment to H1 FY27 (Nov-2026)? Vivid Electromech's record made the same correction on 25-Sep (item #25 "cannot fire: SME reports half-yearly").

**What separates the readings.** The H1 FY27 consolidated print (Nov-2026) and the QIP terms. Watch two numbers:
- H1 operating cash flow. FY26 CFO was ₹5.69 Cr on ₹45.73 Cr PAT (Notion, Verdict Card), and the record's cash-first rule (23-Sep) applies.
- Whether promoters take part in any raise.

If H1 CFO is still below ₹10 Cr and a QIP comes before main-board quarterly reporting starts, the dilution reading wins.

**Fair value consequence.** None from these filings on their own. The live central estimate is the 09-Oct rebuild:
- Owners' PAT: FY27E about ₹20 Cr (EPS about ₹12); FY28E about ₹47 Cr (EPS about ₹25 after a QIP).
- The row's destination PE is about 18x. At 18x, FY28E EPS of ₹25 gives about ₹450. My arithmetic, not filed.
- At ₹565 the stock trades at about 47x FY27E and 22.6x FY28E.

The migration re-rating is the assumption that would have to carry the gap.

**Price.** ₹565 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:40 IST). 52-week range ₹154-820. Market cap ₹898 Cr is on the pre-swap share count; on about 1.715 Cr shares it is about ₹969 Cr (my arithmetic).
- Against the record: +22% on ₹462 cost. Down 14.7% from the ₹662 on file (22-Sep, now stale). Up 2.7% from ₹550 (8-Oct close, 09-Oct entry). 31% below the 52-week high.
- Against the lines: 49% above the ₹380 entry max and well above the ₹339 MoS. The ₹290 stop is 49% below.
- No add on the record's own lines. No stop is near.

**Verdict.** No thesis-broken trigger fires today. Trigger #3 sits on a 22 bp cushion that the next equity raise erases, and today's approval removes one obstacle to that raise. On the rules, the name moves from "catalyst pending" to "catalyst arriving with a dilution cost attached". Position HELD 4%, Decision Status untouched. That is your call.

## Raymond Realty: joint auditor quits over fees, price hits MoS

Raymond Realty is in COMPANIES MASTER as WATCHLIST / AVOID, not held. Entry ₹611 ceiling, MoS ₹550, hard stop ₹520, base FV Y3 ₹1,343, Promoter CONCERN (3 Red) (Notion, properties and Role 2 recommendation). Five tracker rows are linked and all were read: Q2 margin, net debt, H1 CFO (Tier 1); warrant money, Thane Jekegram launch (Tier 2). The Q2 results (27-Oct) were reviewed in the previous window and are not yet on the Notion page; I use that review only as a pointer.

### What it says [FILED]

Raymond_Realty_Announcement_under_Regulation_30_LODR-Re_1b10f2, 8 pages:

- Chaturvedi & Shah LLP, one of two joint statutory auditors, resigned with immediate effect by letter dated 28-Oct-2026, delivered in person at 7:00 PM (p.1, p.4).
- Reason: on 20-Aug-2026 the firm asked CFO Ankur Jindal for higher audit fees for the "expanded scope" of a listed company. On 28-Aug management declined. The firm resigned "in view of" that refusal (p.4).
- The firm's Annexure: no concerns raised, no information withheld (both "Not Applicable"), "any other facts: Nil", and a declaration that there is "no other material reason" (p.7-8).
- It had signed the FY26 audit (5-May), the Q1 limited review (7-Aug) and the Q2 limited review dated 27-Oct, the day before resigning (p.4, p.6).
- Term: appointed at the 3rd AGM, 13-Jun-2022, for FY23 to FY27, ending at the 8th AGM in 2027 (p.6).
- Walker Chandiok & Co LLP continues as statutory auditor. The company says that because no concerns were raised, the Audit Committee was not required to deliberate (p.1).

Item 142 (An_fa562e, 1 page): audio of the 28-Oct 11:30 investor call uploaded, signed 15:13 the same day. Timely.

### Tested against the record

**The positive counterweight on file loses half its support.** The Promoter Background Check lists "Accounting/Auditor clean (Walker Chandiok stable since 2022 + Chaturvedi & Shah joint)" among the counterweights to Promoter CONCERN (Notion, Promoter Background Check). The ring-fencing override that permits WATCHLIST rests on Raymond Realty being cleaner than the group. One of the two auditors behind that line has now left, one year early, over money.

**Stop-loss trigger 3, "new Singhania governance event hits Raymond Realty specifically".** Does not fire on the filed facts. The stated reason is fees, the auditor declares no other reason, and nothing names the promoter. Monitoring #7 (red: new RPT flagged or SEBI action) does not fire either.

**The timing fits the rules, not a rush.** The fee refusal was on 28-Aug. That is more than 45 days after the June quarter ended. Under SEBI's 2019 auditor-resignation circular, an auditor who resigns after that point must still issue the review for the current quarter [INFERENCE on the rule]. So the earliest clean exit after 28-Aug was right after signing the Q2 review. That is what happened. The day-after timing reads as compliance, not as a reaction to the Q2 numbers.

**What the fee refusal says.** Audit fees for a company this size are small against a ₹1,220 Cr gross debt book [INFERENCE; the fee is not disclosed]. Scope has grown: the Q2 subsidiary list added Ten X Mahalaxmi and Ten X Realty South, a ₹210 Cr AIF deal was signed at Ten X Realty West on 26-Oct, and warrants are pending (Notion, 24-Oct and 26-Oct entries). Management chose to lose a joint auditor rather than pay more for that scope. That is a cost decision about audit quality, made by the CFO the Q1 review logged as silent on finance questions (Notion, Q1 review FND-15).

**The 28-Oct call.** It ran at 11:30. The resignation came at 7:00 PM. Management had known of the fee standoff since 28-Aug [INFERENCE from the letter]. The recording is not reviewed here: it is audio, and the transcript is not yet filed. The FND-15 test from the 22-Oct entry (do the Group CFO and CFO answer the interest and cash questions) is still open.

**Framework read.** On FlySBS your file treats "twin pre-IPO auditor resignations" as an auto-AVOID trigger (Notion, FlySBS Section 3). This is different: one joint auditor, post-listing, a stated fee reason, a clean declaration, and the other auditor staying. I do not think the FlySBS rule applies as written. Please rule whether any auditor exit counts as a governance input for Raymond, given the override depends on the "auditor clean" line.

### What it does not say

- The current audit fee and the increase asked for.
- Whether the company will appoint a replacement joint auditor. A vacancy caused by resignation is filled by the board within 30 days and approved by shareholders within three months, if the company chooses to fill it [INFERENCE on Sec 139(8)]. With Walker Chandiok continuing, it may not.
- The ADT-3 the auditor will file with the Registrar. It will carry the auditor's own reasons.

### Two readings

Bull:
- The auditor says in writing there is no concern and no other reason.
- Walker Chandiok, the larger firm, stays, and has been there since 2022.
- The Q2 review was unmodified. The deck logged in the prior window says related-party transactions get an E&Y third-party review.

Bear:
- The one "clean" counterweight on the promoter file is now half gone.
- The company declined to pay for audit scope while adding SPVs, AIF money and warrants.
- The exit comes as the P&L weakens: Q2 PAT down 12% and finance cost doubled (prior-window review of Results_56eda7).

The one observation that separates them: the Q3 FY27 limited review (by mid-Feb 2027), signed by Walker Chandiok alone. Unmodified with no new Emphasis of Matter supports the bull reading. Any modification, or a new emphasis on JDA revenue recognition or authority dues, confirms the bear. An earlier, smaller tell: the ADT-3 text (due within 30 days, by about 27-Nov).

### Fair value

No change. Base FV Y3 ₹1,343 (diluted about ₹1,277) does not depend on audit structure. What changes is the confidence behind the override, not the number.

### Price

₹548 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:39 IST). 52-week ₹349 to ₹742. Market cap ₹3,649 Cr. This is the 28-Oct close: after the Q2 results and the call, before the 7:00 PM resignation [INFERENCE from timings]. So it has not yet absorbed this filing.

- Down 8.2% from ₹597 before the results (28-Oct 06:13 IST, prior window). 4.0% below ₹571 on file (26-Oct-2026).
- ₹2 below the ₹550 MoS. Below the undiluted ₹550-611 band. Inside the diluted band of about ₹523-581 (Notion, 09-Oct). Inside the post-Devil's-Advocate entry of ₹500-550 (Notion, Role 3).
- 5.4% above the ₹520 hard stop. Not held, so the stop is not live.
- On base FV Y3 ₹1,343, about 34.8% a year; on the diluted ₹1,277, about 32.6%. Both clear 25%. My arithmetic.
- Trailing EPS ₹44.0 gives about 12.5x. The ₹614 warrant price is 12.0% above market, so the ₹307 Cr balance is further out of the money.

### Verdict

No stop-loss trigger fires. For the first time the price sits at the MoS and inside the post-Devil's-Advocate zone. The same day, one of the two supports for the override that permits WATCHLIST weakened. Promoter CONCERN (3 Red) still caps the name; at MoS is not buyable on your rules. I read this as no move toward entry. The open margin and net-debt rulings from the Q2 print matter more now that price is at the line. That is your call.

## Onemi Technology: asset quality passes, but a ₹33 Cr consolidation item drives profit growth

Onemi (Kissht) is in COMPANIES MASTER. Decision Status is blank (operator-only). The framework implies AVOID on Gate 0 AVERAGE until G0V is ruled. The entry lines on file are ₹418 base, ₹364 low and ₹345 2:1, with the Stage 11 zone at ₹301.5-350 (Notion, Position Size). These sit in the Position Size text only; the Entry Price Max and Margin of Safety Price properties are blank. Size ceiling Small, Promoter Verdict CONCERN: INTEGRITY. Not held. I read all 15 linked tracker rows.

Eight items. Four of them (index 103, 104, 105, 106) carry the same 20-page PDF: the board outcome, the limited-review results, the Si Creva rights annexure and the IPO deviation statement. The text files and page images are byte-identical, so I read that document once, all 20 pages including the 15 image pages. I also read the 2-page press release (108), the 43-page deck (109) and the ESOP allotment (107). The KPI filing (102) is only a pointer to the website. The KPI document itself is not on the exchange and not in this run.

### What the print says [FILED]

| Item | Q2 FY27 | Q1 FY27 | Q2 FY26 | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Total income | ₹740.29 Cr | ₹676.56 Cr | ₹517.47 Cr | results p.5 |
| Impairment on financial instruments | ₹133.06 Cr | ₹128.09 Cr | ₹122.36 Cr | p.5 |
| Other expenses | ₹308.01 Cr | ₹263.33 Cr | ₹185.71 Cr | p.5 |
| PBT | ₹154.25 Cr | ₹127.71 Cr | ₹83.00 Cr | p.5 |
| Tax | ₹29.13 Cr | ₹32.63 Cr | ₹20.56 Cr | p.5 |
| PAT | ₹125.12 Cr | ₹95.08 Cr | ₹62.44 Cr | p.5 |
| Stage 2 (on-book) | 2.37% | 3.15% | 4.11% | deck p.29 |
| GNPA (on-book) | 2.18% | 2.25% | 2.92% | deck p.29 |
| AUM on / off book | ₹4,199 / ₹5,118 Cr | ₹3,716 / ₹4,284 Cr (tracker) | | deck p.32 |
| Income / avg AUM | 34.2% | 35.9% | 39.4% | deck p.40 |
| Impairment / avg AUM | 6.2% | 6.8% | 9.3% | deck p.40 |

Guidance is raised [MGMT, deck p.5]: AUM growth 60%+ (was 40%+), impairment cost down 15%+ YoY (was 10-15%), GNPA below 2.25% (maintained), RoAE 20%+ (was 19-21%).

### Gate tests, leg by leg

**Thesis-broken T3 (Stage 2 above 4.11% or GNPA above 2.92%, by 30-Nov).** Does not fire. Stage 2 is 2.37% and GNPA 2.18% (deck p.29). Both are inside the STARTING band (Stage 2 at or below 3.3%, GNPA at or below 2.25%), so FTTCP T3 stays +1. The GNPA early warning at 2.50% is not hit. The DECLINING thresholds of 4.11% and 2.92% are exactly the Sep-25 values on deck p.29. So this is a year-on-year test, and it passes by a wide margin.

**Re-engage trigger 2** (Stage 2 at or below 3.3%, GNPA at or below 2.25%, annualised all-in loss at or below 11.0%, collection outsourcing back near ₹47 Cr a quarter):
- Stage 2 leg: passes (2.37%).
- GNPA leg: passes (2.18%), with 7 bps of headroom to the 2.25% line.
- All-in loss leg: not testable. The cash flow statement does show a half-yearly line, "Provision on off balance sheet exposure", of ₹27.88 Cr for H1 FY27 against ₹26.97 Cr for H1 FY26 (results p.7). The tracker note says off-book ECL is published only annually. That is now partly superseded. Annualised against average off-book AUM, the line is about 1.3% (my arithmetic, not filed). But it is a provision movement, not the DLG payouts, and it does not reconcile with the 11.0% FY26 all-in definition. On-book impairment alone is 6.2% of average AUM (deck p.40).
- Collection leg: not testable. Other expenses are ₹308.01 Cr (+17% QoQ, +66% YoY; results p.5), with no split into marketing, collection outsourcing or DLG cost anywhere in the results or the deck.

So two legs pass and two cannot be tested from this print. Please rule on whether trigger 2 fires on the testable legs or waits for the collection and loss data.

**Pricing-spread falsifier ("spread narrows two consecutive quarters").** The answer depends on the reading:
- Gross reading, income less impairment over average AUM: 29.1% in Q1 (35.9 − 6.8) and 28.0% in Q2 (34.2 − 6.2), down 1.1 points. My arithmetic on deck p.40. Q4 FY26 impairment is not on file, so I cannot tell whether this is the first narrowing or the second.
- Net reading, net total income less impairment: 24.8% in Q1 (31.6 − 6.8) and 24.7% in Q2 (30.9 − 6.2). Flat. The cheaper funding offset the yield cut.

Please rule on which spread the row tests.

**Pillar 2L revisit (0.80x if H1 FY27 partner loss runs above about 6% of average off-book AUM).** Not testable. The only partner-loss line in the filing is the off-balance-sheet provision, about 1.3% annualised (my arithmetic), and that is not the loss.

### The finding: the lending subsidiary's profit halved, and a consolidation adjustment explains the growth

The auditor's "Other Matters" paragraph (results p.4) gives the two subsidiaries, Si Creva and the new Invincible Minds, before consolidation adjustments: total revenue ₹463.59 Cr for Q2 and ₹927.39 Cr for H1, PAT ₹20.46 Cr for Q2 and ₹64.09 Cr for H1. Note 11 (p.9) gives standalone parent PAT of ₹71.87 Cr in Q2 and ₹51.94 Cr in Q1.

My arithmetic, not filed:
- Subsidiary PAT in Q1 was ₹64.09 − ₹20.46 = ₹43.63 Cr. In Q2 it was ₹20.46 Cr. That is down 53% QoQ.
- Subsidiary revenue was flat: ₹463.80 Cr in Q1 and ₹463.59 Cr in Q2. Over the same quarter, on-book AUM grew 13% (₹3,716 to ₹4,199 Cr). Revenue over average on-book AUM fell from about 51% to about 47%.
- Consolidated PAT less parent less subsidiaries = ₹125.12 − ₹71.87 − ₹20.46 = +₹32.79 Cr of consolidation adjustment in Q2. In Q1 it was ₹95.08 − ₹51.94 − ₹43.63 = −₹0.49 Cr.
- Consolidated PAT rose ₹30.04 Cr QoQ. The two entities together fell ₹3.24 Cr (parent +₹19.93, subsidiaries −₹23.17). The adjustment swung by +₹33.28 Cr. **The whole QoQ profit increase, and slightly more, came from the consolidation line.**
- Tax adds to it. The effective rate fell from 25.5% in Q1 to 18.9% in Q2, helped by an ₹8.22 Cr deferred tax credit (p.5). At Q1's rate, Q2 PAT would be about ₹115 Cr, +21% QoQ rather than +32%. PBT growth of 20.8% is the cleaner number.

What the filing does not say: what the ₹32.79 Cr adjustment is, and why Si Creva's profit halved. Notes 1-16 are silent on both. The group auditor did not review the subsidiaries. Their figures, about 92% of consolidated assets before adjustments (₹4,558.84 / ₹4,965.34 Cr, my arithmetic), rest on the subsidiaries' own auditors (p.4). That is standard wording and the conclusion is unmodified. It still means the entity that holds the loans was reviewed by a different firm.

Two readings:
- **Bull.** Profit is moving to the fee-earning parent, which is the lending-service-provider path the 22-Sep MoA change opened (Notion, 23-Sep entry). Parent turnover rose from ₹215.74 to ₹288.25 Cr QoQ (+34%), and is +115% YoY (note 11). Off-book AUM grew faster than on-book (+₹834 Cr against +₹483 Cr, my arithmetic on deck p.32). A larger intra-group service fee from Si Creva to the parent would lower Si Creva's profit and lift the parent's, and the group figure would not change. The adjustment could be a timing effect, such as Ind AS 109 fee amortisation, that unwinds [INFERENCE]. The underlying credit data is good: write-offs were ₹177.93 Cr in H1 FY27 against ₹185.29 Cr in H1 FY26 (−4% on a book 68% larger), collection efficiency was 97.12% and first-EMI DPD90 was 0.7% (results p.7, deck p.28).
- **Bear.** The headline "+32% QoQ" rests on an unexplained ₹33 Cr adjustment and a lower tax rate. The lender's own revenue stopped growing while its book grew 13%. Parent trade receivables rose from ₹98.38 Cr to ₹219.75 Cr in six months (standalone BS p.13). That is about 80 days of H1 revenue against about 51 days for FY26 (my arithmetic). Parent operating cash flow was ₹53.84 Cr for H1 against PAT of ₹123.80 Cr (p.14). On-book Stage 1 ECL coverage rose from 3.0% (Mar-26) to 3.4% (Jun) to 3.9% (Sep), while Stage 2 fell (deck p.30). Provisions are being built ahead of a growth book. The ECL allowance charge was ₹83.23 Cr in H1 FY27 against ₹37.24 Cr in H1 FY26 (p.7).

The observation that separates them: Si Creva's own H1 FY27 results. It has listed NCDs (the ₹325 Cr 11% issue of 13-Jul, note 9), so it should file half-year results under Reg 52 by about 14-Nov-2026 [INFERENCE on the rule].
- If Si Creva's Q2 PAT of about ₹20 Cr is explained by a higher intra-group fee to the parent, and its impairment did not jump, the bull reading holds.
- If its own impairment or write-offs rose, the bear reading holds.

Before that, the 29-Oct earnings call or an IR answer may explain the adjustment. Then Q3 FY27 (late January): an adjustment near zero with consolidated PAT still at or above about ₹115 Cr confirms that the run-rate is real.

### Fair value and projection consequence

- FY27 base PAT ₹441 Cr (Notion projections). H1 is ₹220.19 Cr, so H2 needs ₹220.81 Cr. The Q2 run-rate of ₹125 Cr a quarter clears that, but about ₹33 Cr of Q2 is the unexplained adjustment and about ₹10 Cr is the lower tax rate. On a run-rate cleaned of both, H2 is near the base path, not ahead of it. Helps AUM, neutral on PAT until the adjustment is explained.
- FY27 AUM base ₹11,600 Cr. The new 60%+ guidance on ₹7,066 Cr (Mar-26) implies at least ₹11,306 Cr. The guidance now roughly endorses the projection. ₹9,317 Cr plus the Q2 add of ₹1,316 Cr per quarter would give about ₹11,950 Cr (my arithmetic).
- Book. ₹2,377.47 Cr at 30-Sep (p.6) plus ₹832.20 Cr raised = ₹3,209.67 Cr before H2 profit. Add about ₹220 Cr and it ties to the ₹3,423 Cr FY27 projection.
- RoAE. The reported 21.65% is on pre-raise equity. Q2 PAT annualised over post-raise equity is about 15.6% (my arithmetic). That is below the "20%+" guidance and close to the ~17% Pillar 1 base on file.
- Funding. Finance cost fell from ₹81.87 to ₹72.38 Cr while debt rose from ₹2,043 to ₹2,227 Cr (deck p.41). Annualised cost on average debt is about 14.8% for Q1 and 13.6% for Q2 (my arithmetic). The "cost of borrowing −100 bps in H2 FY27" promise is arriving early. The deck's 13.76% average cost (p.33) is the stock measure.
- Guidance definition. "Impairment cost down 15%+ YoY" cannot mean the absolute figure: H1 impairment is ₹261.16 Cr against ₹222.53 Cr, +17.4% YoY (p.5). It must be a ratio to AUM. The deck does not say so.

### The Si Creva rights issue (index 104, 106)

₹624.15 Cr for 23,37,516 Si Creva shares at ₹2,670.14, to be invested by end-December 2026 (p.1, p.19). My arithmetic: ₹624.15 / ₹832.20 Cr = 75.0% exactly, the floor of "at least 75%" in the preferential objects. The remaining ₹208.05 Cr is the GCP slice, which on the record also funds DLG deposits and now the issue expenses (D15-5). The issue expense figure is still not disclosed.

Si Creva's CRAR is already 34.76% (deck p.7) against a 15% regulatory floor, so the infusion buys growth headroom, not compliance. The annexure gives Si Creva's turnover as ₹1,295.21 Cr in FY24, ₹1,092.48 Cr in FY25 and ₹1,541.33 Cr in FY26 (p.20). The FY25 dip matches the partner run-off pattern on the split row. Annexure A uses Si Creva's Mar-26 paid-up capital and net worth (₹1,231.98 Cr), before the ₹637.50 Cr IPO rights infusion (note 4a). That is stale but permitted.

Related party: the annexure names Ranvir Singh and Krishnan Vishwanathan as promoters. Krishnan Vishwanathan is MD & CEO of Si Creva (p.18) and also Director & CFO of the parent (deck p.17). The CFO of the listed parent runs the subsidiary whose profit just halved. That is not a flag by itself, but IR questions on the intra-group fee go to the same person on both sides.

### The other items, briefly

**IPO deviation statement (103).** No deviation; CRISIL is the monitoring agency (p.17). Of the ₹850 Cr fresh issue, ₹834.12 Cr is used and ₹15.88 Cr sits in current accounts (note 4). Issue expenses came in at ₹44.92 Cr against ₹55.92 Cr budgeted. The preferential issue proceeds are after quarter-end and not yet reported.

**ESOP allotment (107).** 69,000 shares; paid-up capital rises from 20,46,07,984 to 20,46,76,984 (p.1). By my arithmetic, ESOPs outstanding fall from 37,96,130 to 37,27,130 if there were no new grants (note 13 says none were granted in H1). The 20.84 Cr diluted count in the valuation is unchanged. The bigger point is in note 13: 96,31,080 ESOP shares were allotted in H1, all of them in Q2 (paid-up 16,84,83,022 at Jun to 17,81,14,102 at Sep). That is 5.4% of the base. Post-IPO ESOP allotments are generally not under the IPO lock-in [INFERENCE on the rule], so this was new tradeable supply in the quarter the price fell from ₹369 to ₹309.

Record correction: the 24-Oct entry said the 8,44,830 ESOP shares were exercised "since the corrigendum". They were in fact on the books by 30-Sep, since 16,84,83,022 + 96,31,080 = 17,81,14,102 (my arithmetic). So the 8-Oct corrigendum's 17,72,69,272 was a relevant-date figure, not a current one. The share math still ties.

**KPI filing (102).** Pointer only. The full document would add the prospectus KPI set; whether it includes marketing, collection or FLDG cost is NOT FOUND.

### Internal inconsistencies and silence

- Partner counts. The press release says "45+ active lending and co-lending partners" (p.2). The deck shows 45+ as on-book debt lenders and "8+" off-book partners (p.32, p.34). The DLG disclosure lists 6 parent REs plus Si Creva pools (tracker).
- DLG coverage gap is wider. DLG-covered outstanding at 30-Sep is ₹3,252.02 Cr (parent) + ₹264.08 Cr (Si Creva) = ₹3,516.10 Cr (tracker, DLG row). Off-book AUM is ₹5,118 Cr. The gap is ₹1,602 Cr, or 31% of off-book (my arithmetic), up from roughly ₹1,000-1,400 Cr logged earlier. The deck shows FLDG at "0-5%" (p.34), so some pools may carry no guarantee [INFERENCE]. That would be less risk to Kissht, but it is not stated.
- Q2 FY26 comparatives were neither audited nor reviewed (note 14). The "PAT doubles YoY" headline uses an unreviewed base.
- Missing: no FLDG cost (a MISS for the second time on the promise ledger; the H1 figure is due 30-Nov), no RBI inspection period, no LAP breakeven update (guided about Q3 FY27), no split of other expenses. LAP branches are 107 against 101 in Q1, so the "80+ branches in FY27" pace is still a MISS.

### Cross-links

- Piramal, Northern Arc, Vivriti (partner commentary row). Piramal flagged early bounce stress in salaried and digital borrowers in the South in July. Kissht's bounce rate is flat at 13.4% (13.6% in Jun) and first-EMI DPD90 is 0.7% (deck p.28). That is no read-through of stress yet. Their Q2 prints are the independent check.
- Moneyview (peer row). The ₹285 floor rests on its 16.4x multiple. Its Q2 print is due by 15-Nov.
- Supply overhang (lock-in row). Foreign corporate bodies held 29.4% at 30-Sep (deck p.19), which ties to the corrigendum's 29.49%. The lock-in ends about 6-8 Nov. The 96 lakh Q2 ESOP shares add to that supply.

### Price

₹322 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:38 IST). 52-week range ₹190-388, market cap ₹6,590 Cr, now on the post-allotment count. This is the 28-Oct close, **before** the results (board ended 19:30 on 28-Oct). It does not price the print.
- +4.2% on the ₹309 on file (25-Oct). 2.5% above the ₹314.11 issue price.
- Below all three entry lines (₹418 / ₹364 / ₹345). Inside the Stage 11 zone, 6.8% above its ₹301.5 floor. 13% above the Moneyview floor of about ₹285.
- About 2.05x post-raise book (₹156.8 per share on 20.47 Cr shares) and 14.1x FY27 base EPS of ₹22.9 (my arithmetic).
- To the Mar-2030 exits: base ₹913 gives about 36% a year and low ₹794 about 30% (3.42 years, my arithmetic). Both clear the 25% hurdle.
- Re-engage trigger 4 (price at or below ₹345-364 after the lock-in): the price leg is met, but the trigger is still before the expiry.

### Verdict

No thesis-broken trigger fires. The asset-quality test, the one the record has been waiting on, passes cleanly. T3 holds STARTING, GNPA is inside guidance and write-offs fell year on year. That moves the name toward the re-engage side on credit.

The profit print is weaker than the headline. All of the QoQ PAT growth sits in an unexplained ₹33 Cr consolidation adjustment, plus a lower tax rate, while the lending subsidiary's own profit halved on flat revenue. Until Si Creva's own results or IR explain that line, the PAT run-rate is not established.

Your "wait for the Q2 print" ruling is now met on credit. Whether it extends to the entity-split question is your call. Decision Status stays operator-only.

## Bandhan Bank: two re-entry gates pass, CFO and CASA still refuse

Status on file: WATCHLIST / AVOID, Promoter Verdict AVOID, Entry Price Max ₹199, MoS ₹135, stop ₹150, calibrated 25% entry band ₹170-180 (29-Jun PM), all tranches cancelled, zero position (Notion, properties and 29-Jun / 22-Jul entries). Position Size reads "Tactical Small (1-1.5%)", but that is the cap; the record states zero held. Three tracker rows linked; I read all three. Four filings today: results with limited review and press release (BBL/SEC/189), deck (190), ESOP grant (191), call audio links (192).

Price: ₹155 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:35 IST), 52-week ₹134-221, market cap ₹25,031 Cr. On file ₹172 (14-Oct). The ESOP letter fixes the 27-Oct NSE close at ₹151.91 (ESOP p.2).

### The quarter in numbers [FILED]

| ₹ Cr | Q2 FY26 | Q1 FY27 | Q2 FY27 | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NII | 2,589 | 2,920.58 | 2,960.48 | results p.3 |
| Other income | 546.4 | 603.83 | 704.86 | results p.3 |
| Opex | 1,824.64 | 2,166.31 | 2,108.94 | results p.3 |
| PPOP | 1,310.35 | 1,358.10 | 1,556.41 | results p.3 |
| Provisions | 1,152.56 | 682.59 | 717.54 | results p.3 |
| PAT | 111.87 | 501.67 | 621.75 | results p.3 |
| Credit cost | 3.4% | 1.8% | 1.8% | deck p.22 |
| NIM | 5.8% | 6.2% | 6.1% | deck p.28 |
| Cost of funds | 6.9% | 6.4% | 6.4% | deck p.28 |
| ROA / ROE | 0.2% / 1.8% | 1.0% / 7.7% | 1.2% / 9.3% | deck p.30 |
| C/I | 58.2% | 61.5% | 57.5% | deck p.30 |
| Wholesale segment PBT | (64.26) | (68.93) | (51.54) | results p.4 |

This quarter is a different kind of quarter from Q1. On 22-Jul the record logged that Q1 PAT was "100% provision-release-driven" with PPOP down 18.6% YoY. In Q2, PPOP rose 14.6% QoQ and 18.8% YoY (deck p.27). Opex was flat once the ₹60.83 Cr one-off Labour Code gratuity booked in Q1 is removed: ₹2,105.5 Cr in Q1 against ₹2,108.9 Cr in Q2 (results p.7 note 12). The QoQ PAT gain of ₹120 Cr came from other income (+₹101 Cr, mostly processing fees and third-party income, deck p.29), the gratuity not recurring (+₹61 Cr) and NII (+₹40 Cr). Higher provisions (-₹35 Cr) and tax (-₹43 Cr) took some of it back. My arithmetic, not filed. YoY, provisions are still the bigger driver: of the ₹510 Cr PAT gain, PPOP gave ₹246 Cr and lower provisions gave ₹435 Cr, before tax.

### Gate tests

**Thesis-broken conditions (Notion, Sec 7 / T5 / 15-Jun refinement):**
- Credit cost above 2.5% for two quarters, headline or ARC-adjusted: not fired. Headline 1.8% (deck p.22). There was no ARC sale in Q2: the NPA movement table shows nil under "NPA sale to ARC" (deck p.22), so adjusted equals headline. Rolling ARC tracker (item 21) stays ₹291.44 Cr, GREEN.
- Wholesale loss above ₹400 Cr in FY27: not fired. H1 loss ₹120.47 Cr (results p.4). That runs at about ₹241 Cr a year on H1, and ₹206 Cr on Q2 alone. My arithmetic.
- Fresh RBI or forensic action: none disclosed. The RBI nominee director's term to 23-Jun-2027 is unchanged (deck p.40).
- CASA below 27%: no new period-end print. Quarter-end CASA is 26.7% (deck p.6), the same 30-Sep figure as the 3-Oct business update. The "fires now or sustained" ruling is still pending. New evidence below.

**Re-entry gates set on 22-Jul (Step 8):**
- (a) EEB 0-90 DPD at or below 3.1% for a quarter: **passes.** SMA-0 1.4% (₹7.5bn), SMA-1 0.8% (₹4.3bn), SMA-2 0.7% (₹3.5bn) at Sep-26, a total of 2.9% against 3.5% at Jun-26 (deck p.24). The deck does not print the total; I summed the three buckets. The Jun-26 buckets sum to 3.5%, which matches the logged figure. The bear threshold on file (3.8% or more, or non-paying customers staying at 0.9% or more) is not met: non-paying customers are 0.8% (deck p.23). The 8C "cleanest metric" now favours the seasonal reading.
- (b) A permanent CFO or CIV named: **fails on the CFO leg.** The deck still lists "Vinay Jain, Chief Financial Officer (Interim)" (deck p.41). The CAE is also still interim (Sandip Bubna, deck p.42). The CIV, Nitin Arora, is listed with no "interim" tag (deck p.42). That suggests the CIV post is now permanent. This is [INFERENCE] from a dropped label, not a filed appointment.
- (c) Wholesale loss running below ₹300 Cr a year: **passes.** Both readings clear it: about ₹206 Cr on Q2 and about ₹241 Cr on H1.

**Reset gates set on 29-Jun after Mantri's exit:** (1) a replacement CFO with comparable-bank credentials: not met. (2) The new CFO publicly reaffirms the FY27 guidance bands: cannot be tested from these filings. Only audio links were filed (BBL/SEC/192); there is no transcript. The 29-Jun entry also says that if either gate is unmet within 90 days, Bandhan "moves from WATCHLIST to OBSERVATION". 90 days from 29-Jun was about 27-Sep. That clock ran out a month ago and the record has not applied it. Ruling needed.

**Monitoring checklist (Sec 8):**
- Item 1, ROA: 1.2%. Amber: the green ladder wants 1.5% or more in Q2; red is below 1.0%.
- Item 2, NIM: 6.1%. Amber: green is 6.3% or more; red is below 5.9%. Management's 22-Jul bar was to "hold 6.2%". It slipped 9 bps.
- Item 3, credit cost: 1.8%. Green, but exactly at the 1.8% Q2 line.
- Item 4, EEB slippages: not disclosed separately. Untestable.
- Item 5, EEB collection efficiency: 99.3% for the quarter and 98.6% for September (both ex-NPA, deck p.23). The press release quotes only the 99.3% (results p.10). This is now a third basis for the item-5 ruling that has been pending since 04-Oct.
- Item 6, wholesale PBT: a loss of ₹51.5 Cr. Amber: green is ₹50 Cr profit or more; red is a loss above ₹100 Cr.
- Item 8, secured mix: 57.3%. Amber: green is 60% or more in Q2.
- Item 10, bulk to term deposits: 37.3% (₹472.2bn of ₹1,265.6bn, deck p.16). Amber.
- Items 11 and 12: CRAR 18.3% and PCR including SRs 74.3%. Both green.
- Item 18, cost of funds: flat at 6.4%. Neither falling nor rising.

### What the deck adds on CASA

- The 04-Oct entry left open "whether the fall is in the average too". It is. Average CASA fell from ₹418.6bn to ₹406.2bn (-2.9% QoQ). Average deposits rose 2.4% (deck p.18). Average SA fell 2.9% and average CA fell 3.0%.
- On averages, CASA was 25.3% in Q2 against 26.7% in Q1. My arithmetic. The average ratio was also below 27% in every quarter shown back to Q2 FY26 (25.6%). So the 27% floor only works as a period-end rule; on an average basis it would always have fired. The averages do not settle the ruling. They do show that the fall is not just a quarter-end blip.
- The press release says "CASA Ratio stood at 27%" (results p.10). The deck says 26.7% (deck p.6) and the 3-Oct business update said 26.71%. The release rounds a number that broke the floor up to the floor itself. A small point, but it is the single number the thesis tests.

### Two readings

- **Bull:** the core is turning, not just the provision line. PPOP rose 14.6% QoQ on flat underlying opex. The EEB DPD pool fell from 3.5% to 2.9%. Credit cost held at 1.8% with no ARC help. The wholesale loss is narrowing. Cost of funds was flat, against the +20-25 bps management guided on 22-Jul. ROA of 1.2% already sits inside the cut 1.2-1.4% band. The stock trades at about 0.95x the 30-Sep book of ₹164 a share (₹26,470 Cr shareholders' funds over 161.11 Cr shares, deck p.32; my arithmetic).
- **Bear:**
  - GNPA stayed flat at ₹49.2bn only because ₹6.1bn was technically written off (deck p.22).
  - Fresh slippages rose to ₹11.3bn from ₹10.8bn. EEB NPAs rose from ₹31.0bn to ₹31.8bn (deck p.25).
  - CASA fell on averages too. NIM is slipping. The housing book shrank 0.4% QoQ and housing disbursements fell from ₹15.0bn to ₹13.4bn (deck p.12).
  - Wholesale is now 34% of the book (₹537.4bn, +31.6% YoY), still loss-making, with 26% of it rated BBB or below or unrated (deck p.14).
  - About ₹83 Cr of Q2 other income is legacy-cleanup income: SR provision release ₹41.5 Cr, ARC collection fees ₹17.8 Cr and bad-debt recovery ₹24.1 Cr (deck p.29). My arithmetic.
  - Both the CFO and the CAE are still interim.
- **The separating observation:** the Q3 business update, about 3-Jan-2027. A CASA ratio at or above 27% with CASA above ₹48,000 Cr confirms the bull reading. A second quarter-end below 27% fires the "sustained" leg on either wording (tracker "BANDHANBNK: CASA ratio vs 27% floor").

### Fair value and projections

- **FY27 base on file:** PAT ₹3,300 Cr, ROA 1.4%, BVPS ₹175 (Notion, Y5 projection table). H1 PAT is ₹1,123 Cr (results p.11), 34% of the base. H2 would need about ₹1,088 Cr a quarter against ₹622 Cr in Q2. At the Q2 run-rate, FY27 PAT lands near ₹2,370 Cr and March-27 book near ₹172 a share. My arithmetic. The FY27 base is out of reach. Book compounding is only slightly behind.
- **Y5 value on file:** the calibrated 29-Jun PM value of ₹533 implies about 28% a year from ₹155. My arithmetic. On the record's own numbers, the 25% hurdle now clears at this price. But that ₹533 assumes a Y5 ROE of 16.5% and a 2.0x-1.95x destination P/B. These were set before the ROA guidance cut to 1.2-1.4% (22-Jul) and the CASA break, and have not been recomputed. Item 9 (CASA 30% by FY27 end) is out of reach, so the +0.20x Y5 franchise premium is the weakest input.

### What it does not say

- No permanent CFO. No 25-Jun Capital Plan outcome. No 24-Aug AGM outcome. These record gaps have been open since July.
- Project finance: 53 accounts, ₹2,014 Cr, 5 with DCCO extended (₹317 Cr), no failed resolution (results p.7 note 11). Whether any project finance account is an NPA (items 7 and 19) is not stated.
- **Q1 wholesale restated:** this filing shows the Q1 wholesale segment loss as ₹68.93 Cr (results p.4, 30.06.2026 column). The record logged ₹77.11 Cr on 22-Jul. Note 18 says only that figures were "regrouped". The ₹8.2 Cr restatement is not explained.
- **Wholesale segment liabilities:** they jumped from ₹6,665 Cr to ₹15,875 Cr QoQ, while treasury liabilities fell from ₹14,126 Cr to ₹10,643 Cr (results p.4). Borrowings fell from ₹14,303 Cr at March to ₹10,008 Cr (results p.5). The bank appears to have swapped borrowings for bulk and wholesale deposits [INFERENCE]. That helps explain how deposits grew fast while the CASA ratio fell.
- LCR is on the website only (note 6), not in these filings.

### ESOP grant (item 28), tested

- 32,98,871 options at ₹151.91, Tranche 9 of ESOP Series 1, vesting 25% a year over four years (ESOP pp.1-2). Dilution about 0.20%; notional value about ₹50 Cr. My arithmetic.
- The price is the 27-Oct close, the day before results. The board approved the results at 15:45 and the meeting ended at 17:45 (results p.2; ESOP p.2). The grant was therefore priced at the pre-results close, inside the closed trading window, at the same meeting that approved a 5x PAT quarter. The SBEBSE pricing formula is followed as filed. This is an optics point, not a breach [INFERENCE].
- Who received the options, including any KMP or the interim CFO, is not disclosed.
- Q2 exercises: 69,348 shares for ₹94.10 lakh, about ₹136 a share (results p.6 note 5; my arithmetic).

### Trackers and price

- **Deposit mix and cost of funds (next check 31-Oct):**
  - Falsifier not hit: cost of funds is 6.4%, not above 6.65%, and NIM is 6.1%, not below 6.0%.
  - Green not met: bulk to term deposits is 37.3%.
  - Health stays Threatens on the mix. Funding cost came in better than guided.
- **Promoter ownership (IHC), 12-Nov deadline:** nothing in today's filings. The price is back from ₹172 (14-Oct) to ₹155. That is consistent with the "rumour-made" reading. The formal test date is unchanged.
- **Price against the levels:** ₹155 is below every buy line on file:
  - the ₹170-180 calibrated band;
  - the valuation card's "BUY: CMP < ₹164";
  - the cancelled ₹160-167 Tranche 3 zone.

  It is 3.3% above the ₹150 stop, which only binds a position, and none is held. It is 14.8% above the ₹135 MoS. My arithmetic.
- **Record gap:** refs BBL/SEC/181-188 are not in the record.

### Verdict

No thesis-broken condition fires. Two of the three 22-Jul re-entry gates now pass. The CFO gate fails, and the CASA leg is still waiting on your ruling. Price is below every entry line on file. The "price invites, gates refuse" ruling, open since 04-Oct, is now the live question. A second question sits beside it: whether the 29-Jun 90-day OBSERVATION rule, which lapsed around 27-Sep, still governs. That is your call.

## Sasken Technol.: margins pass, cash flat, debt-free status ends

Sasken is in COMPANIES MASTER as AVOID. Position Size reads "N/A, AVOID at CMP ₹2,120.60". No Entry Price Max or MoS property is set. The post-AR bands on the page are full BUY below ₹430, BUY on dips ₹430-550, WATCHLIST ₹550-784, AVOID above ₹784 (Notion, AR review 07-Jul, Step 13B). The Downstream Signals property is empty, so no tracker rows are linked.

Seven filings today (items 158-164) are one byte-identical 65-page PDF: board outcome, audited Q2/H1 results (consolidated and standalone), auditor reports, media release, analyst letter and investor deck. Read once in full, including all 20 image pages. This is the print the page has called the re-set master gate since 01-Aug.

**What it says, in numbers [FILED unless marked]** (all from the _cdb380 file)
- Q2 revenue ₹320.95 Cr, down 5.4% QoQ, up 25.6% YoY (p.7). PAT ₹25.59 Cr; owners ₹24.52 Cr; NCI +₹1.07 Cr (p.7).
- Software Services ₹251.58 Cr, +14.6% QoQ, segment result ₹81.00 Cr (32.2%). Product Solutions ₹69.37 Cr, down 42.0% QoQ, result ₹4.86 Cr (7.0%) (p.10, p.42).

- ETR 27.8% (₹9.86 Cr on ₹35.45 Cr PBT), deferred credit only ₹0.37 Cr (p.7).
- H1 consolidated CFO **minus ₹0.23 Cr** (p.9). Inside it: inventory minus ₹59.17 Cr, receivables/unbilled/contract assets minus ₹41.64 Cr, offset by payables and deferred revenue plus ₹36.90 Cr and provisions plus ₹16.62 Cr.
- **Current borrowings ₹80.76 Cr**, from nil at Mar-26 (p.8). Proceeds from borrowings ₹80.76 Cr, interest paid ₹0.78 Cr (p.9). The standalone balance sheet has no borrowings (p.17), so the debt sits in a subsidiary.
- **Payment of contingent consideration ₹18.68 Cr** (p.9). Not explained anywhere in the document.
- Inventory ₹94.48 Cr (Mar ₹35.31 Cr, Jun ₹65.38 Cr per deck p.54). Product Solutions segment assets ₹155.70 Cr (Mar ₹54.90 Cr) (p.10).
- Top-5 49%, Top-10 63% (p.48). $4M+ accounts 6. Active clients 101.
