- "Discussions will be based on publicly available information" and "changes may happen" (p.1).

What it does to the record:
- Closes the 23-Oct gap ("No concall date given"). The cash half of the primary falsifier has been dark since Q1 (Notion, 29-Aug). The 4-Nov balance sheet gives the first read of OCF/EBITDA against the 50% leg. The 5-Nov call is where management has to explain it.
- The deferred tracker move from 23-Oct now has a firm date. Seven quarterly rows sit at 15-Nov: proof gate, organic growth vs IPM, clean gross margin, minority interest, Keimed, hospital occupancy, diagnostic capacity. Results and call are 4-5 Nov. 5-Nov fits. Thresholds from the rows:
  - Proof gate: EBITDA margin below 5.0%, or OCF/EBITDA below 50%, fires.
  - Minority interest above 32% of PAT at H1 fires (Q4 FY26 was 38%).
  - Organic multiple of IPM at 1.2x or below fires (1.42x in Q1, falling three quarters).
  - Gross margin: Q2 is the first clean quarter, free of the sub-distribution exit and the semaglutide effect.
- The GLP-1 row's next check is 31-Oct. The call is the first place to ask about the semaglutide price cut and tirzepatide exposure, so that row fits 5-Nov too.
- Governance M-New-3: both executives whose pay drew 34.67% institutional votes against are on the speaker list (Notion, 19-Aug AGM). This is the first public venue since the AGM for a clarification.
- Oncology: the NPPA 30% trade-margin cap on 137 cancer drugs takes effect 9-Nov (tracker row, Threatens). The 5-Nov call is four days earlier and is the first chance for management to size the exposure and the distributor's share of the 30%.

Internal inconsistency in the invite (my arithmetic, not filed). It lists 13:30 IST as "UK 09:00 (BST)" and "USA 04:00 (EDT)" (p.2). UK clocks go back on 25-Oct-2026 and US clocks on 1-Nov-2026. On 5-Nov, 13:30 IST is 08:00 GMT and 03:00 EST. Both international times are one hour late. Hong Kong and Singapore at 16:00 are correct. Overseas participants who trust the invite will miss the first hour. That is host sloppiness, not a company flag, but the record's institutional-dissent bloc may be partly overseas [INFERENCE].

Record housekeeping: the 29-Aug section says the Q1 result was "filed 17 August" and the call held "10 August". A call cannot come before the result it discusses. One of the two dates on file is wrong.

### EPack PrefabTech: Ghiloth on time, but panel capacity outruns panel demand

EPack is in COMPANIES MASTER as WATCHLIST, not held. Entry is ₹145-162 and MoS ₹130, with Small (2-3%) "on entry only" (Notion, Role 1 and AR verdict). Promoter Verdict is CAUTION. No tracker rows are linked.

Items 45 and 46 are the same filing, uploaded twice. Both carry the same digital signature time, 28-Oct 20:11 IST.

**What it says** [FILED, p.1-2]:
- Commercial production of PEB structures and PUF panels started at Ghiloth, Rajasthan, on 28-Oct-2026. The consent to operate is dated the same day.
- Existing capacity: PEB 1,47,122 MT a year at about 77.5% utilisation, and PUF panels 13.1 lakh sqm a year at about 49.5%.
- Added: 11,300 MT of PEB and 8.0 lakh sqm of PUF panels.
- Investment about ₹100 Cr, funded from IPO proceeds.

**Tested against the file:**
- **Commitment.** "Ghiloth Q3 FY27" was on the FY27 delivery list (Notion, On the Horizon). 28-Oct is inside Q3. Delivered on time.
- **Capacity path.** PEB goes from 1,47,122 to 1,58,422 MT, +7.7%. The guide is 2,20,000 MT by end-FY27 (Notion, Q4 concall guidance). That leaves about 61,600 MT for Q4, almost all of it Gujarat at 50,000 MT. My arithmetic.
- **Panels are the problem.** Panel capacity rises 61%, from 13.1 to 21.1 lakh sqm, while existing panels run at about 49.5%.
  - The monitorable is Mambattu panel utilisation of 75-80% (Notion, Q4 concall guidance). 49.5% is up from 44.1% in FY26 but far short.
  - If panel volume stays flat, utilisation on the enlarged base drops to about 31%. PEB drops from 77.5% to about 72%. My arithmetic.
- **Asset turn.** At about ₹1.2 lakh per tonne and ₹1,300 per sqm (Notion, concall disclosures), the new lines make about ₹240 Cr of revenue at full use on ₹100 Cr. That is about 2.4x, against the 5-6x PEB turn logged. My arithmetic.
  - Depreciation starts now; volume has to follow.
  - Bull reading: North India demand and the order book fill PEB first.
  - Bear reading: this is the "capacity ahead of orders" question Shah asked on the Q4 call, and the panel line has missed before (Role 3).
  - **Separator:** H1 FY27 panel utilisation and operating EBITDA margin excluding other income at the 4-Nov call. Panel below 50%, or operating margin below the 9.5% soft warning, confirms the bear reading.
- **What it does not say.** No order book or customer tied to Ghiloth is given; the rationale is only "growing demand in North India". The utilisation figures have no stated period.
- **Item 47.** The Q2/H1 call is on 4-Nov-2026 at 16:30, hosted by DAM Capital. The Q4 host was PhillipCapital. MD, WTD Nikhil Bothra and CFO Rahul Agarwal are named (p.1-2). The results date is not in this batch.
- **Record gap.** The Notion page has no Q1 FY27 results review. It still calls the mid-Nov print "Q1 FY27"; that print is Q2/H1. So the Q1 gates are untested on file: operating margin at or above 9.5%, non-MSME payables, and data-centre subsidiary revenue. They now resolve with H1.

Aequs is in COMPANIES MASTER with no Decision Status, no entry, no MoS and no stop. Phase 1 verdict REWORK, model unsigned. Open rulings: Hasbro integrity vs structure, the pledge tripwire, the merger route, the institutional-holding source, and the three from the 26-Oct results. Item 11 is two CARE Ratings monitoring agency reports for the quarter to 30-Sep, one on the ₹144 Cr pre-IPO placement and one on the ₹670 Cr IPO. All 24 pages read. The company letter says "no material deviation" (p.1).

The Screener headline is the smallest item in it. ₹8.49 Cr was spent on the company's own CNC machines against ₹8.11 Cr budgeted, ₹0.38 Cr over, with no approval in place (p.15, p.19) [FILED]. Immaterial.

What matters more [FILED]:
- Commingling, flagged three times. CARE says IPO money paid through the current accounts of ASMIPL, AFCPPL and the toy subsidiaries "resulted in commingling of funds" (pp.9, 20, 21).
- ₹23.34 Cr of unused issue money sits in subsidiary bank accounts: ₹18.19 Cr IPO and ₹5.15 Cr pre-IPO. CARE notes twice that the offer document "does not explicitly specify" this (pp.4, 15-16).
- General corporate purposes this quarter went to rent and salaries. ₹4.85 Cr of pre-IPO money paid rent and employee costs at Aequs Toys and Koppal Toys Molding (p.11). ₹2.57 Cr of IPO money paid "rent and maintenance to Aequs SEZ Private Limited" from AFCPPL (p.23).
- ₹16.56 Cr of IPO money was taken this quarter as "reimbursement of issue expenses already paid" (p.21). That is the largest single use in the quarter.
- The ASMIPL machinery object was "delayed for FY26 by two quarters" (p.22).

My arithmetic, not filed:
- Issue-expense row: ₹27.06 Cr at the start plus ₹16.56 Cr in the quarter is ₹43.62 Cr. The table prints ₹27.06 Cr at the end. The column totals (₹591.10 Cr) only work with ₹43.62 Cr. Printing error in the agency's table.
- Total unused across both issues: ₹84.05 Cr. Net debt excluding leases was about ₹361 Cr at 30-Sep (Notion, 26-Oct).

What it touches on file:
- Hasbro integrity-vs-structure ledger. This is a CARE finding, not a management statement, so it is process texture of the kind already logged, not a new integrity item. But it is the first third-party finding on how money moves inside the group.
- Aequs SEZ Private Limited. It is not on the page. The corporate office address is "Aequs SEZ, No. 437/A" (p.1). Whether Aequs SEZ Pvt Ltd is a promoter-group entity is NOT VERIFIED here. If it is, IPO money is paying rent to a related party. The proposed pledge tripwire's third limb is about the promoter's "investments" being Aequs related parties. This is a different question. Worth a check against the RHP related-party list.
- Consumer and toy lines. The money is funding running costs in the line that just fired the falsification line (consumer EBITDA ₹(40.1) Cr, utilisation 17%; Notion 26-Oct). That is consistent with the bear reading, not new evidence.
- Pledge tripwire: not touched. No allotment, no upfront yet.

Odd inside the pre-IPO report: it answers "Yes" to "means of finance changed?" with comment "None", and "Yes" to "other information that may materially affect investors?" with comment "None" (pp.4-5). The subsidiary is spelled KTMCPL and KMTCPL (pp.4, 9). Agency drafting, not the company's.

### Tejas Networks: ICRA reaffirms A+ on ₹6,500 Cr after Q2

Tejas is in COMPANIES MASTER. Decision Status is WATCHLIST, not held. The entry of ₹247-309 and MoS of ₹234 are marked STALE until Role 1 reruns (Notion, 23-Oct Key Note). Thesis-broken conditions on file: FY27 revenue below ₹2,000 Cr, or net debt above ₹4,500 Cr (Notion, Thesis). Net debt was ₹4,384 Cr at 30-Sep, ₹116 Cr under the break (Notion, 17-Oct).

[FILED] ICRA's rating committee reaffirmed, "based on the announcement of the Q2FY2027 results" (Tejas_..._66ffb3.txt, p.2, p.4):

- ₹4,127 Cr fund-based limits: [ICRA]A+(Stable)/[ICRA]A1+.
- ₹40 Cr non-fund-based: [ICRA]A+(Stable)/[ICRA]A1+.
- ₹1,833 Cr term loans: [ICRA]A+(Stable). The lenders are Axis 450, DBS 477, EXIM 450, Bank of Baroda 306 and RBL 150 (p.3).
- ₹500 Cr commercial paper: [ICRA]A1+.
- Total: ₹6,500 Cr (p.1).

The test:

- **No thesis-broken condition fires.** The record has no rating trigger for Tejas. Stable outlook and no downgrade is the neutral result.
- **The record holds an open item on the "rating agency view on structural working capital"**, "Not checked this run" (Notion, 17-Oct unknowns ledger). This filing does not close it. Both letters say the rationale is "enclosed" for release on ICRA's website (p.2, p.4), but neither rationale is in the filing. The rationale is the document that matters. It will say whether A+ rests on Tata support or on Tejas's own credit, how ICRA treats the non-recourse discounted receivables, and what liquidity cushion it sees.
- **The rated limits frame the debt headroom.** ₹6,000 Cr of bank lines plus ₹500 Cr of CP sit against reported net debt of ₹4,384 Cr. The non-recourse discounted balance, which sits outside net debt, is NOT FOUND (Notion, 23-Oct). The filing gives no utilisation figure.
- Minor: the annexure says "Rating Assigned on October 27, 2026" for every line (p.3), although the action is a reaffirmation. That is probably just the date of the rating committee [INFERENCE].

Item 188 ("Rating update", 29-Oct, no PDF) is most likely Screener's echo of the same ICRA action. The full filing would add only the rationale.

Two readings, briefly:

- Bull: an A+/A1+ reaffirmation straight after a ₹207 Cr loss quarter shows the banks and the agency comfortable with liquidity, most likely on Tata parentage [INFERENCE].
- Bear: a parent-support rating says little about Tejas's own cash conversion, which is what the thesis gates on.
- What separates them: net debt at 31-Dec-2026, stated with the Q3 print around mid-Jan-2027. Below ₹4,000 Cr means cash arrived. Above ₹4,500 Cr breaks the thesis (Notion, Q2 Step 8C).

Price: ₹412 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:40 IST), 52-week ₹294-645, down from ₹424 on file (23-Oct). That is 3% above the ₹400 QIP line on file and 33% above the stale ₹309 entry top. The rating does not touch the equity question, which management left unanswered on two calls (Notion, 23-Oct).

No change. No thesis-broken trigger fires. That is your call.

### Zaggle Prepaid: unpriced Propel deal in the low-margin stream

Briefly. Zaggle is in COMPANIES MASTER as a Phase 1/2 work-in-progress record. Decision Status is unset, Phase 3 valuation has not run, and there are no entry or MoS prices (Notion, Status). Promoter TRUSTWORTHY. FTTCP 3 of 8, DEEP WATCH. No tracker rows linked.

What the filing says [FILED] (Zaggle_Prepaid_Announcement_under_Regulation_30_LODR_-__33a892.txt):
- Agreement with AGP City Gas Private Limited, signed 27-Oct-2026, letter ZAGGLE/26-27/87 (p.1).
- Zaggle provides the "Zaggle Propel platform" (p.2 row 2). Domestic. Term one year, unless terminated earlier (p.2 row 6).
- Value: none given. The fee depends on "the number of active users and number of vouchers getting redeemed" each month, so the value is "difficult to ascertain" (p.2 row 7).
- Not a related party. No promoter interest (p.2 rows 8-9).

Tested against the file:

- Which stream it feeds. Propel is the gift-card and voucher line. On file it is about 55% of gross revenue at about 94% cost, and its gross margin fell to 4% (Notion, Headline and FLAG-CASH). The fee here is tied to vouchers redeemed, which fits that line. [INFERENCE] So a win here adds gross revenue in the stream the thesis discounts. It does nothing for the quality signal the thesis wants.
- Trigger 8 (net revenue growth ex-Propel at or above 25% YoY): not helped, by construction. This is Propel.
- Trigger 4 (Propel gross margin above 5.5% for two quarters): not touched. The filing gives no fee rate.
- Trigger 3 (incentive and cashback ratio under 65% of program fees): not touched.
- Trigger 1 (consolidated OCF positive for H1 FY27): not touched. Voucher programs often need float or prepayment to the brand side. The filing does not say who funds the vouchers. [INFERENCE]
- Disclosure pattern. A Reg 30 "order" filing with no value, a one-year term and a fee that depends on usage is a soft disclosure. It tells the market a client name, not a size. The record already grades disclosure candor as the gap: management stopped reporting cross-sell after Q2 FY26 and deflected the OCF question three quarters running (Notion, Business narrative). This filing does not change that grade.

What it does not say: no minimum commitment, no user count, no fee rate, no estimate of annual value, and no word on whether AGP is a new logo or a renewal. Nothing on what AGP uses Propel for (employee rewards or customer loyalty).

## Not in COMPANIES MASTER

### Hexaware Tech.: profit jump is an earnout write-back, operations flat

Hexaware Technologies (HEXT) is NOT in COMPANIES MASTER (notion_page is null). There is no Decision Status, Entry or MoS, and no thesis to test. This is an explanation of the filings, with cross-links to companies that are on file.

**One document, ten postings.** Items 68, 69, 70, 72, 73, 74, 75, 76 and 77 are the same 40-page PDF (ref HEXT/SE/2026/114, dated 28-Oct-2026): the board outcome, the Q3 results, the press release, the limited review reports, the director letters and Annexures B to J. Item 71 is the investor presentation and factsheet (HEXT/SE/2026/115, 27 pages). I read all pages of both, including the seven scanned pages (consolidated results, segment table, notes 1 to 18, standalone results and notes, and the Genachowski letter). Caution on the Screener summaries: Hexaware's financial year is the calendar year, so this is Q3 CY26 (quarter to 30-Sep-2026), not "Q2 FY27" or "Q2 FY26" as six of the summaries say. The results also cover nine months, not a half-year.

**Price context.** Rs 572 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:37 IST). 52-week high Rs 808, low Rs 400, market cap Rs 34,965 Cr. This read was taken before the market opened on 29-Oct, and the board met 17:32 to 19:46 IST on 28-Oct. So the price is the pre-results close and carries no reaction. It is 29% below the 52-week high and 43% above the low (my arithmetic).

#### What the filings say, in numbers

Headline (press release, PDF p.17): revenue USD 411.9 Mn, +1.6% QoQ, +4.3% YoY. Constant currency +1.7% QoQ, +4.5% YoY. In rupees Rs 39,331 Mn, +2.3% QoQ, +12.9% YoY. [FILED]

The rupee gap is exchange rate, not growth. The factsheet gives the average rate at 95.48 against 88.19 a year ago (factsheet p.20, a rupee 8.3% weaker). Dollar growth of 4.3% is the real figure. [FILED, my arithmetic on the gap]

| Item | Q3 CY26 | Q2 CY26 | Q3 CY25 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Reported EBIT (Rs Mn) | 4,791 (12.2%) | 5,236 (13.6%) | 5,124 (14.7%) |
| Adjusted EBIT margin | 13.8% | 13.6% | 14.7% |
| Reported PAT (Rs Mn) | 4,294 (10.9%) | 3,302 (8.6%) | 3,699 (10.6%) |
| Basic EPS (Rs) | 7.06 | 5.41 | 6.09 |
| Adjusted EBITDA margin | 15.6% | 14.1% | 17.6% |

Source: consolidated results p.10 to 11 (scanned), press release p.20, factsheet p.16 (presentation PDF) and presentation p.25.

#### 1. The 30% profit jump is mostly an accounting write-back

PAT rose 30.0% QoQ. Two non-operating items sit inside it (notes 6 and 7, results p.12):
- A gain of Rs 1,663 Mn from re-measuring contingent consideration (earnout) on past acquisitions. It is non-cash. The note does not name the acquisition.
- Restructuring expense of Rs 597 Mn for the Netherlands, a cost.
- Net effect before tax: about +Rs 1,066 Mn (my arithmetic). That is 25% of reported PAT.

The company's own adjusted PAT margin is 9.4% (presentation p.6). On Rs 39,331 Mn that is about Rs 3,697 Mn (my arithmetic, not filed), against Rs 3,302 Mn in Q2. So adjusted PAT is up about 12% QoQ, not 30%.

Where does that 12% come from? My arithmetic on the P&L lines, adjusted for the items above:
- Adjusted EBIT: about +Rs 197 Mn (Rs 5,433 Mn, from USD 56.9 Mn at the average rate, against Rs 5,236 Mn).
- Exchange loss shrank from Rs 783 Mn to Rs 297 Mn: +Rs 486 Mn.
- Finance cost fell from Rs 328 Mn to Rs 254 Mn: +Rs 74 Mn.
- Other income excluding exchange fell from Rs 282 Mn to Rs 128 Mn: minus Rs 154 Mn.
- Net: about +Rs 603 Mn. Exchange and finance cost together are Rs 560 Mn, over 90% of the lift.

So the operating business added roughly 3% to adjusted EBIT. The rest of the improvement is lower exchange losses and lower interest. [INFERENCE]

The headline EBITDA margin of 18.2% (presentation p.25) includes the Rs 1,663 Mn gain (4.2 points). Adjusted EBITDA margin is 15.6%. A year ago it was 17.6%. That is 200 bps lower YoY on the company's own adjusted basis.

The reported tax rate of 21.0% (presentation p.10) against 25.1% in Q2 also looks low. If the earnout gain is not taxable (my assumption, not stated), tax on the other profit was Rs 1,140 Mn on Rs 3,771 Mn, about 30%. So there is no tax tailwind in the underlying number. [INFERENCE]

Two readings:
- Bull: the adjusted EBIT margin is up 17 bps QoQ. The Netherlands cleanup removes a loss-making unit, so Q4 would be clean of both charges. Banking (+6.6% QoQ) and technology/platform (+7.2%) grew well.
- Bear: reported EBIT margin fell 144 bps QoQ and 253 bps YoY. Adjusted EBIT margin is still 88 bps below a year ago. Earnout reversals keep coming (Rs 3,820 Mn in FY25, Rs 1,927 Mn in nine months of 2026), paired with customer-relationship impairments (Rs 1,696 Mn in FY25, Rs 556 Mn in Q3). Both say acquired businesses are earning less than the deal assumed.

The observation that separates them: Q4 CY26 adjusted EBIT margin. A figure above 14.0% with exchange loss near zero says operations improved. A figure at or below 13.8% means the Q3 gain was currency and interest. The result lands around late Jan 2027 (the date is not in these documents).

#### 2. Cost lines: other expenses and corporate costs are growing faster than revenue

- Other expenses Rs 10,678 Mn, +9.7% QoQ. Take out the Rs 556 Mn impairment and Rs 19 Mn CEO fees: Rs 10,103 Mn, +3.8% QoQ (my arithmetic). Still faster than the 2.3% rupee revenue growth.
- Segment profit is Rs 14,058 Mn (35.7% of revenue) against Rs 13,959 Mn (36.3%) in Q2 and 35.3% a year ago. Unallocated corporate expenses are Rs 8,396 Mn. Without the Rs 575 Mn of one-offs they are Rs 7,821 Mn, 24% above Rs 6,284 Mn a year ago, against rupee revenue up 12.9% (my arithmetic, segment table, results p.11). That is where the YoY margin loss sits. The filing gives no breakdown of this cost.
- Segment margins YoY (my arithmetic from the segment table): Financial Services 29.8% against 32.1%. Manufacturing and Consumer 35.2% against 38.6%. Banking 43.3% against 35.2%. Healthcare and Insurance 36.8% against 31.6%. Financial Services is the largest vertical (29.1% of revenue) and its margin is falling.
- Travel and Transportation fell 3.5% QoQ and 8.5% YoY. It is the highest-margin segment (44.2%) and 6.9% of revenue.
- Headcount 35,887, +1,381 QoQ. Only 386 are in IT. The other 995 are BPS (12,705 against 11,710, +8.5%). Revenue per head, annualised (my arithmetic), is USD 45.9k against USD 47.0k a year ago, down 2.4%.
- Utilisation 84.9% against 84.8%. The definition changed from Q2 (platform staff excluded) and prior periods were not restated. The presentation says Q2 was helped 80 bps by the change. So the trend is not like for like. [FILED]
- Americas, 73.5% of revenue, grew +1.5% QoQ but only +1.1% YoY. Europe +12.8% YoY and Asia Pacific +20.0% YoY. Hexaware bought Consulting Professionals Services (UK) on 28-May-2026 (note 14, results p.13): Rs 735 Mn upfront plus up to Rs 606 Mn contingent. Revenue from it is NOT FOUND, so organic against acquired growth cannot be split. Europe's strength may be partly this. [INFERENCE]

#### 3. Cash conversion is slipping while the quote says "strong"

The CFO's quote says "strong cash conversion". The numbers:
- LTM operating cash flow to reported profit is 109.6%, against 124.7% in Q2 and 126.6% a year ago (factsheet p.16). Down 1,516 bps QoQ.
- Nine-month operating cash flow Rs 7,221 Mn against Rs 9,243 Mn (down 21.9%). Working capital absorbed Rs 6,589 Mn against Rs 4,732 Mn (press release p.22).
- Trade receivables and unbilled revenue Rs 33,177 Mn against Rs 25,431 Mn at Dec-25, up 30.5% (press release p.21). DSO is 77 days (billed 41, unbilled 36), against 67 at Dec-25 and 73 a year ago (presentation p.10). Receivables grew far faster than nine-month revenue.
- Cash including mutual funds and restricted cash: Rs 19,052 Mn (USD 199 Mn) against Rs 21,324 Mn at Dec-25.

The ratio is still above 100%. But the reported profit it is divided by contains non-cash earnout gains, so the ratio is not clean in either direction. [INFERENCE]

Dividend load: a second interim dividend of Rs 9.50 per share was declared on 20-Oct (note 17, results p.13). With the Rs 8.50 paid earlier, that is Rs 18.00 for 2026 against Rs 11.50 in 2025. At Rs 572 that is a 3.1% yield on the full year (my arithmetic). Paying Rs 9.50 on about 608.9 Mn shares (610 Mn less 1.07 Mn treasury) is about Rs 5,785 Mn (my arithmetic). Cash would fall to roughly Rs 13,270 Mn before any Q4 operating inflow. Contingent consideration of Rs 3,583 Mn is still on the balance sheet. Record and payment dates are NOT FOUND in these filings.

#### 4. Governance: a CEO handover, a two-for-two board swap, and an AI leader leaves

**CEO.** Vivek Jetley became CEO effective 28-Oct. R. Srikrishna ceased as CEO and Executive Director at close of business the same day (outcome letter p.4). Reasons are only by reference to the 2-Sep intimation, not repeated. Costs in the quarter: separation provision Rs 163 Mn, share option cost reversal (Rs 90 Mn), and professional fees Rs 19 Mn (notes 8 and 12). Net about Rs 92 Mn (my arithmetic). Srikrishna still signed the results at 20:53 IST on 28-Oct as CEO and Executive Director.

Silence: the filing does not say Jetley joins the board. With Srikrishna gone as Executive Director, the executive seat on the board looks vacant. Jetley's pay, background and any joining grants are NOT FOUND in these papers.

**Directors.** Neeraj Bharadwaj and Julius Genachowski, both Non-Executive Non-Independent, resigned at close of business 28-Oct, each "due to other commitments and responsibilities" (letters on pp.28 and 30, scanned). The wording is the same. They are replaced from 29-Oct by Arjun Shah (Managing Director, Carlyle) and Gregory Zeluck (Partner, Co-Head of Asia Buyout, Carlyle, at the firm since 1998). Both are described as "part of Promoter Group" (Annexures B and C, pp.26 to 27). So the filing confirms the incoming pair are Carlyle and promoter group. It does not say who nominated the outgoing pair. From my own knowledge both outgoing directors are Carlyle-linked, but that is not in the filing: verify. [INFERENCE]

Both new directors are additional directors, subject to shareholder approval. Term of appointment is not given (only the start date). Zeluck is on no committee. Shah goes onto the CSR and ESG committees (Annexure J, p.40).

Internal inconsistency: Annexure J says the committee composition is "w.e.f. October 28, 2026", but Shah only joins on 29-Oct (Annexure B). Minor, but the committee seat precedes the appointment by a day. The name "Jospeh McLaren Quinlan" is also misspelled in Annexure J.

Two readings:
- Bull: a senior Carlyle partner joins the board at the moment of a CEO change. That is a promoter leaning in.
- Bear: the shares were relisted on 19-Feb-2025 (note 3). Board reshuffles with senior sponsor people are what can come before a stake sale. The filing says nothing on promoter holding, lock-in or any sale plan. [INFERENCE]

What would separate them: the Dec-2026 shareholding pattern (due about mid-Jan 2027) or any block or bulk deal in HEXT. A fall in promoter holding of more than 2 percentage points by that filing would support the bear reading. Current promoter holding is NOT FOUND in these filings.

**Senior management.** Girish Pai, EVP and Global Head of Data, resigned by letter dated 27-Oct, last day 13-Nov-2026, "to pursue other career opportunities" (pp.38 to 39). The letter is addressed to "Vivek and SriKrishna", so he gave notice before the handover. Notice is 17 days. No successor is named. The Annexure says "Global Head - Data"; the letter says "Head of the Data & AI Service Line". The presentation (p.4) leads with AI for IT, AI for Business and "ZeroVity", and a new Claude Partner Network preferred-partner status. The Data and AI head leaving two weeks after the CEO change is the most strategically relevant exit. Watch item: further senior management exits in Q4 filings. Three or more by the Q4 print would read as a leadership reset, one more as noise. [INFERENCE]

**Late filing flag.** The board meeting ended at 19:46 IST (outcome letter p.5). The outcome letter is digitally signed 21:58:55 IST, 2 hours 12 minutes after. The results were signed by the CEO at 20:53 IST, 67 minutes after. The investor presentation is signed 22:22. If the 30-minute outcome window under Regulation 30 and Regulation 33 applies as I understand it, the filing came after it. The exchange acceptance time is not in the PDF, so check the BSE or NSE timestamp before treating this as a breach. [INFERENCE]

#### 5. Entity rationalisation: small, but two disclosures are thin

- **Netherlands wind-down.** Mobiquity BV, a step-down subsidiary in digital customer experience consulting, goes into voluntary liquidation (Annexure H, p.36). Its 2025 turnover was Rs 1,746 Mn (1.3% of consolidated) and net worth was NEGATIVE Rs 311 Mn (minus 0.5%). The restructuring provision in the quarter was Rs 647 Mn: Rs 603 Mn severance and Rs 44 Mn legal fees. Net of a Rs 72 Mn lease write-back and plus Rs 22 Mn accelerated amortisation, that gives the Rs 597 Mn P&L line (my arithmetic: 647 minus 72 plus 22). Completion is expected by the end of Q2 FY27 (June 2027, note 7). Note 15(b) shows Mobiquity Consulting BV was merged into Mobiquity BV only on 1-Jan-2026, with court clearance on 30-Jan-2026. So the merger and the liquidation are nine months apart. Annual turnover of Rs 1,746 Mn is about Rs 437 Mn a quarter, roughly USD 4.6 Mn, about 1.1% of quarterly revenue (my arithmetic, at 2025 turnover). The "Others" revenue line has already fallen from USD 14 Mn to USD 10 Mn QoQ (factsheet p.18). The link is possible, not proven. [INFERENCE]
- **Link to the earnout reversal.** Notes 6(a) and 6(b) do not name the acquisition whose earnout was written back and whose customer relationships were impaired. The Netherlands closure, the negative net worth and the earnout gain point at the same group of acquisitions (Mobiquity or Softcrylic), but that is inference. The first thing to ask on the call (8:00 IST, 29-Oct) is which acquisition, and how much goodwill sits behind it. Goodwill is Rs 38,172 Mn, 52.9% of total equity (my arithmetic, balance sheet p.21). It is not impaired in these results, while customer relationships were. The question is whether the year-end test changes that.
- **Mergers.** IT Glitterz LLC (turnover USD 0.75 Mn) merges into Hexaware Technologies Inc (USD 591.76 Mn). Identity and Access Solutions Canada Inc (CAD 2.93 Mn) merges into Hexaware Technologies Canada Ltd (CAD 39.11 Mn). Annexure G (pp.34 to 35) names no expected completion date and no approvals needed.
- **Slump sale.** Tech SMC Square India Pvt Ltd moves from Hexaware Technologies Inc to Tech SMC Squared (GCC) India Pvt Ltd. Turnover Rs 69.5 Mn (0.05% of group), net worth Rs 55.1 Mn (0.09%). Price will be set by a registered valuer, related party at arm's length, completion by 30-Jun-2027 (Annexure F, p.32). Immaterial. It says nothing on the tax cost of an Indian slump sale.
- **Mauritius subsidiary.** A new wholly owned company with up to USD 1 Mn in tranches (p.3). Purpose, business and activity are NOT given: "will be intimated in due course". Regulation 30 normally asks for those details at the time. Silence is the point to note.

#### 6. ESOP allotment and share count

13,00,000 shares of face value Re 1 were allotted at Rs 382.5 to the Hexaware Employees Benefit Trust (p.5). That is about Rs 497 Mn (my arithmetic) and 0.21% of the 610 Mn shares. The price is 33% below the Rs 572 market price. The Trust passes shares to employees only on exercise. It held 1,073,623 treasury shares at 30-Sep (note 5), so after this the Trust holds about 2.37 Mn, excluded from EPS until exercised. The allotment is more than twice all shares issued on option exercise in the whole nine months (599,506, note 4). Dilution is small. It looks like pre-funding of a larger exercise wave. [INFERENCE]

#### 7. Risks stated in the notes

- Natsoft Corporation and Updraft LLC claim USD 500 Mn for alleged patent infringement and breach of contract (note 16, results p.13). At 95.83 that is about Rs 47,915 Mn: 66% of equity, 13.7% of market cap and 2.5 times cash of USD 199 Mn (my arithmetic). The Northern District of Illinois dismissed the federal patent claims. The plaintiff then filed amended claims and the matter continues. Management says the claim is without merit and not expected to be material. Which claims survive, and any trial date, are NOT FOUND. This is a stated claim, not a finding.
- A European customer disputed USD 9.1 Mn (Rs 782 Mn). The group provided for it in 2025. The customer is now under preliminary insolvency in Berlin. Recovery proceedings in New York continue. Already charged, so no new hit.
- 22 subsidiaries were reviewed by other auditors (limited review report, p.6): revenue before consolidation adjustments Rs 5,697 Mn in Q3, about 14.5% of consolidated revenue, profit Rs 118 Mn. Conclusion unmodified. Normal for a global group, worth knowing.
- The standalone statement (p.15) shows Q3 PAT Rs 2,018 Mn against Rs 2,706 Mn a year ago (down 25%) and nine-month PAT Rs 6,156 Mn against Rs 6,792 Mn (down 9%). Standalone note 13 says the company voluntarily changed how later changes in contingent consideration are booked (against investment cost, not profit) and restated Q2 2026 and nine months 2025 on a standalone basis. Consolidated is not affected.

#### 8. Client breadth: the headline leaves something out

The press release says one client was added in the USD 20 Mn+ band (16 to 17). The factsheet (p.19) shows the rest: USD 10 Mn+ clients fell from 34 to 32 QoQ, USD 1 Mn+ from 206 to 205, and USD 75 Mn+ clients are 2 against 3 a year ago. Top 5 clients are 24.7% of revenue, down from 26.0%, and top 10 are 35.4%. Concentration is easing, but the middle of the pyramid is thinner.

#### Cross-links to companies on file
- Sasken Technol.: a mid-size technology services name. Comparison point for margin and utilisation trends in IT services.
- R Systems Intl.: another mid-size IT services company in this run. Same comparison on BFSI and Americas growth.
- Fractal Analyt.: a data and AI business. Hexaware's Head of Data and AI leaving matters if talent in this lane is scarce. Also a read-across for the "AI factory" deal language in Hexaware's wins.
- TAAL Tech: engineering services. Draws on the same offshore talent pool.
- Theme: the Anthropic Claude Partner Network preferred-partner status (presentation p.4). No terms or revenue were given.

#### Bottom line
No thesis exists for this name, so no trigger fires and there is no position to move. What the filing shows: dollar revenue growth of 4.3% YoY is modest. The profit headline is largely a non-cash gain. Operating profit is up about 3% QoQ, with most of the lift from exchange and interest. Receivable days and cash conversion are moving the wrong way. Governance has several changes at once. Whether any of that belongs in COMPANIES MASTER is your call.

### Vaibhav Global: reported growth is rupee weakness, retail profit fell a third

Vaibhav Global (VGL) is not in COMPANIES MASTER. It sells fashion jewellery and lifestyle goods to US, UK and German households through TV channels (Shop LC, Shop TJC, Ideal World) and websites. It also makes jewellery in Jaipur and sells to its own channels and some outside buyers (the "B2B" segment). Seven items today. Three of them (Board Meeting Outcome, Corporate Action-Dividend, Financial Results) are the same 16-page PDF filed under three headings (text is byte-identical), so I read it once.

**The headline and what is under it**

The press release headline is "Revenue up 7.0% to ₹938.4 Cr" (Press release p.2) [FILED]. Under it:
- The rupee was about 9% weaker against the US dollar and about 10% weaker against the pound and euro (Press release p.4) [FILED]. Constant-currency retail (B2C) revenue fell 3.2% in Q2, and group revenue fell 1.7% (Press release p.3) [FILED]. For H1 the constant-currency figures are minus 0.5% (B2C) and minus 0.3% (group). So all of the reported growth is currency.
- Q2 EBITDA ₹76.2 Cr against ₹88.1 Cr a year ago, down 13.5%. Margin 8.1% against 10.0% (Press release p.3) [FILED].
- Q2 profit before tax ₹44.4 Cr against ₹60.2 Cr, down 26.3% (Results PDF p.3) [FILED]. Profit after tax ₹41.7 Cr against ₹47.6 Cr, down only 12.3%. The gap is tax. The Q2 tax charge is ₹2.7 Cr, a 6.1% rate, against 21.1% a year ago and 20.4% in Q1 (Results PDF p.3, my arithmetic). The deck says Q2 includes a ₹4.34 Cr foreign tax credit (Presentation p.11) [MGMT]. At last year's 21% rate, Q2 profit would be about ₹35 Cr, down about 26% (my arithmetic, not filed).
- EBITDA here includes other income. PBT 4,442 + finance cost 490 + depreciation 2,692 = 7,625 lakh, which is the ₹76.2 Cr. Other income in it was ₹8.2 Cr (Q2 FY26: ₹10.5 Cr). Without it, Q2 operating EBITDA is about ₹68.0 Cr (7.2% of revenue) against ₹77.6 Cr (8.8%), down 12.3% (my arithmetic from Results PDF p.3).

**Where the money was lost**
- Retail (B2C) EBITDA fell 35.3% to ₹33.8 Cr. Margin 3.9% against 6.3% (Press release p.3, my division).
- UK EBITDA fell 66.8% to ₹7.3 Cr. UK margin 2.9% against 8.8%. UK revenue fell 7.0% in pounds and units fell 5.9% (Press release p.3-4) [FILED].
- US EBITDA ₹28.7 Cr against ₹31.1 Cr. US revenue fell 4.1% in dollars while units rose 4.8%. That implies average price per unit fell about 8.5% in dollars (my arithmetic: 0.959 / 1.048). US sold more pieces for less money. Budget Pay (interest-free instalments) rose to 65% of B2C from 62% (Presentation p.16) [MGMT].
- Europe ex-UK: revenue up 8.0% in euros, EBITDA a loss of ₹2.2 Cr against a loss of ₹0.9 Cr. The release calls Europe "growth in constant currency" but the EBITDA got worse.
- B2B EBITDA rose 18.5% to ₹42.4 Cr on revenue of ₹70.9 Cr. That is a 60% margin on the revenue shown, which cannot be an ordinary margin. B2B revenue is stated "net of inter-segment eliminations" (Press release p.3) but its EBITDA is not. So most of that profit is the group's own sourcing and manufacturing margin on goods it sells to its retail arms [INFERENCE]. B2B is now 56% of group EBITDA against 41% a year ago. The split between retail and B2B depends on transfer pricing, so the more useful number is the group total.
- The cost line that moved with it: content and broadcasting expense (TV airtime and production) was ₹204.6 Cr, up 17.6%, against TV revenue up 2.7% (Results PDF p.7 note 5; Presentation p.15). It was 21.8% of group revenue against 19.8%, a rise of 198 basis points, which is the whole EBITDA margin fall of 191 basis points. Part of the rupee cost rise is currency, but the gap is wide.

**Is the margin story the company tells true?** Partly.
- The deck says gross margin was 65.5%, up 196 basis points (Presentation p.11) [MGMT]. On the face of the results (revenue less materials, purchases and change in inventory) I get 67.9% against 65.6%, about 230 basis points up. The definitions differ (not stated), but the direction agrees. Gross margin did improve. In-house brands were 57% of B2C revenue against a 50% target met a year early (Press release p.2; Presentation p.25).
- The margin walk (Presentation p.12): gross margin +2.0 points, digital marketing minus 2.0, shipping minus 1.0, SG&A minus 0.7, other income minus 0.3. The company spent its gross margin gain on customer acquisition and freight, and sales did not follow.

**The H1 comparison is flattered by a refund.** The deck footnote says Q1 revenue included a ₹25.6 Cr tariff refund (Presentation p.10) [MGMT]. Nothing else says where it fell in profit.
- Revenue without it: Q1 ₹891.5 Cr (+9.6%), H1 ₹1,829.8 Cr (+8.2%), against the reported +9.7%.
- If the whole refund reached EBITDA (not filed; refunds usually do), H1 EBITDA without it is about ₹152.7 Cr against ₹162.7 Cr, down 6.2%, and H1 margin about 8.3% against 9.6% (my arithmetic, not filed). Q1 without it would be about ₹76.4 Cr, the same as Q2's ₹76.2 Cr. On that reading, the underlying run-rate was already about ₹76 Cr a quarter in Q1, before the Shopify move.
- The release says H1 EBITDA and PAT grew "broadly in line with revenue" (Presentation p.10) [MGMT]. H1 PAT of ₹98.1 Cr is up 15.2% (Presentation p.10). H1 tax is 14.9% of PBT against 19.7%, so H1 PBT grew only 8.7% (Results PDF p.3). That sentence holds only if the refund and the tax credit are left in.

**Digital: two filings give two different digital bases.**
- The press release says e-commerce was 42.4% of B2C in Q2 against 42.8% a year ago, and 43.7% for H1 against 43.7% (Press release p.4). The regional percentages in the same table rebuild to the same figures: about ₹352 Cr digital last year, so digital grew about 4% in rupees, which is about minus 4 to minus 5% in constant currency (my arithmetic, using the 8.5-point currency gap between B2C rupee growth and constant-currency growth).
- The deck says digital revenue was ₹367.5 Cr, up 9.1% (Presentation p.11, p.15). That means a prior-year base of ₹337 Cr, and 337 / (337 + 487) is 40.9%, not 42.8%. For H1 the deck says +14.7% (base about ₹667 Cr), but the press release percentages imply a base near ₹698 Cr (+9.7%).
- The two filings differ by about ₹15 Cr a quarter in last year's digital base. No restatement or definition change is stated. Also the press release prints the Q2 share change as "(4) bps"; 42.4 less 42.8 is 40 bps.
- Whichever is right, digital fell in rupees from Q1's ₹398 Cr to ₹367.5 Cr (minus 7.7%) and its share fell from 45.1% to 42.4%. The Shopify migration was meant to lift this number. It fell.
- The deck keeps the FY27 target of 50% digital (up from 44% in FY26) (Presentation p.37) [MGMT]. H1 is 43.7%. If H2 B2C revenue equals H1's ₹1,749 Cr, H2 digital must be about ₹983 Cr, a 56% share and 28% above H1 (my arithmetic, not filed). Possible in a holiday quarter, but it needs a clear break from Q2.

**The cash flow the press release does not mention** (Results PDF p.5, p.4) [FILED]
- Operating cash flow was ₹23.6 Cr in H1 against ₹68.0 Cr. Inventory rose ₹114.3 Cr in cash terms against ₹43.3 Cr. Inventory on the balance sheet is ₹933.9 Cr against ₹811.3 Cr at March, up 15.1% in six months while revenue rose 9.7% on a reported basis.
- Capex was ₹63.2 Cr against ₹10.8 Cr. Capital work in progress went from ₹13.4 Cr to ₹52.0 Cr. The filing does not say what for. The UK move of four warehouses and two studios into one headquarters is the likely candidate [INFERENCE]. The deck says the Shopify migration cost about a third of the earlier re-platforming (Presentation p.26) [MGMT], with no figure.
- Free cash flow (operating cash flow less capex) was about minus ₹39.7 Cr against plus ₹57.3 Cr a year earlier (my arithmetic). Dividends paid were ₹52.9 Cr. The deck says dividends are "funded entirely from FCF" for 15 to 20 straight quarters (Presentation p.32) [MGMT]. For H1 FY27 that does not hold on the filed cash flow, unless the company means free cash flow before working capital, which it does not say.
- Cash fell from ₹333.5 Cr to ₹242.7 Cr. Borrowings, all in the parent, rose from ₹112.2 Cr to ₹129.6 Cr. My net cash (cash, other bank balances, current investments, less borrowings; leases excluded) is about ₹170 Cr against ₹278 Cr at March, a fall of about ₹108 Cr (my arithmetic). The deck says "₹186 Cr net cash" (Presentation p.20) [MGMT]. I cannot reconcile the ₹16 Cr gap; no definition is given.
- The deck has a new format called "Rising Auction", where customers bid on "tail inventory clearing aged SKUs" (Presentation p.27). That is the company saying it holds aged stock.
- In the company's favour: bad-debt allowances were ₹16.3 Cr in H1 against ₹20.6 Cr, and receivables are flat at ₹338.7 Cr despite the higher Budget Pay share.

**Two readings**

Bull (has evidence): the cause is one-off. The Shopify migration and UK consolidation are done (Press release p.2). Europe, B2B and in-house brands grew. Gross margin rose about 200 basis points. Q3 (October to December) is the seasonally strongest quarter. The inventory build and capex are preparation for it. AI tooling cut re-platforming cost (Presentation p.26).

Bear (has evidence): reported growth is all currency; retail EBITDA is down 35%; the UK lost two-thirds of its EBITDA; digital share fell after a migration meant to raise it; H1 EBITDA growth needs the Q1 refund; PAT was held up by a 6% tax rate; cash flow turned negative with inventory up 15% and aged-stock clearance on the menu. The FY30 revenue target of ₹5,000 to 5,500 Cr (Presentation p.37) implies about 7.9% to 10.5% a year in rupees from ₹3,692 Cr in FY26 (my arithmetic), while constant-currency growth is now around zero.

The one observation that separates them: Q3 FY27 (October to December, results expected late January or February 2027, date not filed). Bull needs B2C constant-currency growth at or above 0%, digital share back at or above Q1's 45.1%, and UK EBITDA margin back above about 6% (H1 was 3.8%). Bear is confirmed if constant-currency B2C is still negative and inventory is above about ₹900 Cr at December. The thresholds are mine.

**What the filings do not say**
- No quantified Shopify impact: no lost revenue, no one-off cost, no recovery date. "Temporary" is the only word offered.
- No October trading comment, although the quarter started three weeks ago.
- No full explanation for the 6% tax rate. The ₹4.34 Cr foreign tax credit is about 10% of PBT, so the rate would be about 16% without it (my arithmetic). That is still below the 20% to 21% of the prior year and Q1, and the rest is not explained.
- No definition of "net cash" and no free cash flow figure in the release.
- Nothing on capex purpose or UK inventory "temporarily offline" in rupees.

**Smaller items**
- Dividend: second interim of ₹1.50 a share, record date Wednesday 4-Nov-2026, to be paid within 30 days (Results PDF p.1; Record Date letter). Total declared this year so far ₹3.00 (Results PDF p.7). At 16.78 Cr shares the new dividend is about ₹25 Cr (my arithmetic). At ₹188 it is a 0.8% yield for this payment.
- ESOP: 39,489 shares allotted on 28-Oct to the employee welfare trust. Paid-up capital is now 16,78,09,185 shares (ESOP letter) [FILED]. The 30-Sep capital in the results was 3,352.48 lakh (16,76,24,000 shares), so about 1.85 lakh shares were allotted in October, of which this filing is only 39,489. About 1.46 lakh were allotted earlier in the month (my arithmetic). Dilution 0.11%. H1 allotments were 6,00,232 shares at ₹2 to ₹188.95 (Results PDF p.7).
- RSU grant of 8,018 units at ₹2, vesting 20/30/50% over three years (Results PDF p.1-2). Immaterial.
- Auditor: B S R & Co. LLP, limited review, unmodified conclusion. Other auditors reviewed four subsidiaries that carry ₹137.4 Cr of Q2 revenue before consolidation adjustments, about 15% of group revenue (Results PDF p.13) [FILED]. Normal for a group with foreign subsidiaries.
- The board met 18:00 to 23:30 IST in Austin, USA (Results PDF p.2, p.7). Results were filed at 00:20 IST on the 29th and the press release at 01:32 IST. A five-and-a-half-hour results meeting is long, but nothing in the filing explains it. No finding.
- Press release flaw: "Europe (excluding UK): Q2 FY27 Revenue stood at ₹110.0" has no unit (Press release p.2). It is ₹110.0 Cr.

**Price context:** ₹188 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:40 IST), 52-week range ₹174 to ₹293, market cap ₹3,151 Cr. That is 36% below the high and 8% above the low. The read was taken before the market opened on the results, so it is the 28-Oct close, not a reaction. On trailing EPS of about ₹16.7 (FY26 ₹15.97, less H1 FY26 ₹5.12, plus H1 FY27 ₹5.87, my arithmetic) the stock is about 11x. At a normal 21% tax rate the trailing EPS is about ₹13.7 and the multiple about 14x (my arithmetic).

**Where it moves:** not in master, so no trigger to fire. If the name were added, the filing says the digital and UK recovery is the whole case, and Q3 is the first test.

**Cross-links on file:** USD and GBP earners show a roughly 9% currency tailwind in reported growth this quarter. Check constant currency for Aeroflex, Aequs, Syngene Intl. and Fractal Analyt. (all on file) before reading their growth as organic. No direct business link to any on-file name.

### Sky Gold & Diamonds: profits double on revenue, but the cash story needs a reading

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. I have the investor presentation (item 179) and the call invite (item 178). I do not have the filed results statement or the auditor's review report in this set. Every number below is the company's own presentation [FILED but unaudited, and company-prepared].

**Headline numbers [FILED] (deck p.6, p.9, p.10).**
- Q2 FY27 revenue ₹2,390.7 Cr, up 61% YoY and 18.8% on Q1 (₹2,012.8 Cr).
- Q2 EBITDA ₹189.8 Cr, margin 7.9% (Q2 FY26 6.7%, Q1 7.8%).
- Q2 PAT ₹129.4 Cr, up 93%, margin 5.4%.
- H1 revenue ₹4,403.5 Cr (+68%), EBITDA ₹346.5 Cr (+102%), PAT ₹234.3 Cr (+112%).
- Gross margin 9.6% against 8.2% a year ago. Management credits more value-added and export business (p.16).
- H1 cash from operations is +₹136.7 Cr, against minus ₹100.9 Cr in H1 FY26 (p.12). FY26 full year was minus ₹44.9 Cr (p.38).

**What improved.** Operating cash flow has turned positive for the first time in the three years shown (FY24 minus ₹137.2 Cr, FY25 minus ₹272.2 Cr, FY26 minus ₹44.9 Cr; p.38). Statutory auditor is now MSKA & Associates LLP, a BDO International member firm, and the quarter was reviewed by them (p.13). A new internal auditor, Doshi Jain & Co., was appointed. Promoters say they will take no salary from FY27 and be paid by dividend only (p.15). The company bought Purvi Gems for ₹9 Cr (p.13).

**Testing the cash flow claim.** The deck calls H1 operating cash flow of ₹136.7 Cr proof of the asset-light model. The balance sheet (p.11) shows how it was made, set against 31-Mar-26:
- Trade payables jumped from ₹92.2 Cr to ₹437.5 Cr (+₹345 Cr). MSME dues alone are ₹182.3 Cr.
- Receivables rose ₹562.6 Cr to ₹855.8 Cr (+₹293 Cr). Inventory rose ₹785.9 Cr to ₹1,002.5 Cr (+₹217 Cr).
- Without the payables rise, operating cash flow would have been about minus ₹209 Cr (my arithmetic, not filed).
- Cash generated from operations is ₹162.5 Cr against EBITDA of ₹346.5 Cr, 47% conversion. CFO against PAT is 58% (my arithmetic).

So the positive cash flow rests on suppliers financing the growth. That is allowed, but it is a different claim from "asset-light advance gold". Advance gold removes metal from the balance sheet; it does not explain why payables rose fivefold. The deck gives no payables ageing. For the MSME ₹182.3 Cr, the 45-day payment rule under the MSME Act (tax disallowance if not paid in time) is the item to ask about [INFERENCE].

**The unexplained line: "Other current financial assets" rose from ₹18.8 Cr to ₹382.3 Cr (p.11).** That is +₹363.5 Cr. In the same period "Bank balances other than cash" fell from ₹227.5 Cr to ₹26.6 Cr (−₹201 Cr), and borrowings rose from ₹847.9 Cr to ₹939.4 Cr (non-current plus current; +₹91.5 Cr). Liquid cash and bank fell from ₹235.4 Cr to ₹65.2 Cr. Net borrowings rose from about ₹612 Cr to about ₹874 Cr, about +₹262 Cr, even though CFO is positive (my arithmetic). The deck does not say what the ₹382 Cr is. It is not described anywhere in the deck. Possible readings [INFERENCE]: gold or metal deposits, margin money, or a receivable from the delayed land sale. I cannot tell from the filing.
- Bull reading: it is a gold-related, short-term, collateralised item that reverses.
- Bear reading: it is cash moved out of reporting bank balances into a loosely described asset, and it is the reason net debt rose while the deck headlines "positive cash flow".
- What separates them: the balance sheet note on this line, in the full results, or the 31-Oct call. If it is still above about ₹300 Cr at 31-Mar-27 with no named counterparty, the bear reading gains weight.
- Also unknown: which section of the cash flow carries it. Investing outflow is ₹127.5 Cr (p.12), which does not leave room for ₹363 Cr unless the bank balance release is netted inside it.

**Other items to test.**
- Held-for-sale assets are unchanged at ₹110.1 Cr. The scorecard (p.21) says the land sale is delayed beyond Aug 2026 and moved from a promoter purchase to an external sale, "to minimise related party transactions". No buyer, price, or new date is given.
- Exceptional expense of ₹7.4 Cr in Q2 has no description. "Operational PAT" (₹134.9 Cr, 5.6% margin) is built by adding it back. The definition says non-operational income is also removed, but the arithmetic only adds back the ₹7.4 Cr: 166.0 + 7.4 = 173.4 for operational PBT (p.9). Other income of ₹14.4 Cr stays in. The scorecard (p.21) compares the 5.6% "operational" margin with the 4.5 to 4.75% guidance. Reported margin is 5.4%, still above guidance. The asterisk is there. This is a small point.
- Interest cost is ₹25.5 Cr in Q2, covered 7.8 times by EBIT. That is about 11% on average borrowings annualised (my arithmetic).
- Employee cost is ₹20.2 Cr, up 54% YoY, even though promoters stopped salaries from FY27. The deck does not say what promoter pay was.
- Tax rate is about 22% (₹36.7 Cr on ₹166.0 Cr).
- p.18 still says "currently generates a PAT margin of ~4.5%", which is the FY26 figure. Reported H1 is 5.3%. Stale line.
- p.17 has no new FY27 or FY28 guidance. It only restates the old FY27 target (₹5,000 Cr revenue, 3.5% PAT margin), now passed by H1 revenue of ₹4,403 Cr. No volume (kg) figure is given, so I cannot split the 61% revenue growth into gold price and tonnage.

**Guidance versus delivery (p.21).** FY24 ₹1,700 Cr guided, ₹1,745.5 Cr actual. FY25 ₹3,300 Cr guided, ₹3,548 Cr. FY26 about ₹6,100 Cr, ₹6,294.9 Cr. Revenue guidance has been beaten each year. The misses are in the plan changes: gold metal loan conversion by Dec 2025 not achieved (GML nil at March 2026), and the land sale slipped. The record on revenue is clean. The record on cash and related-party items is where the slippage sits.

**Valuation context.** ₹858 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:39 IST); 52-week range ₹285 to ₹1,084; market cap ₹13,293 Cr. Trailing PAT is FY26 ₹281.8 Cr less H1 FY26 ₹110.6 Cr plus H1 FY27 ₹234.3 Cr, about ₹405.5 Cr, so about 33x (my arithmetic, reported PAT including minority). The deck went out at 13:12 on 28 Oct, so the 28-Oct close already includes part of the reaction. The stock is 21% below its high and about 3x its low.

**Cross-links.** Vaibhav Global is on today's list and is also gold jewellery, but it is a retail and e-commerce model, so read-across is on gold price and demand only. I have not checked its master status. The same Q2 gold price move flows through any jewellery name on file.

**Next dated item.** Analyst call on Saturday, 31-Oct-2026, 11:00 IST (item 178). MD Mangesh Chauhan, CFO Siddharth Sipani and CS Nikita Jain are listed. Three questions to put: what is the ₹382 Cr line, what is the payables ageing, and what is the land sale status.

### Refex Industries: headline PAT up 13%, but sequential fall and cash drain

Refex Industries is not in COMPANIES MASTER (notion_page is null), so there is no thesis to test. This is an explanation of four filings: the press release, the investor presentation, the unaudited results with limited review reports, and the board outcome. Items 145 and 146 are the same 16-page PDF. I checked the page images and they are byte-identical, so the results were read once.

**What the headline says [FILED, Refex_..._Pr_2034ab.txt p.2; results PDF p.4]**
- Standalone, continuing operations, Q2 FY27: revenue ₹559.5 Cr (+36.1% YoY), EBITDA ₹90.5 Cr (+21.7%), PAT ₹59.5 Cr (+13.1%). H1: revenue ₹1,178.8 Cr (+54.7%), EBITDA ₹195.9 Cr (+71.8%), PAT ₹133.2 Cr (+55.4%).
- Margins fell year on year. EBITDA margin 16.2% against 18.1%. Gross margin 19.2% against 21.3% (presentation p.7).
- The release title says "Maintains Growth Momentum". The filed quarterly columns say something different, below.

**The quarter against the previous quarter (my arithmetic from the Jun-26 column, results p.4, not filed as such)**
- Revenue: Q1 ₹619.3 Cr, Q2 ₹559.5 Cr. Down about 9.7% quarter on quarter.
- PAT: Q1 ₹73.6 Cr, Q2 ₹59.5 Cr. Down about 19%.
- EBITDA: H1 ₹195.9 Cr less Q2 ₹90.5 Cr gives Q1 about ₹105.4 Cr, a 17.0% margin. Q2 is 16.2%.
- Year-on-year growth slowed hard. Q1 FY26 revenue was ₹762.2 Cr less ₹411.1 Cr, which is ₹351.1 Cr. So Q1 FY27 was up about 76%, and Q2 is up 36%. PAT growth went from about +122% in Q1 to +13% in Q2. The "+55.4% H1" headline is mostly a Q1 number.
- Ash and Coal segment revenue fell from ₹610.5 Cr to ₹546.6 Cr. Its segment EBIT margin fell from about 18.3% to 16.7% (results p.4 segment table).
- Management blames monsoon. That is plausible for ash evacuation and trucking, and it is a [MGMT] claim. The filing gives no volume data to check it.

**The headline mixes two bases (important to read correctly)**
- The standalone segment table shows Ash and Coal at ₹546.6 Cr plus "all other segments" at ₹12.9 Cr. So the ₹559.5 Cr headline is almost entirely the ash business.
- The Venwind wind revenue (₹201 Cr in Q2, ₹499 Cr in H1, per the release) sits in the consolidated segment table, not in the standalone headline (results p.12). The press release puts both side by side in one list.
- Consolidated continuing revenue is ₹761.3 Cr in Q2 against ₹916.3 Cr in Q1 (down about 17%) and ₹414.7 Cr a year ago (up about 84%). Consolidated continuing PAT is ₹59.6 Cr in Q2 against ₹74.5 Cr in Q1 (results p.11).

**Venwind (wind turbines, subsidiary) [FILED, results p.12; presentation p.32]**
- Wind segment revenue: Q1 ₹297.1 Cr, Q2 ₹201.8 Cr (down about 32% quarter on quarter, my arithmetic).
- Wind segment EBIT: Q2 ₹9.0 Cr, a margin of about 4.5%. Q1 was a loss of ₹0.3 Cr. H1 EBIT ₹8.7 Cr on ₹498.9 Cr revenue, about 1.7%. This is a thin-margin pass-through business so far.
- Confirmed order book ₹1,124 Cr, more than 400 MW secured, pipeline 1.5 GW (presentation p.32). First deliveries to all three customers are done.
- Management targets about 5 GW of annual capacity "over the next five years" (presentation p.37). Against a secured 400 MW and a first-year delivery pace of about ₹500 Cr in six months, that target has no filed bridge.
- The presentation does not give Venwind's order book at 30-Jun, so I cannot say if it grew or shrank in Q2. NOT FOUND.

**Ash and Coal order book [FILED, presentation p.5, p.18]**
- New orders ₹346 Cr in Q2. Order book ₹1,721 Cr at 30-Sep-26.
- Book to bill for Q2 is about 0.63x (₹346 Cr over ₹546.6 Cr segment revenue, my arithmetic). If all revenue burns the book, the opening book was about ₹1,922 Cr, so the book shrank by roughly ₹200 Cr in the quarter. This is inference. The prior book is not in these filings. NOT FOUND.
- The release calls it a "record order book" (presentation p.6). The Q2 figure alone does not show a record.

**Ready-mix concrete (RMC) entry [FILED, presentation p.23 to 24]**
- First plant at Chahroli, Pune started 25-Sep-26. Hoskote (Karnataka) and Kalyan (Maharashtra) are described as operational. A phased rollout is listed for Mumbai, Bengaluru, Hyderabad, Kolkata, Ahmedabad, Nagpur, Chandigarh region and Chennai.
- Model is lease-based and asset-light. No capex, no revenue target, no margin target and no capacity per plant is given. Silence on all four.
- The logic is fly ash replacing part of cement. It is a sensible forward link. It is also a new, local, low-margin and competitive business for a company whose cash cycle is already stretched (see below).

**Cash and working capital: the part the headline skips [FILED, standalone cash flow p.7 to 8, balance sheet p.6]**
- H1 cash from operations ₹24.1 Cr, against ₹64.2 Cr a year ago. That is about 0.18x of H1 PAT and 0.12x of H1 EBITDA (my arithmetic).
- Working capital absorbed ₹165.4 Cr (presentation p.8). Trade receivables rose ₹168.6 Cr to ₹905.9 Cr. Contract assets rose ₹99.3 Cr to ₹833.4 Cr.
- Receivables plus contract assets: ₹1,739 Cr at 30-Sep against ₹1,471 Cr at 31-Mar, up ₹268 Cr in six months. That is about twice H1 PAT. On H1 revenue that is roughly 267 days (my arithmetic, a rough measure).
- It was funded by suppliers and bank debt. Trade payables up ₹63.5 Cr to ₹397.7 Cr. Short-term borrowings up ₹64.5 Cr to ₹197.3 Cr. Finance cost ₹23.8 Cr in H1 against ₹12.0 Cr, so it doubled.
- Current tax liabilities rose from ₹69.7 Cr to ₹99.4 Cr. H1 tax expense is ₹44.7 Cr and tax paid was ₹15.0 Cr. Tax is being accrued faster than paid.
- Standalone cash flow also shows ₹28.5 Cr lent to subsidiaries and ₹7.9 Cr invested in subsidiaries in H1. The parent is funding the group.
- Consolidated is better than standalone but moves the same way. Receivables ₹1,250.9 Cr against ₹886.3 Cr (up about 41%). Inventories ₹113.4 Cr against ₹76.1 Cr. Continuing cash from operations ₹84.6 Cr against continuing PBT ₹181.2 Cr (about 0.47x) (results p.14 to 15).

**"Net debt free" claim (presentation p.37) [FILED, consolidated balance sheet p.14, my arithmetic]**
- Consolidated borrowings ₹29.2 Cr long term plus ₹197.6 Cr short term, about ₹226.9 Cr. Cash and bank balances ₹322.4 Cr plus ₹153.3 Cr, about ₹475.7 Cr. Net cash about ₹249 Cr before leases (₹64 Cr). The claim holds today.
- The direction is the point. Gross debt is up. Short-term borrowing is up about 48% in six months. If the receivable build continues, net cash goes first.

**Mobility (RGML) and the demerger [FILED, results p.5, 13; presentation p.34 to 35]**
- Mobility is shown as discontinued operations, so its losses are outside the ₹60 Cr headline. Q2 segment revenue ₹36.1 Cr, EBIT loss ₹12.2 Cr. H1 loss ₹26.2 Cr. Loss after tax: Q2 ₹8.9 Cr, H1 ₹18.8 Cr.
- Consolidated profit after all operations: Q2 ₹50.7 Cr, owners' share ₹50.1 Cr. H1 owners' share ₹113.9 Cr. Basic EPS for Q2 is ₹4.34 continuing and minus ₹0.65 discontinued, so about ₹3.69 in total.
- The Q2 loss narrowed about 10% from Q1 (₹13.6 Cr loss). Management says the mobility business is "progressing steadily towards profitability". Filed numbers show a ₹12 Cr quarterly loss on ₹36 Cr revenue. That is a long way from profit.
- Demerger structure: RGML merges into Refex Industries, then the mobility undertaking is carved out into Refex Mobility Ltd, with shares issued 1:1 to Refex shareholders and then listed. Shareholders and creditors approved it on 5-Aug-26. The second motion was filed on 14-Aug-26. The next NCLT hearing was 28-Oct-26, the same day as these filings.
- The outcome of the 28-Oct hearing is not in any filing. The presentation says completion "in next few months". Open item.
- The auditor's Other Matter paragraph flags the pending NCLT approval and the held-for-sale classification. The conclusion is unmodified. Standard, but it is the auditor pointing at it.
- Held-for-sale assets ₹227.1 Cr and liabilities ₹132.0 Cr on the consolidated balance sheet. Net about ₹95 Cr (my arithmetic).

**Two readings**
- Bull: Q2 is a monsoon dip. Ash book ₹1,721 Cr plus Venwind book ₹1,124 Cr gives about ₹2,845 Cr of visible work. Venwind has crossed from losses to a small profit. RMC gives ash a use. Demerger could separate a loss-making business. Promoter holding rose from 55.85% to 56.6% over six months (presentation p.50). Net cash is still positive.
- Bear: Revenue, EBITDA margin and PAT all fell quarter on quarter. Book to bill in ash is below 1. Cash conversion is 0.18x. Receivables plus contract assets are about 267 days of H1 revenue. Finance cost has doubled. Wind margin is about 4.5% at best. Mobility burns about ₹12 Cr a quarter. FII holding fell from 1.71% (Sep-25) to 0.6% (Sep-26).
- The observation that separates them is the Dec-26 quarter. Bull needs standalone revenue back above about ₹620 Cr (the Q1 level) and H2 operating cash flow above H2 PAT, so that receivables plus contract assets stop growing past about ₹1,740 Cr. Bear is confirmed if they rise again. These thresholds are mine, not filed.

**Inconsistencies and oddities**
- Release headline says "growth momentum". Filed quarterly columns show a sequential fall in revenue and PAT.
- The release lists Venwind revenue under a "standalone" heading, but it is consolidated only.
- Consolidated cash flow comparator: depreciation for Sep-25 is ₹28.9 Cr, but the Sep-26 figure is ₹12.2 Cr and the P&L for Sep-25 shows ₹9.6 Cr continuing. The comparator seems to include discontinued operations and the current column does not. Not like for like.
- The presentation CMP of ₹232.2 on 27-Oct gives market cap ₹3,186 Cr. This run's price is lower (below).
- Item 144 in the Screener list calls it "PAT ₹60 crore". The filed figure is ₹59.5 Cr. Rounding only.

**Price context** ₹223 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:39 IST). 52-week range ₹188 to ₹382. Market cap ₹3,055 Cr. About 42% below the 52-week high and about 19% above the low. Market cap is about 11.5x H1 PAT annualised (₹266 Cr, my arithmetic). The market is not paying for the headline growth.

**Cross-links** Not in COMPANIES MASTER. Two loose links to names on file, both inference only. Venwind uses permanent magnet generator (PMG) drivetrains (presentation p.29), so check whether Permanent Magnets supplies that chain. Ash handling serves thermal power and cement, and the same cement names (ACC, Ambuja) appear as Pranik Logistics customers in this batch.

**Who it matters to** Anyone tracking small-cap thermal-services, wind OEM entry or the Refex Mobility listing. The things to read next are the NCLT order of 28-Oct, the Q3 receivable build, and whether Venwind margin lifts above 5%.

### Raymond Lifestyl: 14% sales growth, but profit is falling and cash is flattering

Raymond Lifestyle is not in COMPANIES MASTER, so there is no thesis, gate or price line to test. This review explains the filing and says what is unusual in it. Raymond Lifestyle (formerly Raymond Consumer Care, demerged from Raymond Ltd) filed Q2 and H1 FY27 results on 28-Oct-2026 after the close. The board met 3:15 to 5:50 pm and the filing was signed at 19:05 (Investor presentation, p.1). Three of the four PDFs (items 137, 139, 140) are the same 25-page bundle: auditor reports, results, balance sheet, cash flow, notes and the press release. Item 138 is the investor presentation. The Screener summary for item 139 says "FY26"; the numbers it quotes (₹3,590.61 Cr revenue, ₹44.94 Cr PAT) are H1 FY27. That is a Screener label error, not the company's.

**Price context.** ₹499 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:39 IST), 52-week range ₹492 to ₹1,267, market cap ₹3,039 Cr. The read is before the open, so this is the 28-Oct close and carries no reaction to the results. The stock sits 1.4% above its 52-week low and 61% below the high. I have no entry or stop lines for it.

#### What the filing says [FILED]

| Consolidated, ₹ Cr | Q2 FY27 | Q2 FY26 | YoY | H1 FY27 | H1 FY26 | YoY |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue from operations | 2,075 | 1,832 | +13.2% | 3,591 | 3,263 | +10.0% |
| Other income | 49.5 | 33.0 | +49.7% | 94.2 | 77.6 | +21.5% |
| EBITDA (company, includes other income) | 270 | 259 | +4% | 404 | 381 | +6% |
| Depreciation | 104.9 | 91.4 | +14.8% | 214.4 | 180.3 | +18.9% |
| Finance cost | 73.7 | 60.0 | +22.9% | 137.2 | 117.4 | +16.8% |
| PBT before exceptional | 90.9 | 107.6 | -15.5% | 52.6 | 82.8 | -36.5% |
| PAT | 67.5 | 75.2 | -10.2% | 44.9 | 55.4 | -18.8% |
| EPS (₹) | 11.08 | 12.34 | | 7.38 | 9.09 | |

Source: results PDF pp.5-6, 28-Oct-2026. Q1 FY27 was a loss of ₹22.6 Cr (p.5), so H1 profit rests on Q2.

Segments (presentation pp.15-17, EBITDA, ₹ Cr, Q2 FY27 vs Q2 FY26):
- Branded Textile: revenue 971 vs 906 (+7%), EBITDA 188 vs 186, margin 19.3% vs 20.6%.
- Branded Apparel: revenue 559 vs 465 (+20%), EBITDA 49 vs 41, margin flat at 8.8%.
- Garmenting: revenue 339 vs 269 (+26%), EBITDA 25 vs 14, margin 7.4% vs 5.3%. H1 EBITDA 47 vs 6.
- High Value Cotton Shirting: revenue 222 vs 212 (+5%), EBITDA 21 vs 25, margin 9.5% vs 12.0%.
- Emerging Business: revenue 100 vs 87 (+15%), EBITDA minus 23 vs minus 9. H1 loss 42 vs 22.

#### The EBITDA growth is other income, not operations

The press release headline is "strong execution", EBITDA +4%. Strip out other income and the picture changes (my arithmetic, not filed; EBITDA built as PBT plus finance cost plus depreciation, from pp.5-6):
- Q2 FY27 EBITDA excluding other income is ₹220 Cr against ₹226 Cr a year ago, down 2.6%. Margin on revenue is 10.6% against 12.3%, down about 170 bps.
- H1 FY27 on the same basis is ₹310 Cr against ₹303 Cr, up 2.3%, with margin 8.6% against 9.3%.
- Q2 PBT excluding other income is ₹41.5 Cr against ₹74.6 Cr, down 44%. H1 PBT excluding other income is a loss of about ₹41.6 Cr.
- The five segment EBITDAs add to ₹260 Cr against ₹257 Cr, a gain of ₹3 Cr on ₹243 Cr of extra revenue. Of the ₹11 Cr rise in reported EBITDA, about ₹16 Cr is higher other income.

Other income in H1 includes interest income of ₹61.7 Cr and realised investment gains of ₹22.4 Cr, less a fair value loss of ₹9.8 Cr (cash flow, p.10). The cash flow also shows a "reversal on share based payments" of ₹11.2 Cr in H1 against ₹0.9 Cr a year ago. That reversal is a non-cash credit to profit; the filing does not say which quarter or why. [INFERENCE] it is an ESOP cost reversal, probably forfeiture.

Where the margin went (my arithmetic from p.5):
- Gross margin (revenue less materials, stock-in-trade purchases and inventory change) fell to 51.1% from 54.0% in Q2, down about 300 bps. It was 54.0% in Q1 FY27, so the drop is sequential too.
- Purchases of stock-in-trade rose 34% (₹794 Cr vs ₹592 Cr) against revenue up 13%. More of the sales are bought-in or lower-margin, which fits the garmenting and wholesale-channel push.
- Other expenses (selling, marketing, rent) rose 20.7% (₹418 Cr vs ₹346 Cr) against revenue +13.2%. The company calls this "increased marketing investments" (press release, p.23).
- Manufacturing and operating cost fell 5.4% (₹181 Cr vs ₹192 Cr) while sales rose 13%. That cushions margin. The filing does not explain a fall at over 90% capacity utilisation (presentation, p.6). It may be a regrouping.
- Depreciation up 15% and finance cost up 23% turned ₹11 Cr of EBITDA growth into a ₹17 Cr fall in PBT.

#### Branded Apparel grew in the wrong channels for margin

Apparel revenue is up 20% but EBITDA margin is flat at 8.8% and H1 EBITDA is unchanged at ₹67 Cr. Store data (presentation p.18): exclusive brand outlets (EBO) are 450 against 503 a year ago, a net fall of 53. TRS (Raymond Shop franchise) stores rose to 1,134 from 1,110. Total stores are 1,634 against 1,663 (net minus 29; 53 exits, 24 openings). So the apparel growth came from large format stores, trade and e-commerce (p.15), while own brand stores shrank. The company itself cites "adverse channel mix". Ethnix lost a net 27 stores (p.17).

#### Cash flow and net cash: the headline does not reconcile on a plain read

The company says net cash is ₹69 Cr against net debt of ₹246 Cr a year ago, "cash generation of ₹300+ Cr" (press release, p.23). The filed numbers need care (all my arithmetic from pp.8-10, ₹ Cr):
- Gross borrowings rose to ₹1,882 from ₹1,200 at March (up ₹682 Cr, +57%), and from ₹1,333 at June (up ₹549 Cr in the quarter). Year on year they are up 27.5% from ₹1,476.
- Cash, other bank balances and current investments are ₹1,334. Net debt on those alone is about ₹548 Cr.
- Adding ₹530 Cr of non-current investments gives about ₹18 Cr net debt. Adding the new ₹75.7 Cr long-term loan (see below) gives about ₹58 Cr net cash. The company's ₹69 Cr is reachable only if non-current investments and that loan are counted as cash-like. The filing does not state the definition.
- On the same broad basis, March net cash was about ₹165 Cr. It fell by about ₹107 Cr in H1. The year-on-year improvement was banked in H2 FY26, not this half.
- Operating cash flow is minus ₹369 Cr in H1 against minus ₹115 Cr (consolidated, p.10). Standalone is minus ₹360 Cr against minus ₹209 Cr.

The odd part is inside that outflow. The "trade and other receivables" line absorbs ₹809 Cr (₹394 Cr last year). Trade receivables rose only ₹157 Cr; other current and financial assets rose about ₹105 Cr more. Inventories (₹281 Cr) and payables (+₹365 Cr) tie to the balance sheet; receivables do not, by about ₹546 Cr. Meanwhile "bank balances other than cash" rose ₹446 Cr (₹92 Cr to ₹537 Cr) while investing flows show only ₹1.5 Cr of net deposit redemption. Standalone shows the same pattern: a ₹468 Cr gap against a ₹444 Cr deposit build.

- [INFERENCE] the deposit build is being run through operating cash flow. If so, underlying operating cash flow is about plus ₹77 Cr, not minus ₹369 Cr. It also means the company borrowed about ₹670 Cr net (financing flows, p.10) and parked about ₹446 Cr in bank deposits. In H1 FY26 the same deposits were shown in investing, so the presentation may have changed.
- Benign reading: deposits are margin or lien against LC and bank guarantee lines, and borrowing is seasonal stocking before festive and wedding sales.
- What decides it: the cash flow note on classification, and the deposit lien details in the annual report. Neither is in these filings.

Working capital days: 82 against 105 a year ago (presentation, p.11), but 77 at March (p.6). So the year-on-year gain is real and it was made by March. H1 saw a 5 day rebuild. Payables rose to ₹1,787 Cr from ₹1,437 Cr (+24%), which funds a lot of the stock build.

#### Other points inside the filing

- **Bad debts.** Standalone "bad debts to accounts receivable" is 6.29% for Q2 and H1, against 0.00% in Q1 and 0.04% a year ago (p.16). On average standalone trade receivables of about ₹831 Cr that is roughly ₹52 Cr of gross bad debts (my arithmetic, formula basis assumed). FY26 carried a ₹70 Cr exceptional charge for receivable allowance and Apparel inventory write-down (note 2). [INFERENCE] Q2 writes off against that allowance, so P&L neutral. No note says so. Net loss allowance charged in H1 is only ₹7 Cr (cash flow, p.20).
- **New long-term loan.** Standalone "long-term loans given by the Company" is ₹75.65 Cr in H1 (cash flow, p.20), plus ₹100 Cr invested in non-current investments. A ₹50 Cr short-term loan on the March balance sheet was repaid (₹50.1 Cr incl. interest). The consolidated balance sheet also shows the ₹75.65 Cr, so the borrower is not a consolidated subsidiary. Borrower, rate and security are not disclosed. The half-year related-party disclosure under Regulation 23 will say whether it is a group entity.
- **Debt service.** Standalone DSCR is 0.88x in Q2 (1.05x a year ago, 0.43x in Q1), interest cover 3.40x. The disclosed covenant is the NCD asset cover: 2.84x against a 2.00x floor on the 9.00% Series P debentures (p.17), down from 2.97x in March. No breach.
- **Where the profit sits.** Standalone H1 PAT is ₹13.7 Cr (EPS ₹2.26) against ₹63.5 Cr. Four subsidiaries reviewed by other auditors made ₹35.8 Cr of H1 profit, about 80% of the consolidated ₹44.9 Cr (auditor report, p.3). These are largely the garmenting units. Standalone H1 PBT is ₹19.3 Cr on other income of ₹101.6 Cr, so the standalone business, excluding other income, lost about ₹82 Cr before tax in H1.
- **Audit.** Walker Chandiok, unmodified. Two small subsidiaries (nil revenue, assets ₹8.5 Cr) were not reviewed by their own auditors; the auditor calls them not material.
- **Balance sheet quality.** Goodwill and intangibles are ₹5,275 Cr against equity of ₹9,652 Cr, and deferred tax assets are ₹1,131 Cr. Market cap ₹3,039 Cr is 0.31 times book, but book is more than half intangible. TTM EPS is about ₹5.88 (FY26 ₹7.59, less H1 FY26 ₹9.09, plus H1 FY27 ₹7.38), a P/E near 85. On TTM EBITDA of ₹827 Cr and the company's net cash, EV including ₹1,052 Cr of lease liabilities is about ₹4,020 Cr, about 4.9x (6.0x excluding other income). All my arithmetic.
- **Sloppiness.** The Lifestyle press release disclaimer names "Raymond Realty Limited" (p.25). Copied from the sister company's template. Minor.

#### Guidance test

FY27 target on the presentation: "double digit top line and higher double digit EBITDA growth" (p.6). FY26 EBITDA was ₹804 Cr (my build of FY26 PBT before exceptional ₹200 Cr plus depreciation ₹371 Cr plus finance cost ₹233 Cr agrees).
- Top line: H1 total income +10%, revenue +10.0%. Needs H2 revenue growth of about 10% (₹3,986 Cr against ₹3,625 Cr). Q2 ran +13%, so achievable.
- EBITDA: H1 is ₹404 Cr, +6%. "Higher double digit" is not defined. At +15% FY27 needs ₹925 Cr, so H2 of ₹521 Cr against ₹423 Cr, up 23%. At +20% H2 must be ₹561 Cr, up 33%. With gross margin down 300 bps and other income already 23% of H1 EBITDA, that is a stretch. Management says "on track" (p.19). I treat that as [MGMT], untested.
- Separating observation: Q3 EBITDA excluding other income. Below about 10.6% of revenue, the Q2 level, would say the margin squeeze is not seasonal. The Q3 result is due by 14-Feb-2027 (45 days after the quarter end).

#### Macro claims made by the company [MGMT]

Presentation p.4 says Brent is above $105 to $110, the rupee is near 94 to 95 per dollar, and wool and cotton are at multi-year highs. I did not verify these. If they hold, they are a cost headwind for every input-heavy name and a tailwind for exporters; garmenting is the one segment growing on them (US-India tariff rationalisation and UK FTA, p.16).

#### Cross-links to companies on file
- Raymond Realty (on file, T05): same group, same auditor firm. Its co-auditor Chaturvedi & Shah resigned on the same day. Raymond Lifestyle has a sole signing partner at Walker Chandiok, so no parallel change here. The unnamed borrower of the ₹75.65 Cr loan may or may not be a group company; check the related-party disclosure before linking the two.
- Arvind Fashions and Credo Brands (both on file): the best domestic apparel comparison. Test their LFS, wholesale and e-commerce mix against Raymond's finding that apparel growth is coming from low-margin channels while own stores shrink.
- Any exporter on file with US or UK garment or textile exposure benefits from the same tariff and FTA read-through. Garmenting H1 EBITDA of ₹47 Cr against ₹6 Cr is the datapoint.

#### Unknowns
Resolved: Q2 and H1 numbers, segment split, store counts, covenant status. Open: net cash definition, cash flow classification of the ₹446 Cr deposit rise, borrower of the ₹75.65 Cr loan, the ₹52 Cr gross bad debt write-off, Q3 margins.

### Zota Health Care: EBITDA flat on 55% growth, and a ₹70 Cr half-year loss

Zota Health Care is not in COMPANIES MASTER. It makes generic and branded medicines and runs Davaindia, a chain of generic-medicine stores (company-owned "COCO" and franchise "FOFO"). Three items today. The three PDFs failed to download (HTTP 403 in INDEX.md). I read the company's press release from the NSE archive instead (nsearchives.nseindia.com, ZOTA_28102026181545_Pressrelease28102026.pdf) [FILED, same filing as item 214]. I have not read the investor presentation (item 213) or the results statement (item 215). Figures from screener.in below are third-party and marked as such.

**What the press release says (all [FILED] unless marked)**
- Q2 FY27 consolidated revenue ₹199.3 Cr, up 54.58% on ₹128.9 Cr, and up 14.8% on Q1's ₹173.6 Cr (my division). My check of the percentages holds.
- Davaindia sales ₹168.5 Cr, up 76.25%. Everyday Herbal (a subsidiary) ₹8.1 Cr, up 79%. Exports fell from ₹10.9 Cr to ₹4.7 Cr, down 57%.
- EBITDA ₹7.92 Cr, margin 3.97%. A year ago it was ₹7.96 Cr, margin 6.17%. So EBITDA is flat year on year on 55% more revenue. The release stresses it is "positive": Q1 was minus ₹9.55 Cr and H1 is still minus ₹1.64 Cr.
- Gross margin 60.17% (Q2 FY26 59.33%; Q1 FY27 61.96%). Gross profit rose ₹43.4 Cr year on year, but EBITDA did not move. So operating costs rose about 63% (₹68.5 Cr to ₹112.0 Cr, my arithmetic) against revenue up 55%. There is no operating leverage yet. Sequentially, gross profit rose ₹12.4 Cr and EBITDA improved ₹17.5 Cr, so costs fell about ₹5.1 Cr against Q1, on 15% more revenue. The press release does not explain what was in Q1's costs.
- 2,840 stores against 2,055: 1,859 COCO (1,207 a year ago) and 981 FOFO (848). Added 20, closed 5 (all FOFO) in the quarter. Store GMV ₹187.9 Cr against ₹106.0 Cr. GMV per store per quarter (my arithmetic, closing store count): COCO ₹7.4 lakh against ₹5.6 lakh, FOFO ₹5.1 lakh against ₹4.6 lakh. Store productivity improved, more for company-owned stores. Average wallet spend per visit: COCO ₹283 against ₹229, FOFO ₹286 against ₹282.
- Management says it "consciously moderated" store additions in Q2 and will accelerate in H2 [MGMT]. No number is given for the FY27 store plan.
- The company converted ₹119.52 Cr of unsecured loan and accrued interest owed by its wholly owned subsidiary Davaindia Health Mart Ltd (DHML) into equity: 2,35,512 shares at ₹5,075 each (2,35,512 x 5,075 = ₹119.5 Cr, checks). "No fresh cash outflow" [FILED]. This shows the subsidiary that runs the stores has absorbed ₹119.5 Cr of parent money that will not come back as cash. Interest on that loan would have sat in Zota's standalone profit and will now stop [INFERENCE].
- New in Q2: an inter-segment elimination of ₹6.71 Cr (Everyday Herbal sales to Davaindia). Q1 and Q2 FY26 show none. Either the flow began in Q2, or Q1 revenue was not eliminated. Q2 is 83% of Everyday Herbal's own sales, so a similar flow in Q1 would be about ₹5 Cr. Worth one question.

**The loss: reconciling the summary**
The screener summary for item 215 says "standalone profit ₹301.96 lakh, consolidated loss ₹6,990.49 lakh" (₹69.9 Cr). That looked inconsistent with EBITDA of ₹7.9 Cr.
