Screener's consolidated quarterly table (third-party, read today) shows Q2 FY27 net loss about ₹26 Cr and Q1 FY27 about ₹44 Cr, with depreciation ₹29 Cr and interest ₹6 Cr a quarter in Q2 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026). 44 + 26 = 70, so the ₹69.9 Cr figure is probably the H1 consolidated loss, not Q2 [INFERENCE; the results PDF would settle it]. Using screener figures, Q2 is EBITDA ₹7.9 Cr, less depreciation ₹29 Cr and interest ₹6 Cr, plus other income, equals about minus ₹27 Cr before tax.
- So the press release, titled "Healthy Q2FY27 Performance", never prints a profit or loss line. Silence is information: the company reports PAT in the results statement, but the release leaves it out.
- Screener's operating profit for Q2 is ₹3 Cr and other income ₹5 Cr. The company's ₹7.9 Cr EBITDA therefore appears to include about ₹5 Cr of other income [INFERENCE]. Without it, operating EBITDA is near ₹3 Cr, about 1.5% of revenue.
- Depreciation of about 14.5% of revenue is mostly store leases and fit-outs on the COCO network (Ind AS 116) [INFERENCE]. The company's EBITDA looks better than it is because lease costs sit below that line.
- The standalone profit (₹3.0 Cr, quarter or half not stated in the summary) versus a consolidated loss tells the same story: the parent makes its money selling to the retail subsidiary and the subsidiary loses it.

**Two readings**

Bull: Davaindia sales +76% and GMV +77% on a store base up 38%. Same-store productivity is rising. Sequential EBITDA turned from minus ₹9.6 Cr to plus ₹7.9 Cr. A slower Q2 expansion lets stores mature, and management says H2 will resume adds. The loan-to-equity conversion strengthens the subsidiary's balance sheet.

Bear: EBITDA did not grow in a year in which revenue rose 55%. Depreciation and interest (about ₹35 Cr a quarter) are several times EBITDA, so the group is losing about ₹26 Cr a quarter after them (screener). Borrowings are ₹271 Cr (screener). Export revenue is down 57%. The parent has put ₹119.5 Cr into the subsidiary. The price ₹1,271 gives a ₹4,402 Cr market cap on a trailing loss of about ₹114 Cr and revenue of about ₹679 Cr (screener, 29-Oct-2026), about 6.5 times sales.

The observation that separates them: Q3 FY27 consolidated EBITDA excluding other income above about ₹15 Cr, and Q3 net loss shrinking below about ₹15 Cr, (my thresholds) and a stated H2 store-addition number. If store additions resume and EBITDA ex other income stays near ₹3 Cr, bear wins.

**What the full filings would add:** the results statement (PAT, tax, exceptional items, finance cost, lease depreciation, segment results), the investor presentation (store-level EBITDA, COCO versus FOFO economics, cash flow, net debt), and the valuation basis for the ₹5,075 DHML share price.

**Price context:** ₹1,271 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:40 IST), 52-week range ₹1,022 to ₹1,740, market cap ₹4,402 Cr. Read before the market reacted to results filed on the evening of 28-Oct.

**Cross-links on file:** same theme, no direct link in the filings: Entero Healthcar (healthcare distribution and pharmacy), Akums Drugs and Aarti Drugs (generic manufacturing). Whether any of them supply Davaindia is not stated.

### PVR Inox: record quarter, but price and Hollywood did the lifting

PVR Inox is NOT in COMPANIES MASTER (notion_page is null). There is no thesis, entry line or gate to test. This is an explanation of the five filings, with the points that matter to anyone who owns or tracks it. Price: ₹1,445 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:38 IST); 52-week high ₹1,518, low ₹900; screener market cap ₹14,193 Cr. The results were filed at about 14:13 IST on 28-Oct, so this price only partly reflects them.

**What was filed (items 114 to 118).** The press release, the investor deck, the results with the auditor's limited-review report, the board outcome, and the analyst-call audio link. Items 117 and 118 are the same 13-page PDF (the board outcome letter with the results attached). Nothing is missing, it is only filed twice. I could not hear the call, so management's spoken guidance is NOT READ. The audio link in item 114 points to a host named "originserver-static1-uat" (a UAT, i.e. test, server). That is odd for a public disclosure and may not stay live.

**The headline numbers, Ind AS 116 adjusted (the company's preferred basis), consolidated, continuing operations.**
- Q2 FY27 revenue ₹21,327 mn, up 17.3% (PVR_Inox_Announcement_under_Regulation_30_LODR-In_b24ecc.pdf p.15 [FILED]).
- EBITDA ₹4,752 mn, up 44%, margin 22.3% against 18.1% (deck p.28).
- PAT ₹2,733 mn, up 111%. Reported (Ind AS) PAT is ₹2,631 mn, EPS ₹26.82 (results p.9).
- H1: revenue ₹37,750 mn (+15%), EBITDA ₹7,048 mn (+56%), PAT ₹3,438 mn (+233%) (deck p.29).
- Net cash ₹1,407 mn after a ₹3,000 mn buyback. Gross debt ₹5,009 mn against ₹7,586 mn at March (deck p.30). Lease liabilities are ₹58,079 mn and are outside this "net cash".
- Auditor (S.R. Batliboi): unmodified limited review. One subsidiary and the JV were not reviewed by their own auditors; the report calls them not material (revenue ₹142 mn in Q2).

**Where the growth came from.** Admissions rose only 4% (44.5 mn to 46.3 mn, deck p.8). Average ticket price rose 14% (₹262 to ₹298) and food spend per head rose 14% (₹134 to ₹153). Ticket revenue and F&B revenue each grew about 18%. Weighted average screens grew only 1.0% (deck p.15). So this was a price and mix quarter, not a volume quarter. Occupancy went from 28.7% to 30.4%, still below the pre-COVID 32% (deck p.17).

**The industry did the heavy lifting, and PVR leans on Hollywood.**
- Industry gross box office was ₹4,443 Cr, up 23%, the first ₹4,000 Cr quarter (press release p.3). English films rose 87% to ₹1,024 Cr. Hindi rose 12%, regional 12%.
- PVR's own share of the English gross is ₹548 Cr of ₹1,024 Cr, about 53% (deck p.11; my arithmetic, not filed). On single films: Spider-Man ₹275 Cr of ₹608 Cr (45%) and The Odyssey ₹142 Cr of ₹233 Cr (61%). On Hindi, Hanuman Ansh is ₹140 Cr of ₹351 Cr (40%) (deck p.12).
- PVR's ticket revenue grew 18% against the industry's 23% gross growth. The bases differ (gross against net of tax), so this is not a clean share-loss number. It is a reason not to read "record" as "gaining share". [INFERENCE]
- English heavy quarters depend on release timing. The next test is not far: the deck lists Ramayana (5-Nov), Hunger Games (20-Nov), Avengers: Doomsday (17-Dec), Dune: Part Three (18-Dec), King (24-Dec) and Jumanji (25-Dec) (deck pp.32 to 34). Q3 has real tentpoles. October releases are not shown at all.

**Quarter shape: Q2 is not the run-rate.** Q1 by subtraction (H1 less Q2; my arithmetic, not filed): revenue ₹16,423 mn, EBITDA ₹2,296 mn (14.0% margin), PAT ₹705 mn, admissions 36.6 mn. Q2 carried 67% of H1 EBITDA and 79% of H1 PAT. Anyone who annualises Q2 will overstate. Annualising H1 PAT (₹3,438 mn x 2 = ₹6,876 mn) on the screener market cap gives about 20.6x. Using the post-buyback share count (96.1 mn x ₹1,445 = about ₹13,886 Cr, because screener's ₹14,193 Cr still uses the old 98.2 mn shares) gives about 20.2x. These are crude and H1 is itself content-lucky.

**Three things inside the profit jump to read carefully.**
1. Depreciation policy change. Q2 depreciation is ₹211 mn lower because projector lives went from 10 to 15 years and laptop lives from 3 to 5 years, applied prospectively (deck p.28, footnote; I did not find it in the OCR text of the filed results notes, so it is disclosed in the deck [FILED, deck only]). At the Q2 tax rate (912 / 3,645 = 25%), that is about ₹158 mn of PAT, 5.8% of Q2 PAT, about ₹1.6 per share (my arithmetic). It runs about ₹211 mn every quarter from here. A 15-year life for projectors is a judgement call. EBITDA is untouched.
2. Pre-tax bridge, adjusted basis (my arithmetic): PBT rose ₹1,908 mn (₹1,737 mn to ₹3,645 mn). EBITDA gave ₹1,452 mn, lower depreciation ₹237 mn (of which ₹211 mn is the policy change), and lower finance cost ₹220 mn (₹410 mn to ₹190 mn, from debt repayment). So about 76% of the PBT gain is operating, 12% is the depreciation change, 12% is deleveraging.
3. Other income rose to ₹276 mn from ₹191 mn "primarily due to reversal of old provisions" (deck p.14). That is non-recurring and about ₹85 mn.

**Operating leverage is real.** Incremental revenue ₹3,140 mn produced incremental EBITDA ₹1,452 mn, a 46% drop-through (my arithmetic). Fixed costs ex distribution grew 8.6% against revenue 17.3% (deck p.15). Rent grew 11.6%, faster than the 7.1% fixed escalation, so variable rent is rising with box office. Advertising was weak: ₹1,310 mn, up 4.3% in Q2 and 1.3% in H1. Convenience fees rose 25% on 72% online share.

**Cash, tax and the buyback.**
- H1 free cash flow ₹6,035 mn on the company's definition: EBITDA 7,048 + working capital 734 - capex 1,323 - finance cost 424 (deck p.19). It reconciles to the filed cash flow within about 1.5%: operating cash ₹13,891 mn less capex ₹1,336 mn, lease repayments ₹6,227 mn and interest ₹385 mn is ₹5,943 mn (results p.13; my arithmetic).
- Cash tax is near zero. Q2 current tax was about ₹20 mn of ₹877 mn total tax (the rest is deferred), and the deferred tax asset fell from ₹5,184 mn to ₹4,151 mn (results p.12). At the H1 pace (₹1,037 mn deferred charge) that asset lasts about four more half-years. After that, cash taxes rise and free cash flow falls even if EBITDA does not. [INFERENCE]
- Payables rose about ₹1,209 mn since March (₹5,948 mn to ₹7,157 mn trade payables) after a blockbuster quarter. Some of the ₹734 mn working-capital inflow will reverse if film releases thin out.
- Buyback: 20,68,965 shares (2.11%) at ₹1,450, ₹3,000 mn, cash out ₹3,034 mn with costs (results p.3 and p.10, via OCR; the OCR also shows ₹1,430 and "10,68,965" in places, but 2,068,965 x ₹1,450 = ₹3,000 mn, so those are scan errors). Stock is at ₹1,445, level with the buyback price, 4.8% below the 52-week high.
- New Capital Allocation and Dividend Distribution Policy approved 28-Oct: net debt to LTM EBITDA within 0.5x after any distribution; about 30% of annual "surplus cash flow" via dividend and/or buyback at the Board's discretion (press release p.4; deck p.20). Surplus is defined after scheduled debt repayment. H1 surplus before the buyback was ₹6,035 - ₹2,586 = ₹3,449 mn; 30% is about ₹1,035 mn. The ₹3,000 mn buyback is already 87% of H1 surplus. If FY27 surplus is, say, double H1, 30% is ₹2,070 mn, below what was already paid. So the policy does not promise a second return this year. The wording is "statement of intent; any distribution remains at the Board's discretion" (deck p.20).

**ROCE claim, 16.5% TTM against about 13% pre-COVID (deck p.16, p.30).** TTM EBIT ₹7,769 mn over capital employed ₹46,948 mn. Capital employed is gross debt plus net worth after removing ₹46,338 mn of merger goodwill and Ind AS 116. With goodwill left in, the same EBIT over ₹93,286 mn (5,009 + 88,277) is about 8.3% (my arithmetic). Removing goodwill is a defensible choice, but 16.5% is a goodwill-free number, and TTM EBIT now includes the lower depreciation.

**Screen growth is back-ended.** Network 1,797 screens in 114 cities (deck p.25). H1 opened 24 and exited 25, so net -1 (my arithmetic; the press release says the exits were mostly loss-making). The FY27 target is 90 to 100 new screens, so H2 must open about 66 to 76, nearly three times H1. Most are asset-light or FOCO. The deck's pie chart on model mix (23 / 64 / 13) has an unclear legend order. SMART Cinema, the Tier III and IV format, opened its first site in Muzaffarpur (4 screens, 4-Sep); two to three more are planned this fiscal. Market sizing is "about 300 cities and 1,000 screens" (deck p.24). No capex budget or per-screen cost is disclosed.

**Small oddities.**
- Deck p.35 carries a stray editing note above the Love & War poster: "poster please update to the recently released Ranbir Kapoor poster". A draft instruction left in a filed deck.
- Discontinued operations (Zea Maize, divested 29-Jan-2026) are stripped from the comparatives, so the YoY growth is like for like. Q2 FY26 PAT of ₹1,296 mn is restated.
- The audit report on both sets is unmodified.

**Two readings.**
- Bull: margins of 22% at 30% occupancy against pre-COVID 17 to 18% at 32% is a structural shift from merger synergies and capital-light growth. Net cash, a payout policy, falling finance cost, strong December slate. Evidence: fixed-cost growth 8.6% against revenue 17%; debt down 34% in six months (₹7,586 mn to ₹5,009 mn); incremental drop-through 46%.
- Bear: admissions +4% while the industry's gross grew 23% means pricing and Hollywood carried the quarter. A quarter that is 67% of the half's EBITDA is content luck. Depreciation policy and provision reversals add about 8% to Q2 PAT combined (about ₹158 mn + ₹64 mn after tax at 25% on ₹85 mn; my arithmetic). Cash tax is flattered by a deferred tax asset that is being used up.
- The one observation that separates them: Q3 FY27 (Oct to Dec) admissions growth and EBITDA margin. If admissions stay at +4% or lower with ATP still +14% through a quarter that has Ramayana, Avengers, Dune and King, it is price-led. If admissions grow 8% or more, volume is back. Also watch whether the ~66 to 76 H2 openings are visible by the Q3 screen count.

**Cross-links.** No direct on-file company. The weak theme link is discretionary consumer and mall footfall, which Arvind Fashions and Credo Brands (both reporting this week and on file) also sit on. I draw no conclusion from that.

**Verdict.** Not in master, so no gate fires. The filing is positive on cash and balance sheet and neutral to cautious on quality of the profit jump. If you ever underwrite it, the first questions are admissions per screen, the depreciation change, and the cash-tax runway.

### Kotyark Industries: profit jumps on a thin top line, consolidated cash flow does not tie

Kotyark Industries is not in COMPANIES MASTER, so there is no thesis to test. This is an explanation of the filing for a name that is not on file. Items 88 and 89 are the same 15-page PDF posted twice (byte-identical), so they are read once. All figures are Rs lakh unless stated. The scan has a text layer with OCR errors, so I read the key tables from the page images (pp.5, 6, 7, 12, 13, 14).

**What the company is.** Biodiesel maker, one segment, India only (Notes, standalone p.8). Registered office is Chhani, Vadodara. The auditor's note says it supplies Oil Marketing Companies and runs a Swaroopganj unit in Rajasthan (Limited Review Report, p.4). The stock is a small cap: Rs 34.6 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:37 IST), 52-week range 17.4 to 46.0, market cap Rs 391 Cr. The price is about 25% below the 52-week high and about 99% above the low (my arithmetic, not filed).

**The numbers, standalone (p.6) [FILED]**
- Q2 revenue 5,741.77, against 8,491.85 in Q1 (down 32%) and 6,251.65 in Q2 last year (down 8%).
- Q2 PBT 495.44 against 259.11 a year ago (up 91%). Q2 PAT 370.65 against 271.38 (up 37%). Q1 PAT was only 105.99, so H1 PAT is 476.64 against 386.48 (up 23%).
- PBT margin on revenue: 8.6% in Q2 against 4.1% a year ago (my arithmetic, not filed).
- The margin gain is not a sales story. Depreciation fell to 248.51 from 353.48 in Q2 last year, a saving of about 105. Finance cost fell to 185.13 from 207.64. Together that is about 127 of the 236 PBT increase (my arithmetic). Part of the rest sits in the inventory line: "changes in inventories" is a 3,383.37 charge this quarter against a 1,683.79 credit in Q1, so Q1 built stock and Q2 sold it.
- The tax charge looks normal at about 25% of PBT (124.79 on 495.44), though last year's Q2 had a deferred tax credit of 116.27, which is why PAT grew less than PBT.

**The numbers, consolidated (p.13) [FILED]**
- Q2 revenue 6,126.73. Q2 PBT 835.77 against 319.88. Q2 PAT 623.98, of which owners 577.54 and minority 46.44. H1 PAT 1,070.18 against 660.98.
- Consolidated revenue is exactly standalone plus 384.96 (6,126.73 less 5,741.77, my arithmetic). The auditor says the subsidiary Kotyark Bio Specialities reported revenue 384.96 and PAT 253.31 for the quarter (p.11). So the subsidiary adds nothing but its own revenue, with no elimination of any sales to the parent.
- That subsidiary earns a 66% net margin on revenue (253.31 on 384.96). On my arithmetic its pre-tax margin is about 88% (consolidated PBT 835.77 less standalone PBT 495.44 is 340.33, over revenue 384.96). For a plant-based commodity business, that is not an operating margin. It looks like income from a service, a lease, a fee, a trading spread or an intercompany flow. [INFERENCE] The filing does not say which.
- H1 on the same basis: consolidated PBT 1,582.98 is 2.0 times standalone PBT 787.44. The subsidiary supplies about 795 of PBT on about 1,091 of revenue (my arithmetic).

**What is unusual, in order of importance**

1. The consolidated cash flow does not reconcile to the consolidated balance sheet. Standalone ties exactly: borrowings rise by 1,488.55 on the balance sheet (pp.5) and the cash flow shows 1,488.55 net proceeds (p.7). Consolidated does not:
   - Borrowings on the consolidated balance sheet rise 2,775.73 (non-current 2,077.64 to 2,791.64, current 4,409.28 to 6,471.01). The consolidated cash flow shows net borrowing of 4,638.36. Gap about 1,863 (my arithmetic).
   - Trade receivables rise 5,015.69 on the balance sheet (1,769.79 to 6,785.48). The cash flow shows an increase of 8,106.58. Gap about 3,091.
   - Trade payables rise 546.22 on the balance sheet (72.40 to 618.62). The cash flow shows 3,634.92. Gap about 3,089.
   - The receivable and payable gaps are nearly equal and opposite, so they probably wash out inside operating cash flow. [INFERENCE] That pattern fits a roughly Rs 31 Cr intercompany balance that was eliminated on the balance sheet and not in the cash flow. The filing gives no reconciliation.
2. Standalone trade payables are 3,897.79 but consolidated are only 618.62. About 3,279 of the parent's payables vanish on consolidation, so they are owed to a group company. [INFERENCE] The same sub is where the high margin sits. March shows the mirror: standalone non-current borrowings 3,363.50 against consolidated 2,077.64, a 1,286 difference. Related-party flows between parent and subsidiary look large. The results carry no related-party disclosure, and a Limited Review does not require one.
3. Consolidated cost lines are not the standalone lines plus the subsidiary. In Q1 consolidated "cost of materials consumed" is 5,863.92 against standalone 8,932.86, while consolidated "purchase of stock in trade" is 3,313.05 against standalone nil. About 3,069 of material cost was moved into a purchase line in consolidation. The filing does not explain it.
4. Cash conversion is poor. Standalone H1 operating cash flow is +762.33 (p.7), but only because trade payables rose 3,688.19. Receivables rose 4,936.90 (1,769.79 to 6,706.69). Consolidated H1 operating cash flow is negative 2,282.35 (p.14). The standalone receivable base went from about 17 days of FY26 revenue (1,769.79 on 29,649.16) to about 108 days of Q2 revenue annualised (6,706.69 on 5,741.77 times four). My arithmetic, not filed. Receivables almost quadrupled in six months while Q2 sales fell. The filing gives no ageing and no concentration.
5. Borrowings rose to fund it. Total consolidated borrowings are about 9,263 against about 6,487 in March (my arithmetic). The company also paid a dividend of 513.96 (p.7), which is Rs 5 on the pre-bonus share count (102.79 lakh shares, my arithmetic) while cash flow from operations was negative on a consolidated basis. Cash on hand is 1,708.97, up from 138.57, so the cash balance is borrowed.
6. The auditor's emphasis paragraph is unresolved (pp.4, 10). Four storage tanks of raw material (Veg Ester) at Swaroopganj were seized during the renewal of the Bio-Fuel Authority registration in Rajasthan. Criminal proceedings were started against one Director. The Rajasthan High Court order dated 26-Aug-2025 lets the company keep operating to meet its Oil Marketing Company supply commitments. The seized material is still carried as inventory "as the Management is confident of its release". No amount is stated. Total inventory is 13,269.37 standalone, so the exposure is some part of that. The paragraph is repeated from earlier periods and is not a qualification. Silence on the quantum is the gap.
7. A new statutory auditor. Talati & Talati LLP signed this review. The FY26 numbers were audited by the previous auditor (report dated 27-Apr-2026) (p.4). The filing does not give a reason for the change.
8. LLP exit. In June quarter the company disposed of its stakes in Asia Bio Fuels LLP and Parth Renewable Energy LLP "at the value same as the carrying cost" (p.4). The notes say these were 55% subsidiaries and were deconsolidated from 1-Apr-2026 (p.15). The buyer is not named and the filing does not say whether it is a related party. Standalone investment in subsidiaries fell from 140.00 to 85.00 and 110.00 came in as disinvestment while 55.00 went out as debentures into a subsidiary (p.7). Net, 55 of the 110 went straight back into a group entity. [INFERENCE] This is the money flow most worth asking about.
9. Bonus issue. 10,27,91,160 shares in 10:1 ratio in the June quarter (p.8). Share capital went from 1,027.91 to 11,307.03. Reserves fell from 14,857.64 to 4,546.34 standalone. This ties: 14,857.64 less bonus 10,279.12, less dividend 513.96, plus H1 profit and OCI about 482, is about 4,546 (my arithmetic). Reported EPS is shown twice, unrestated (2.64 for Q2 last year) and restated (0.24), which is confusing but correct. Use the restated row.
10. Minor. Item 88's cover says approval was given at 3:00 to 4:55 pm. The signing stamp on the cover letter is 17:15, 20 minutes later. This is normal.

**Two readings**
- Bull: standalone is clean (the cash flow ties to the balance sheet), PBT is up 91% in the quarter, the company has a high margin subsidiary and a bonus plus dividend show owner-friendly intent. Valuation is about 20 times H1 annualised owner profit (391 against 961.31 times 2, which is 1,922 lakh, my arithmetic) and about 2.1 times book (391 against 186.5, my arithmetic).
- Bear: the quarter's margin comes from lower depreciation, lower interest and a subsidiary margin of 88% that nobody has explained. Receivables quadrupled, consolidated operating cash flow is negative 22.8 Cr, the consolidated cash flow does not tie, standalone payables to a group company are about 33 Cr, and there is an open seizure and criminal case.
- The observation that separates them: the Q3 consolidated cash flow and a related-party schedule. If consolidated operating cash flow is positive and receivables fall below about 60 days of sales (about 4,000 on Q2 standalone sales), the bull case gains. If receivables stay above 6,500 with no ageing disclosed, the bear case gains. Check the Q3 result, due around mid-February 2027 (estimate, no date is filed).

**What the filing does NOT say.** The subsidiary's nature, ownership split (minority is about 18% on my arithmetic: 46.44 over 253.31), customer concentration, the seized stock quantum, the buyer of the LLP stakes, any related-party note, and any reason for the receivable jump. There is no press release or presentation in the window.

**Cross-links to companies on file.** None direct. The Oil Marketing Company supply angle sits next to the oil and gas names on file (Asian Energy), but the businesses differ. On receivables quality, Kotyark resembles the small cap names on file where working capital jumped with flat sales.

**Verdict in the operator's frame.** Not in master, so no thesis-broken trigger is possible. If you ever open a file, I would write the first gate as: consolidated operating cash flow positive, a related-party schedule with the subsidiary, and the seized stock amount. That is your call.

### Shilchar Technologies: H1 profit down 38% as Q2 margin recovery stays partial

Not in COMPANIES MASTER, so there is no thesis to test. This is an explanation of the filings. Items 174 and 175 are the same document (identical text, same five pages). Item 173 is the investor presentation. The results PDF has a noisy text layer. I rebuilt the figures and cross-checked them to the presentation (revenue, PBT and PAT tie). Comparatives and balance sheet lines below come from the results PDF and carry that caveat.

**The headline in the exchange summary is misleading.** Item 175 says "net profit rose to Rs 5,384 lakh". That is the six-month figure, and it is down. H1 FY27 PAT is ₹53.84 Cr against ₹87.43 Cr in H1 FY26, a fall of about 38% (results PDF, p.3; my arithmetic on the percentage). H1 revenue is ₹327.22 Cr against ₹330.03 Cr, so flat (results PDF, p.3). H1 PBT is ₹73.76 Cr against ₹117.13 Cr. The same summary also labels the period "Q2/H1 FY2026". It is FY2027.

**Quarter, as filed [FILED] (presentation p.6):**
- Q2 revenue ₹192.61 Cr: up 43% on Q1 (₹134.61 Cr), up 12% on Q2 FY26 (₹171.28 Cr).
- Q2 EBITDA ₹40.06 Cr, margin 20.8%. Q1 was 16.4%. Q2 FY26 was 31.3%. Margin is still 1,049 bps below a year ago.
- Q2 PAT ₹32.98 Cr, down 28% YoY, up 58% QoQ. EPS ₹28.83.
- Operating expenses rose 30% YoY on 12% revenue growth (₹117.68 Cr to ₹152.55 Cr). That is the whole story: the quarter grew, but each rupee of revenue carried much more cost.

**What management says [MGMT] (p.7).** Q1 was hit because Middle East exports were disrupted by the West Asia crisis. Q2 recovered, North America gained after tariff issues were resolved, and a higher export share "should translate directly" into better margins. The company says it is on track for its revenue target, with deliveries scheduled in H2, and Gavasad Expansion 3 is to be commissioned in April 2027.

**Testing the export-margin claim against the company's own history.** The presentation (p.20, p.28) gives export share and EBITDA margin by year: FY23 exports 56%, margin 18.9%; FY24 52%, 28.5%; FY25 43%, 29.6%; FY26 48%, 29.2%. The highest-export year had the lowest margin. So export share alone does not explain margin in the five-year record. Pricing, product mix and freight matter more. The filing gives no Q1 or Q2 export share. That is the number the claim needs. This is an observation, and the older years had a much smaller company.

**What is not said.**
- No numeric revenue target. "On track with our revenue target" has no figure. The only number is "Order pipeline for FY27 ~₹800 Cr" (p.25). A pipeline is not an order book and not a target. If anyone reads ₹800 Cr as the year's revenue, H2 would need about ₹473 Cr, roughly ₹237 Cr a quarter, which is 23% above Q2 (my arithmetic, not filed, and only under that reading).
- No order book figure, no order intake figure.
- No reason for the cost jump beyond "export mix". Copper, CRGO and freight are not discussed. Cost of materials in the results is ₹135.58 Cr on ₹192.61 Cr of revenue, about 70% (my arithmetic).
- Funding and size of the capex. Capacity goes from 7,500 to 14,000 MVA (p.14, p.9 says 6,500 MVA added). CWIP is ₹33.77 Cr against ₹6.41 Cr in March. H1 capex outflow is ₹32.93 Cr, including advances (results PDF, p.5). Total project cost is not stated in these papers.

**Cash and balance sheet (results PDF, p.4-5).**
- Cash from operations H1: about ₹12.4 Cr against ₹52.3 Cr in H1 FY26, against PAT of ₹53.8 Cr. Conversion is about 23% (my arithmetic).
- Trade receivables went from ₹153.60 Cr (31-Mar) to ₹188.88 Cr. That is about 90 days on Q2 annualised revenue (my arithmetic). The cash flow shows a ₹37.3 Cr receivable build.
- Other financial assets rose in both parts: current about ₹2.2 Cr to ₹29.4 Cr, non-current about ₹7.9 Cr to ₹17.9 Cr. Nature is not disclosed in the statement. The cash flow shows a ₹26.2 Cr "other assets" outflow.
- Liquid pool (investments, bank, cash) is about ₹208 Cr against ₹246 Cr in March, which matches the presentation's ₹245.95 Cr (p.30). It fell despite profit because of ₹14.3 Cr dividend, ₹32.9 Cr capex and working capital. Still debt free. Finance cost is ₹0.10 Cr a quarter.
- Other income is ₹5.80 Cr in Q2 and ₹13.97 Cr in H1. Of the H1 figure, ₹5.36 Cr is a fair-value gain on investments (cash flow, p.5). That is mark-to-market, not operating.

**Two readings.**
- Bull: Q1 was a one-off shock. Q2 shows revenue back to ₹193 Cr, margin up 442 bps in a quarter, North America filling the Middle East gap, ₹800 Cr pipeline, new capacity from April 2027, debt free with cash about 48% of market cap (₹208 Cr of ₹4,345 Cr; my arithmetic).
- Bear: Margin is still 10 points below last year and the cause (cost ratio, not just mix) is unexplained. Receivables and "other assets" are absorbing cash. The company is adding 87% more capacity (7,500 to 14,000 MVA) while H1 revenue is flat YoY and FY26 utilisation was 79% (p.14).
- One observation that separates them: Q3 EBITDA margin. Above about 25% with exports named as the cause supports the bull case. Stuck near 20% means the cost base has reset lower. Date: Q3 results, around late January 2027. Add H2 revenue against the ₹193 Cr run-rate.

**Price context.** ₹3,798 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:39 IST); 52-week range ₹2,851 to ₹5,399; market cap ₹4,345 Cr. This is the 28-Oct close. The board met 4:30 to 5:20 pm, after market close, so the price does not yet show the result. On FY26 PAT of ₹158.16 Cr (presentation p.29) that is about 27x. On H1 PAT doubled it is about 40x (my arithmetic, crude). The stock is 30% below its high.

**Small points.** The cover letter says "Wedesday". The Q1 revenue appears as ₹134.61 Cr in the presentation and is consistent with H1 less Q2. The statutory auditor is CNK & Associates LLP; the report is a limited review with no qualification.

**Who this matters to.** Anyone tracking Indian transformer and T&D capex names. Same-day names on the list that may carry related read-across: Atlanta Electricals and Quality Power Electrical Equipments. I have not checked their master status. Transrail Lighting (on file per the 1-Oct review) sits on the same grid-capex demand, but it is a tower and EPC business, so read-across is on demand only.

### HEG Advanced Materials: postal ballot cements a family-run battery platform

HEG Advanced Materials is NOT in COMPANIES MASTER. Per the 1-Oct review it appears only as a peer reference on the Graphite India page, so there is no thesis to test. Price: ₹229 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:37 IST); 52-week high ₹278, low ₹172; market cap ₹7,544 Cr. Whether screener's 52-week range is adjusted for the 1-Sept demerger is NOT FOUND, so treat the range with care.

**What was filed (item 67).** A 41-page postal ballot notice dated 28-Oct. E-voting runs 29-Oct (9:00) to 27-Nov-2026 (17:00) on NSDL; cut-off date 23-Oct; results by 1-Dec; resolutions are deemed passed on 27-Nov if the majority is met (HEG_Advanced_Materials_Shareholder_Meeting__Postal_Ballot-Notic_7788f4.pdf pp.2 to 4). Eight items: two non-independent directors (ordinary resolutions), a new Chairman, MD and CEO with remuneration (special), and five independent directors (special). All were board-appointed on 1-Sept-2026, the date the Composite Scheme took effect. SEBI Reg 17(1C) requires shareholder approval within three months (by 30-Nov), so the 27-Nov close leaves three days of margin.

**The real news is in the explanatory statement: what the listed entity now is.** Read the structure carefully, because the names are confusing.
- Under the Composite Scheme effective 1-Sept-2026, the graphite electrode business moved to a resulting company called HEG Graphite Limited (the annexure calls it "Now HEG Limited"). The listed entity here, HEG Advanced Materials (formerly HEG Limited), kept the non-graphite assets, and Bhilwara Energy Limited merged into it (p.36).
- What the listed entity describes itself as (Annexure-II, pp.36 to 38): a battery materials and energy-storage platform. Holdings named: Malana and AD Hydro (two operating hydro projects, 278 MW), a 14 MW wind project (operating since March 2014), Replus Engitech (BESS, a 1 GWh factory near Pune, expanding to 6 GWh), TACC Limited (anode, 20,000 MT a year, "expected to commence operation from next Financial Year"), and Bhilwara Energy Storage Solutions (a 100 MW / 200 MWh GUVNL standalone BESS project, commissioning "later part of FY 2026-27"; the notice's own text prints it as "100 MWh/200 MWh", which looks like a typo).
- Standalone financials, restated to the appointed date of 1-Apr-2024 (p.36): FY26 total income ₹17,888 lakh (₹179 Cr), PBT ₹10,303 lakh, PAT ₹9,664 lakh (₹96.6 Cr), net worth ₹4,82,998 lakh (₹4,830 Cr). FY25 total income ₹261 Cr, PAT ₹161.8 Cr. FY24 (graphite business, pre-appointed date) income ₹2,537 Cr, PAT ₹231.5 Cr. So the FY24 line is a different business and is not comparable. Standalone income is mostly dividends from subsidiaries; consolidated numbers are NOT in this filing.
- My arithmetic, not filed: ₹7,544 Cr market cap on ₹4,830 Cr standalone net worth is about 1.56x book. That net worth is FY26 restated and at book value of subsidiaries.

**Item 3, the remuneration, is the item that needs a vote decision.** Riju Jhunjhunwala becomes Chairman, Managing Director and CEO from 1-Sept-2026 to 31-Aug-2031 (five years). His father, Ravi Jhunjhunwala, gave up the same roles here and moved to Chairman, MD and CEO of HEG Graphite Limited. The stated reason is the two-company limit on managing directorships, since Ravi is also Chairman and MD of Malana Power (pp.20 to 21). Ravi stays as a non-executive director here. Riju was Vice Chairman (non-executive) since 2010.
- Basic salary ₹35 lakh a month with a 15% annual increase, fixed for three years to 31-Aug-2029 (p.4). That is ₹4.20 Cr in year one. If the 15% compounds: ₹4.83 Cr in year two and ₹5.55 Cr in year three, about ₹14.6 Cr basic over three years. If simple, ₹4.83 Cr then ₹5.46 Cr. The wording does not say which (my arithmetic). Plus perquisites in three categories and a car for personal use. The only capped perquisites: utilities at 10% of basic, medical at one month's basic.
- Special resolution is needed under Reg 17(6)(e) because pay exceeds ₹5 Cr or 2.5% of net profit, whichever is higher (p.22). On FY26 standalone PAT of ₹96.6 Cr, 2.5% is about ₹2.4 Cr, so the ₹5 Cr limb is the live one, and year-three basic alone passes it.
- Terms a minority holder should note: minimum remuneration is payable even in a loss year; he may draw it "in addition to" pay from other companies even above the Schedule V limit; no performance link, no commission, no stock options and no severance (p.41). Remuneration for the last two years of the five-year term (Sept 2029 to Aug 2031) is not set.
- Other pay on file for him (p.40): RSWM Limited, where he is Chairman, MD and CEO, ₹451.86 lakh in FY26, and Bhilwara Energy (merged into this company) ₹209.89 lakh. FY26 total ₹661.75 lakh (₹6.6 Cr). After the merger the Bhilwara Energy line ends and a ₹4.2 Cr line here starts, so his group pay moves from about ₹6.6 Cr to about ₹8.7 Cr in year one on basic alone (my arithmetic).
- Inconsistency: the resolution title and text say "Managing Director & CEO"; the explanatory statement and a later resolved clause make him Chairman as well. Minor, but the chair is now an executive and promoter-family, so the Board needs at least half independent. Five independents are proposed. The total board size is NOT FOUND in the notice, so that test cannot be completed.
- The "reason for inadequate profit" boilerplate (p.39) says the company only runs a 14 MW wind project and depends on subsidiary dividends. That contradicts a positive ₹96.6 Cr standalone PAT and reads as the standard Schedule V form, not a real profit warning.

**The non-independent directors.**
- Om Prakash Ajmera, 65, FCA and ACS, with the LNJ Bhilwara Group for over 40 years. He is the "Group CFO of the Company (without remuneration)", sits on this company's Audit Committee and Nomination and Remuneration Committee, chairs its Stakeholders Relationship Committee, and chairs the Audit Committee of Replus (pp.19, 30). A non-executive director who also acts as the group CFO and sits on the audit committee overseeing numbers he prepares is a governance question. The committee columns in the annexure are interleaved in the OCR, so the allocation between him and the other director is my best reading.
- Yash Kela, 35, founder of Singularity AMC (AIFs managing over ₹3,000 Cr). Listed directorships include Lohum Cleantech and ILJIN Technologies (battery value-chain names), Sterling Green Power Solutions and Sterling and Wilson Data Center (p.30). Member of the Risk Management Committee. He brings battery and investment-industry contacts, and also possible related-party overlaps, which the notice does not address.

**The independents (items 4 to 8).** Manish Sharma (ex-Panasonic Life Solutions India chairman, electronics), Ameya Prabhu (Aarna Holdings, NAFA Capital; waives sitting fees), Pushp Jain (KLJ Group chemicals), Sanjeev Mehra (power sector; already an independent director of Malana Power and AD Hydro, two HEG subsidiaries), and Rajiv Dewan (practising chartered accountant; ex-senior roles in textile companies). Mehra's seat on two subsidiary boards is normal in groups but is a reason to ask how independent a person is when the same family controls both. None has attended any board meeting yet ("N.A", pp.31 to 37). Shareholding: nil for those shown.

**What the notice does not say.**
- The promoter shareholding and the vote needed. The special resolutions need 75% and promoter votes are not excluded on this item, so passage is close to assured. Foreign investment is 20.35% at 30-Sept (p.37).
- Capex and funding for the 6 GWh Replus expansion, the 20,000 MT anode plant and the 200 MWh BESS. For a ₹7,544 Cr company that is the key number and it is not given.
- Any consolidated profit and loss, so the real earning power is hidden.
- Whether the price and the 52-week range are comparable across the demerger date.

**Cross-links.**
- Graphite India (on file, peer reference): the electrode business HEG used to run is now a separate entity, "HEG Graphite Limited". The old HEG listing is no longer a graphite-electrode peer. Whether HEG Graphite is separately listed, and when, is NOT FOUND in this notice and should be checked before the peer reference is used again.
- Replus and the Indus Towers MoU (1-Oct review): that MoU intended 1.5 GWh of capacity over two years. The factory today is 1 GWh (p.38), so the MoU only works if the 6 GWh expansion happens. The notice gives no date for it. Same telecom battery lane as Transrail's memorandum clause logged on 28-Jul; the crowding point stands.
- RSWM Limited (Riju Jhunjhunwala is its chairman and MD): not on file today; checked only as a group-pay link.

**Verdict.** Not in master, no gate fires. Governance read: family control is now complete (son replaces father as executive chair), the pay is high and uncapped in several ways for a company whose standalone income is about ₹179 Cr, and the audit committee includes the group CFO. The business read: the filing confirms a capital-heavy battery build with no financing detail.

### Pranik Logistics: BB+ reaffirmed with limits up 69%, free liquidity thin

Pranik Logistics is not in COMPANIES MASTER (notion_page is null). The filing is a CARE Ratings press release dated 27-Oct-2026 (item 127, 6 pages, read in full). It is a credit read, and it has more in it than the "reaffirmed" label suggests.

**The action [FILED, p.1, p.4]**
- Long-term bank facilities ₹48.00 Cr (enhanced from ₹29.35 Cr): CARE BB+; Stable, reaffirmed. This is the fund-based cash credit.
- Long-term and short-term facilities ₹2.80 Cr (enhanced from ₹0.65 Cr): CARE BB+; Stable / CARE A4, reaffirmed. This is the bank guarantee line.
- Total limits ₹50.80 Cr against ₹30.00 Cr, up about 69% (my arithmetic). BB+ is below investment grade. The previous reviews were 24-Apr-25 and 06-Mar-26, both BB+; Stable (p.4).

**Numbers [FILED, p.2 to 3]**
- Revenue ₹159.73 Cr in FY26 against ₹104.76 Cr, up 52.5%. Q1 FY27 revenue ₹50.17 Cr against ₹39.16 Cr, up about 28%. The text says ₹50.17 Cr and the table says total operating income ₹50.31 Cr. Small gap, probably other operating income. Not explained.
- PBILDT margin 9.55% in FY26 against 11.45%. Q1 FY27 about 9.2% (₹4.64 Cr on ₹50.31 Cr, my arithmetic). CARE expects 9 to 11% over the medium term.
- PAT ₹7.04 Cr against ₹6.44 Cr, up about 9% on 52% revenue growth. PAT margin about 4.4% against 6.1% (my arithmetic). Q1 FY27 PAT ₹2.04 Cr.
- Overall gearing 0.99x against 0.51x. Debt to equity 0.24x against 0.15x. Total debt to gross cash accruals 3.62x against 2.03x. PBILDT interest cover 5.04x against 6.46x, and 4.55x in Q1 FY27.
- Working capital cycle 93 days against 82 days. Collection period 96 days against 86 days. Credit period given to clients is 60 to 90 days, with almost no credit from suppliers.
- Average working capital utilisation 92% over the 12 months to August 2026.
- Liquidity ₹16.55 Cr including ₹15.23 Cr of lien-marked fixed deposits. So free liquidity is about ₹1.32 Cr (my arithmetic).
- Gross cash accruals ₹11.92 Cr in FY26. Debt repayment about ₹6 Cr expected in FY27.
- Top three customers 62% of income in FY26, against 65% and 77% in FY25 and FY24. Named customers: Qwik Supply Chain, ACC, Ambuja Cements. New in FY26: Flipkart Group entities, BigBasket, Meesho, Piramal Pharma. 46 leased warehouses.

**Testing CARE's own words against its own numbers**
- One of CARE's negative triggers is "elongation of its operating cycle to over 90 days" (p.1). The FY26 cycle is 93 days. The trigger is already past, on CARE's own figure, and the rating was still reaffirmed. The Q1 FY27 cycle is not given.
- Another trigger is TOI below ₹60 Cr. FY26 is ₹159.73 Cr, so that one is far away. The positive trigger needs TOI above ₹120 Cr (met) with margin above 10% on a sustained basis (not met: 9.55%).
- The outlook text calls the financial risk profile "comfortable" while the same report shows gearing near 1.0x, utilisation at 92% and free liquidity near ₹1.3 Cr. The words are softer than the data.
- CARE says gearing will weaken further: new vehicle purchases and an ECLGS loan are planned this year (p.1). Growth is being bought with debt.

**Two readings**
- Bull: Revenue is growing at a 38% three-year rate, customer mix is broadening to e-commerce and pharma, concentration is falling, interest cover is still above 4.5x, and the banks raised limits by about 69%.
- Bear: Profit is not keeping up with revenue. Cash cycle is above the agency's own trigger. Working capital lines are 92% used, so the larger limits may simply be absorbed. B2B growth costs margin.
- The observation that separates them is the Q2 FY27 working capital cycle and utilisation. Back under 90 days and utilisation below about 85% would favour the bull. Above 95 days with utilisation near 95% or more would favour the bear. These thresholds are mine.

**What it does not say** No debt maturity ladder, no cash flow from operations, no promoter pledge, no detail on the ECLGS amount. The lender list is behind a link (Annexure-4).

**Price context** ₹37 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:38 IST). 52-week range ₹36.2 to ₹84.4. Market cap ₹40.8 Cr. The stock is about 2% above its low and about 56% below its high. Market cap is about 5.8x FY26 PAT of ₹7.04 Cr (my arithmetic). Whatever the market is pricing, it is not the revenue growth.

**Cross-links** ACC and Ambuja are also ash customers of Refex Industries in this batch (Refex is not on file either). No company on file is directly linked.

### Vishnu Chemicals: Parwada plant stopped from 28-Oct, no restart date

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. No thesis to test. Item 206, one page (Vishnu_Chemicals_Intimation_Regarding_Temporary_Stoppage__f95739.pdf, p.1). The one operational item in this batch.

- [FILED] Operations at the Visakhapatnam Parwada Plant undergo "temporary stoppage" from 28-Oct-2026 to synchronise "forward integration and new capital projects" with the unit (p.1). The company will inform the exchanges when it restarts.
- The filing is time-stamped 15:17 IST on 28-Oct, the same day the stoppage began. No advance notice.
- What the filing does NOT say: how long the stoppage lasts, what share of group capacity or revenue Parwada is, which products it makes, what the forward-integration project is, its cost, or whether stock covers customers during the stop. Silence on duration is the key gap. A stop with no end date is open-ended.
- Plain reading: new equipment is being tied into a running plant and the company chose to halt it. The text says the work is done "alongside the unit's existing operations", which implies a planned pause and a restart, not a failure. [INFERENCE]
- Bull reading: capex is being commissioned and the stop is the cost of growth. Bear reading: Q3 FY27 volumes and margin take a hit, and a long delay pushes out the benefit. The observation that separates them is the restart intimation. Within about 4 to 6 weeks (my threshold, not company guidance) supports the bull reading. No restart notice by the Q3 results date supports the bear reading.
- Price: ₹568 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:40 IST). 52-week range ₹470 to ₹744, market cap ₹3,827 Cr. About 24% below the high and 21% above the low (my arithmetic, not filed). The 05:40 IST read is the 28-Oct close, taken before the 29-Oct session, so it may not reflect this news. Check the 29-Oct trade.
- Vishnu Chemicals makes chromium and vanadium chemicals (my knowledge, not from the filing). Parwada is in Visakhapatnam district, where Pajson Agro also has a unit. Geography only.
- Cross-link: none on file. The Q3 results commentary is where the cost of the stoppage will show.

### Chandan Healthca: EGM seeks ₹85 Cr related-party and guarantee approvals

Chandan Healthcare is not in COMPANIES MASTER, so no thesis test. Item 36 is a shareholders' meeting notice with no PDF (HTTP 403). Summary: an EGM on 20-Nov-2026 to approve related-party transactions and personal guarantees, each up to ₹85 Cr [summary only].

Arithmetic (my arithmetic): ₹85 Cr is 15.5% of the ₹548 Cr market cap. If both items run to the cap, the combined headroom is ₹170 Cr, 31% of market cap.

Price context: ₹224 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:36 IST), 52-week range ₹185 to ₹358, market cap ₹548 Cr. About 37% below the high and 21% above the low.

What I cannot tell from the summary, and the notice will:
- Who the related parties are, and the nature of the transactions (loans, purchases, leases, services).
- Whether the "personal guarantees" are given by promoters or directors for company borrowings (common, usually costless) or by the company for someone else (a risk). The wording is ambiguous. If promoters guarantee company debt and the company pays them a commission, that commission is itself a related-party payment.
- Whether the ₹85 Cr is a per-party cap or an aggregate.
- Related parties cannot vote on their own related-party resolutions, so the public holders decide. Turnout and the public vote split on 20-Nov will show how they read it.

Two readings: the benign one is that this is lender-driven housekeeping before a bank facility, where the promoter backs the company. The adverse one is that money is moving to or from promoter-linked entities at scale in a ₹548 Cr company. The notice, and the Regulation 23 related-party disclosures, separate them.

Loose cross-link: Unihealth Hospitals and Entero Healthcare are on file in the healthcare delivery and distribution space, but there is no direct link.

Why it still matters: an IPO disclosure penalty means the offer document was wrong or incomplete on something SEBI considered material. The summary does not say which item. The things worth finding in the order are whether it concerns use of IPO proceeds, related party dealings, litigation, or promoter holdings. Use of proceeds or related party issues would reach the current business; a procedural lapse would not. The company filed it as a "material issue" under Regulation 30, so it considers the order disclosable.

### Silkflex Polymer: director puts ₹24.6 Cr into 12 lakh warrants

Silkflex Polymers (India) is not in COMPANIES MASTER, so no thesis test. This is an insider disclosure with no PDF and no summary text. The filing type reads "Buy of 1,200,000 warrant worth Rs 2460.00 lacs by director" (item 177). The PDF download failed (HTTP 403), so I am working from the type line only.

Arithmetic (my arithmetic, not filed): ₹2,460 lakh is ₹24.6 Cr. Divided by 12,00,000 warrants gives ₹205 per warrant.

Price context: ₹287 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:39 IST), 52-week range ₹76 to ₹309, market cap ₹333 Cr. That is 3.8 times the low and 7% below the high.

What the numbers imply:
- If the ₹24.6 Cr is the full price, the warrants cost ₹205 against ₹287 now, a 28.6% discount. The paper gain is about ₹9.8 Cr on 12 lakh warrants. A preferential price that is set off an earlier average, in a stock that has risen from ₹76, explains the gap. [INFERENCE]
- If ₹24.6 Cr is only the 25% upfront payment, the full price would be ₹820 per warrant, nearly three times the market. That is not credible, so I read it as full payment.
- Size: the director's cheque is 7.4% of market cap. 12 lakh warrants are about 10% of the current share count (about 1.16 Cr shares, from market cap over price) and about 9.4% of the enlarged base if converted.

What the full Form C or allotment filing would add: director's name and role, whether he is a promoter, holding before and after, the allotment date, the issue price and whether it was a preferential issue approved by shareholders, and the conversion period (up to 18 months). A promoter-director subscribing at this scale is a conviction signal; a discount to market also means dilution of the public holders. Both readings face the same test: the price per warrant.

No cross-link on file.

### Urban Enviro: ₹69 Cr rights issue at ₹80 opens 4-Nov

Urban Enviro Waste Management is not in COMPANIES MASTER. Two items (197, 198), both headed only "Updates", with no PDF (the downloads failed, HTTP 403). **What it means**
- ₹80 is a 27% discount to ₹110 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:40 IST). If the ratio is 1:1, a holder who does not take up the rights is diluted and the theoretical ex-rights price is about ₹95, 14% below ₹110 (my arithmetic: (110 + 80) / 2). Because the record date was 28-Oct and the settlement cycle is T+1, the stock probably went ex-rights on 28-Oct [INFERENCE]. If so, the ₹110 read this morning may already be an ex-rights price, and this card cannot tell.

Screener shows ₹190 Cr, which implies about 1.73 Cr shares and a ratio nearer 1 for 2. Either the aggregator's 1:1 or the screener share count is wrong (an earlier 1:1 bonus in Feb 2025 may explain a stale count). The letter of offer will settle it. At 1 for 2 the ex-rights price would be about ₹100.

### Cummins India: ₹10.4 Cr GST order under Section 74, appeal likely

Cummins India is not in COMPANIES MASTER (notion_page is null). Two filings, one matters a little.

**GST order [FILED, Cummins_..._82bcfc.txt p.1 to 2]**
- Order from the Additional Commissioner, Office of the Principal Commissioner, CGST and Central Excise, Jaipur, received 28-Oct-2026, disclosed the same day at 17:25 IST. No delay.
- IGST demand ₹5,19,10,293 under Section 74 of the CGST Act, plus an equal penalty of ₹5,19,10,293 under Section 122(2)(b). Total ₹10.38 Cr before any interest (my arithmetic). Period: 2020-21 to 2023-24. The filing says "assessment years"; GST works on financial years.
- Why it matters in law: Section 74 is the section used where the officer alleges fraud, wilful misstatement or suppression. That is why the penalty equals the tax and the look-back is long. It does not mean fraud is proven. The company has not been shown to have lost anything yet, and it plans an appeal.
- Management says the penalty arises "solely on account of an underlying adjustment made during the assessment proceedings", and that there is no material impact on financials or operations [MGMT]. The company is "evaluating appropriate legal remedies, including filing an appeal" (p.1).

**Scale (my arithmetic, not filed)**
- ₹10.38 Cr against a market cap of ₹128,066 Cr is about 0.008%. Immaterial to value.

**What it does not say**
- The nature of the "adjustment" (input credit, classification, place of supply, or other) is not stated. The Jaipur link (a branch, a supply location) is not stated. Interest is not stated. Whether the company will pay a pre-deposit to appeal (7.5 percent of demand is the usual statutory pre-deposit, not in the filing) is not stated. Whether a provision exists is not stated.
- Same-day disclosure with the full Annexure A is correct form. There is no sign of delay.

**Call notice [FILED, ..._An_9371d3.txt p.1 to 2]**
- Q2 and H1 FY27 results conference call Monday 16-Nov-2026, 10:00 IST. The results date itself is not stated. Under the 45-day rule the results must be out by 14-Nov-2026 (my reading of the LODR timeline).
- Mr. Adegbile Adewunmi is named as Managing Director "effective November 1, 2026" and will lead the call. This is the first call under the new MD, a point worth listening for on capacity and export commentary.

**Price context** ₹4,620 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:36 IST). 52-week range ₹3,883 to ₹6,143. Market cap ₹128,066 Cr. About 25% below the high and 19% above the low.

**Cross-links** Cummins sells gensets and power equipment into data-centre and infrastructure build. On file in this batch, Atlanta Electricals and Bajel Projects serve the same power-equipment demand. No filed link, theme only.

### Mahindra Logistics: Nippon fund sells 1.39% over three and a half years

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. The filing is a Regulation 29(2) disclosure by Nippon Life India Asset Management for Nippon India Mutual Fund (scanned pages, read from the images, items 96, p.1-3). Nippon is not a promoter.

**Numbers [FILED] (p.2-3).**
- Before: 44,02,595 shares, 4.4346% of capital.
- Sold: 13,75,681 shares, 1.3857%, in the open market.
- After: 30,26,914 shares, 3.0489%.
- Capital base used: 9,92,77,402 shares of ₹10 (₹99.28 Cr).
- Sale window stated: 28-Mar-2023 to 26-Oct-2026.

**Reading it.** At ₹431 the sold block is worth about ₹59 Cr (my arithmetic, using today's price, not the sale prices). The window is 3.5 years long, so this is the sum of many sales, not one day's trade. It is a fund running down a position, not a block deal. The date field gives the window only. It does not tell us how much was sold in the last weeks.

**What it does not say.** The "before" percentage (4.4346%) is computed on today's share count. The filing does not give the holding at 28-Mar-2023 or the share count then, so part of the fall could be dilution rather than selling. It does not give prices, and it does not say which threshold triggered the filing. The sale of 1.39% on its own is below the 2% change that normally triggers a Regulation 29(2) filing for a holder, so the cumulative window is the likely reason. That is inference.

**Why it is not important.** A mutual fund trimming from 4.4% to 3.0% is portfolio management. Mahindra Logistics is at ₹431 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:37 IST), close to the 52-week high of ₹451 and 57% above the low of ₹275. Market cap ₹4,282 Cr. Selling into strength is normal behaviour. No change to any view.

- New centres in Pune (Punyaee Business Square), Bengaluru (Prestige Tech Forest) and Mumbai (Lodha i-Think). About 145,000 sq ft of chargeable area, on the company's latest "Awfis 6.0" design. These sit on top of about 100,000 sq ft in the Elite centre at Aerocity, New Delhi, launched "recently". The cumulative figure is about 245,000 sq ft (that is the company's sum, and it adds up).
- The strategy line is premiumisation: a higher share of Grade A+, Gold and Elite centres "by the end of FY27". No target percentage is given.
- Company profile in the release: 18 cities, 250+ centres, 3,600+ clients.

**What it does not say [my reading, silence is information]**
- No seat count, no rent or revenue-share terms, no capex per sq ft, no lease tenure, no occupancy, no expected breakeven date. The release reports space added, which is cost incurred, not revenue earned.
- No opening date per centre. "Launched" and "added" are used loosely, and the footnote says counts include fit-out centres. Whether these are operating or still fitting out is not clear.
- No link to the numbers due in six days.

**Dates [FILED, p.1 of each]**
- Board meeting Tuesday 3-Nov-2026 for Q2 and H1 results (the Screener line calls it "FY2026"; the filing says quarter ended 30-Sep-2026, which is FY27).
- Earnings call Wednesday 4-Nov-2026, 10:30 IST, hosted by Nirmal Bang. Speakers: Amit Ramani (Chairman and MD), Sumit Lakhani (CEO), Sumit Rochlani (CFO). The presentation will follow the results.
- Trading window closed from 1-Oct-2026 until 48 hours after results.

**What to test on 3 and 4 Nov** The premium push raises capex and pre-opening cost before revenue. The numbers that show whether it works are chargeable-area occupancy, rent per seat, and the lease-cost line against revenue. None is in today's filing.

**Price context** ₹240 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:35 IST). 52-week range ₹216 to ₹639. Market cap ₹1,715 Cr. About 62% below the high and about 11% above the low. The stock is near its low on the eve of results.

**Cross-links** None firm. The theme (Grade A office demand, GCC leasing) touches real-estate names on file, such as Raymond Realty, only loosely. No company-specific link.

### Karnika Industri: postal ballot opens 29 October for main board move

Karnika Industries is not in COMPANIES MASTER, so no thesis test. Item 86 is a shareholders' meeting item with no PDF (HTTP 403). Summary: postal ballot e-voting starts 29-Oct-2026, to approve migration to the main board and the appointment of Priyanka Hirawat [summary only]. The close date of voting is not in the summary; postal ballots normally run about 30 days.

Why the main board move matters (background, not from the filing): a migration from an SME platform needs a special resolution by postal ballot, and under SEBI's ICDR rules the votes of non-promoter holders in favour must be at least twice those against. So the public holders have a veto here. A main board listing widens the buyer pool (funds that cannot hold SME stock) and ends SME trading rules. It does not change the business.

Price context: ₹126 (screener.in, 29-Oct-2026 05:37 IST), 52-week range ₹90.2 to ₹177, market cap ₹781 Cr. About 29% below the high and 40% above the low.

Not in the summary: the capacity of Priyanka Hirawat (independent, executive or woman director seat) and any promoter link. Both are in the postal ballot notice. Check the notice for the migration eligibility basis and for any other special business bundled in. No cross-link on file.

## Cross-links

**Profit is not reaching cash this season.** Nine results on file today show H1 profit running well ahead of operating cash. Aarti Pharma converts 0.20x (H1 CFO ₹30 Cr on PAT ₹147.6 Cr), Route Mobile 0.22x after a ₹116.83 Cr vendor advance, Macfos -1.66x, Sasken -₹0.23 Cr, Fabtech -₹41.14 Cr, Aeroflex 0.69x and PNGS Gargi 0.44x. Off the master list, Refex shows CFO ₹24 Cr against PAT ₹133 Cr and Sky Gold is cash-positive only because payables rose about 5x. The pattern matters for the gates: most of your cash lines were written for full-year data, and today's H1 numbers are pulling the rulings forward.

**Working capital funded by someone else.** PNGS Gargi passes 0.35x only because trade payables rose ₹26.92 Cr (₹6.25 to ₹33.17 Cr), and Sky Gold's cash flow turns on the same lever. In both cases the supplier's identity decides whether this is trade credit or a related-party transfer; for Gargi, if the supplier is P N Gadgil & Sons, trigger 4 (RPT restructuring) is in play. Fabtech's T08 analyst made the same point: working capital, not PAT, decided both reads in that batch.

**Inventory rebuilt with imported inputs.** Macfos (inventory ₹79.57 Cr to ₹115.94 Cr, ST debt ₹63.12 Cr above the ₹59.8 Cr logged lines) and Forbes Precision (inventory ₹56.42 Cr to ₹66.32 Cr, materials 40.5% of sales) both reversed a one-quarter destock in Q2, and both import key inputs from China. Aequs (H1 inventory +₹106.7 Cr, negative CFO, on file 26-Oct) makes three names this week where stock building absorbs growth. If this is pre-buying ahead of price or supply risk, it should unwind in H2; if not, the cash multipliers on all three need cutting.

**Accounting tailwinds inside headline profit.** Several prints carry one-off or policy-driven profit. Aeroflex's WDV-to-SLM switch, back-dated to 1-Apr, put a ₹8.41 Cr catch-up into Q2. PVR Inox's projector useful-life change is worth about ₹211 mn a quarter. Hexaware's 30% QoQ PAT is mostly a ₹1,663 Mn earnout write-back. Onemi's whole QoQ PAT growth sits in a ₹32.79 Cr consolidation adjustment. Route Mobile clears its 10% line only after a ₹6.9 Cr add-back. When checking a gate this season, test it on the clean number first.

**The weak rupee flatters reported growth.** The rupee averaged about 95.5 to 95.6 against the dollar this quarter (Hexaware: 95.48 against 88.19 a year ago). Hexaware's 12.9% rupee growth is 4.3% in dollars; Vaibhav Global's +7% is -1.7% at constant currency. Syngene's hedge book turned the same move into a ₹61 Cr Q2 loss, while TAAL Tech, whose hedging policy is not on file, books it as margin. TAAL's 3-Nov proof gate has a dollar leg (USD 6.3m x ~95.6 = ~₹60.2 Cr), so read its rupee revenue against that line, not against Q1.

**MFI and small-ticket credit stress, read across four names.** Satin (Q2 results today) borrows 5.6% of its funds from Bandhan Bank (Q2 results today). Satin's lender count fell from 77 to 69 and its new ₹92 Cr Assam calamity restructuring is classed standard. Bandhan's EEB 0-90 DPD improved from 3.5% to 2.9%. Aye Finance (held) blamed "industry wide stress" in micro-business and MFI loans for its covenant note, and Paisalo's low Q2 credit cost came from a provision write-back while 20.1% of its AUM sits in Maharashtra, the state its CBO calls most drought-hit. Satin's and Bandhan's stage-3 and write-off trends are the free outside check on Aye's claim.

**Auditor exits in one group and one rule.** Raymond Realty's joint auditor Chaturvedi & Shah resigned on fees on 28-Oct. Raymond Lifestyle is the same group and the N02 analyst notes the same auditor firm. The FlySBS page treats auditor resignations as an auto-AVOID, but that rule was written for twin resignations; this is one joint auditor on fees, with Walker Chandiok staying. Sky Gold and Kotyark also changed statutory auditors without a stated reason. The Raymond Realty ruling should state whether a fee exit counts, so the rule reads the same way across names.

**Promoter and related-party texture.** Paisalo's promoter entity EVC pledged 1.10% to Jio Credit as collateral for an unnamed third party's loan, taking group pledge to 8.62% against the 9% line. Aequs paid ₹2.57 Cr of IPO money as rent to Aequs SEZ Pvt Ltd, whose related-party status is unverified. Goodluck's GDAL raise shows ₹30 Cr less cash (₹245.90 Cr) than the ₹276 Cr raised. Arisinfra put a fourth subsidiary on parent credit. Each is small alone; each is a one-question IR check that closes a governance item.

**Same family, same handover.** Aarti Pharma's CFO resigned by letter dated 8-Jul, disclosed only on 28-Oct, across the AGM and the 1-Oct MD handover. Aarti Drugs, which shares the 1-Oct MD handover on file, has no CFO on its 4-Nov call panel and a CFO tripwire about 61 days out (~29-Dec). Read the two together: both companies now run their finance seat through a transition at the same time.

**SME half-yearly calendars break quarterly gates.** Unihealth, Vivid Electromech, Rajesh Power and Monarch report half-yearly. Rajesh Power confirmed there was never a Q1 FY27 print, so the August "binary gate" had nothing to read. Vivid's record re-pointed its quarterly gates to H1 on 25-Sep; Unihealth's has not. Pace Digitek's Reading A vs B is the same problem in another form.

**Grid and data-centre capex, margin first.** Shilchar's Q2 margin is 20.8% against 31.3% a year ago, with H1 PAT down 38%. That is the first same-season margin read before Atlanta Electricals' 17% gate (call 2-Nov) and Quality Power's 31-Oct print. Aeroflex is doubling skid capacity into data-centre cooling, which lifts the portfolio's data-centre concentration (about 15.5% on file with KRN, E2E and Sterlite). EPack's Ghiloth adds 61% panel capacity while panels run at 49.5%.

**Aerospace machining chain.** Aequs buys CNC machines with IPO money, Goodluck's GDAL plans an ₹80 Cr aerospace components line, Macpower sells CNC machines and Forbes Precision's premium-mix row waits for a Tier-2 aerospace customer to name TOTEM as a vendor. Unimech (call audio posted) and TAAL (prints 3-Nov) are the listed export reads. No filing today links any two of them directly; the T07 link is marked inference.

**Telecom battery lane, again.** HEG Advanced Materials' postal ballot confirms the post-demerger battery platform: Replus runs 1 GWh today against the 1.5 GWh Indus Towers MoU logged on 1-Oct. That is the same telecom battery lane Transrail's memorandum clause targets. The crowding point you logged on 28-Jul still stands. The Graphite India page's peer reference also needs updating: electrodes now sit in HEG Graphite Limited.

**Results cluster 31-Oct to 7-Nov.** Many gates on file print inside eight days. 31-Oct: Quality Power, Shyam Metalics, Neogen. 2-Nov: Divgi Torq, Monarch, Rajesh Power, Akums, Arvind Fashions. 3-Nov: Arisinfra, Inox, Parag, R Systems, Sedemac, TAAL, Cosmo First. 4-Nov: Bajel, C.E. Info, Uniparts, eMudhra, Credo, GMM, Entero. 5-Nov: Finkurve. 7-Nov: Spectrum. At least ten tracker rows still carry 14 or 15-Nov next-check dates for prints that land earlier, and several rulings below are needed before those dates.

## Three things you did not ask about

1. **PNGS Gargi's growth premium rests on guidance the company no longer prints.** This is the largest position on file (10%, framework override, cost ₹884) and it trades at ₹515, 24.3% below the ₹680 weekly-close exit. The H1 deck dropped the ~35% revenue CAGR line and moved store monetisation to FY28. The thesis carries a +5x growth premium on that guidance, so the exit-line question and the valuation question now point the same way.

2. **Unihealth is already under its own promoter floor.** Trigger #3 is a 65% promoter line, and the Sep-26 shareholding shows 64.82% on level; only the 2 lakh promoter warrants at ₹151 lift it back (65.22% fully diluted). The postal ballot just cleared the main-board move that opens the door to the QIP management is weighing for Q3/Q4 FY27; a ₹100 Cr raise at ₹550 takes promoters to about 59% (T23 arithmetic). The warrant conversion date is the cheapest early signal of whether the promoters will defend the floor, on a 4% held position.

3. **Bandhan's "price invites" rests on a stale value.** At ₹155 the stock is below the ₹170-180 band and the ₹164 BUY line, which looks like an entry signal. But the ₹533 Y5 value that makes ₹155 clear the 25% hurdle was set before the ROA guidance cut and the CASA break. The record's own 90-day OBSERVATION clock (no permanent CFO by about 27-Sep) also lapsed without being applied. A recompute decides whether the price really invites.

## Reading notes

**Tool limits met**
- The concurrent subagent cap of 20 was reached once; the Notion check agent waited for a free slot.
- Notion fetch results share one tool-results folder. T17 (Bajel Projects) first got the PNGS Gargi page through a name collision, caught it and re-fetched. All analysts then copied fetch files to their own scratchpad. A separate check found no wrong-company data in any output; 33 of 33 re-checked master pages matched.
- 22 PDFs failed with HTTP 403 on the NSE SME / Emerge link, and the fallback link also failed. Those items were read from the Screener summary only (list in the opening paragraph).
- The 7 concall audio links were not transcribed. Every call-dependent test from 28-Oct (Route Mobile, Bandhan, PVR Inox, Syngene, Unimech, Vivid, Knack) is open until a transcript is filed.

**Facts from secondary sources, not filings**
- Screener summaries only: FlySBS Aviation (Hawker 900XP induction), Unihealth (postal ballot result; vote split not read), Vivid Electromech (call held), Macpower CNC (Equirus meeting), Silkflex, Taurian MPS, Chandan, Karnika, Cellecor, Tirupati Forge, Urban Enviro, Womancart and Sambhv Steel (a rating line with no agency or content).
- Zota Health Care: press release read from the NSE archive; the results statement and deck were not read, and the ₹69.9 Cr loss is inferred as H1 from screener quarterly figures.
- Urban Enviro: record date and ratio come from an aggregator and the company site, and conflict with the screener share count.
- Broker or conference statements marked [MGMT]: Finkurve "NPA around 1%" (Bharat Connect), Parag Q1 PAT and cheese capex (broker note), Cosmo's Garware US-refund read-across, Unihealth's QIP plans.
- Prices: screener.in for all 106 names, read 05:35 to 05:40 IST on 29-Oct, mostly before the day's results traded. No "found by search" matches.

**Corrections to earlier cards and to the record**
- Satin Creditcare: Q1 consolidated ROE 16.7% was the reported figure (deck p.15). The ~13.75% GAAP estimate of 23-Oct should be struck.
- Aarti Pharma: the ~₹22 normalised EPS on file is stale; trailing EPS is ₹28.21.
- Sasken: Q1 Top-5 concentration is 56% per the new deck, closing the Q1 ambiguity.
- Bandhan: Q1 wholesale loss now shows as ₹68.93 Cr, restated from ₹77.11 Cr without explanation.
- Paisalo: screener item 120 "trade" is the 26-Oct pledge, not a sale; promoter holding is unchanged.
- Credo Brands: the page's "130.79M vs 130.74M shares" is a rupee paid-up figure, not a share count; the EPS on file is right.
- Akums: the reachability note cites ~₹466 as the lowest tested price, but the screener 52-week low is ₹409.
- Stale Current Market Price properties: C.E. Info (₹1,185 from 13-Jul vs ₹831), Atlanta (₹1,728 from 14-May vs ₹1,638), Inox (₹1,491.70 from 17-Apr vs ₹1,910), TAAL (₹5,714.6 pre-split vs ₹826 post-split), Divgi (₹1,175 from 29-Aug vs ₹958).
- Gates that passed with nothing logged: Cosmo First Q1 master gate, FlySBS August re-engagement gate, Akums Q1, Parag Q1 binary, EPack Q1 review, Bajel Saudi JVCo deadline (30-Sep), Bandhan 90-day OBSERVATION rule.
- Entero: the record's Q1 dates conflict (result 17-Aug, call 10-Aug).
- Screener period labels are wrong for R Systems, Hexaware and Womancart (see opening paragraph). Unimech's feed line says "H1 FY2026" for the FY27 call.
